# Shopping List App: Product & Technical Spec (v0.2)

Working title: **The Perfect Bite**. A better "Out of Milk": a shopping list that auto-organizes ingredients by aisle category, ties ingredients to recipes, lets a household share everything, and takes adds from Siri.

This document is written to be handed to Claude Code as the build brief. Section 10 is the build order; section 12 lists open questions. Anything marked **[Default]** is a decision made in this spec to keep moving that the product owner can overturn. Anything marked **[SPIKE]** must be proven in M0 before the design depending on it is built.

### Changes from v0.1
- **Stack**: Expo + TypeScript instead of native Swift/SwiftUI, matching the stash project (`../stash`). Native Swift only for the Siri App Intent. Reuse stash's patterns and code where they fit (section 8.4).
- **Auth**: Google only. During development, use stash's dev sign-in stub (email + one-time code through local Supabase). Sign in with Apple is dropped.
- **Siri**: new requirement 12, "Hey Siri, add to The Perfect Bite" (section 4.9).
- **Notifications**: moved from non-goals to Later, with a design sketch so v1 doesn't block them (section 11).
- **Ingredients keyed by normalized name** instead of a client-provisional UUID, so offline adds need no id rewriting (sections 5, 6).
- **Sync**: field-level last-write-wins using client timestamps, replacing row-level LWW by server `updated_at` (section 7).
- **Households**: a user can belong to several households. The household owns all lists and recipes; users only have access through membership. Joining or leaving never moves data (section 4.7, Q1-Q3, Q6).
- **Local dev**: same setup as stash, spelled out in section 8.3.
- **Duplicates**: in-app adds, single and recipe alike, auto-Combine with an undo toast instead of a blocking prompt (section 4.4, Q10).
- **Schema fixes**: added `is_grocery_item`, household counting, `updated_at` columns; defined owner transfer; added a quantity display rule.

---

## 1. Goals and non-goals

### Goals (v1)
1. Fast capture: typing an ingredient and hitting return should be the whole interaction. Saying it to Siri should work too.
2. Lists organize themselves: items land in the right category section without the user doing anything.
3. Recipes are first-class: an ingredient on a list can remember which recipe(s) it came from.
4. Households share everything in real time, and the app works in a store with bad signal.

### Platforms
- **iOS** is the v1 target (needed for Siri), built with Expo.
- **Web** via react-native-web is Later. Keep it possible: no iOS-only APIs outside `targets/` and clearly isolated modules.
- **Android** is not a target, but nothing should rule it out.

### Non-goals (v1)
- iPad-optimized layouts, Apple Watch, Android.
- Prices, budgets, store/aisle maps, barcode scanning.
- Pantry tracking. (Recipe import from URLs and meal planning were non-goals; they are now requirements 14 and 16, sections 4.10 and 4.16.)
- Location reminders and per-household muting of push notifications (Later, section 11.1).
- Sign in with Apple, email/password accounts.

---

## 3. Glossary

- **Household**: the "account" from requirement 7, and the **owner of all shared data**. A group of users who share all of its lists and recipes. A user can belong to several households and always belongs to at least one; a solo user is a household of one. Users never own lists or recipes directly.
- **Personal household**: the household created for a user at sign-up. Nothing special about it except that it exists from day one; it can be renamed, shared, or left like any other.
- **Ingredient**: a global, canonical catalog entry (e.g. `milk`), shared across *all* users of the app, identified by its **normalized name**. Holds the default category.
- **List item**: an ingredient on a specific list, with a quantity and checked state.
- **Contribution**: one reason a list item is on the list (added by hand, by Siri, or from recipe X), with its own quantity. A list item has one or more contributions; its displayed quantity is their sum.
- **Category**: a store section (Produce, Dairy, ...). A fixed system set, plus optional custom categories, each belonging to one household.
- **Override**: a household's choice of category for an ingredient, replacing the global default for everyone in that household.
- **Default list**: the list Siri adds to when the user doesn't name one. Per user.

---

## 4. Product behavior

### 4.1 Lists (req 10)
- A household can have any number of lists. Each list belongs to exactly one household and has a name, an optional emoji, a colour, and a sort position.
- **List colour**: one of the aisle hues (green, blue, rose, amber, grey; DESIGN.md), or **Default** (stored as null). Like the name, it belongs to the list and syncs, so every member of the household sees the same colour, and any member can change it (the same rules as renaming). It is never per user or per device. A value the app doesn't know (a hue added by a newer version) shows as Default. Set it in the list's **Edit list** sheet (⋯ menu, **Edit list…**): a row of swatches, Default, Green, Blue, Rose, Amber, Grey, with the current one ringed. It shows as the list's dot in the Your lists sheet (below): the hue's dot; a Default list's dot is `primary` for the Siri default list and an empty ring otherwise. Archived lists keep their colour (faded like the rest of the row). Colour is decoration: VoiceOver reads the row the same whatever its colour.
- **The List tab is the list** (mockups E, G, H: https://claude.ai/artifact/M587vDvPQqF4KYAZEigusC). The first tab ("List", SF symbol `checklist`) shows the **current list** as its root screen: the list header and the rows, with the tab bar showing; the add bar opens from the tab bar's **+** (below). There is no Lists home and no Back. The current list is the list last shown on this device (`lastOpenedListId`, a device setting), falling back to the user's default list, then the first list; archived and deleted lists don't count. If the current list is deleted or archived (here or on another device), the tab moves to the next one by the same rule. With no lists at all: while an invite is waiting (as for a new user who was invited, 4.7) the tab shows the **invite card** (4.7, Invites waiting); otherwise "No lists yet." with a **New list** button. Either way, while lists are archived, a button under it opens the Your lists sheet.
- **The tab bar's +**: a round **+** sits apart on the right of the tab bar, the tabs to its left (the app draws its own tab bar for this, as native tabs set only a search tab apart there; VoiceOver "Add"). Adding a grocery item is the most common thing anyone does, so it always adds an item: on a list it opens that list's add bar, focused, leaving Select mode first; anywhere else (Recipes, Plan, Settings) it opens the List tab with its add bar focused. A **long-press** on + (a medium haptic) opens a native menu over the button: **Add item** (`plus`, the same as a tap) and **Add recipe** (`book`, the Add recipe sheet, 4.5, which holds the link and photo imports). VoiceOver offers the menu as a custom action on +, "More ways to add", which shows the same items as an action sheet (the native menu can't be opened from code). The Recipes tab has its own Add recipe button in its header (4.5), so adding a recipe never depends on finding the long-press. On a list the add bar is hidden at rest, so the rows run down to the tab bar; it goes again once the field loses focus. With text in it too, one tap outside the bar (the list's empty space between or below the rows, the header, or the compact title) hides the keyboard and the bar at once and throws the text and its chips away, while a tap on a row, chip or control does what it always does and leaves the bar up. + then brings the bar back empty and focused.
- **An empty list** (no items at all, `docs/design/list-empty-state/` board C) shows, about 40% down the sheet, a cart glyph in a soft green tile, the headline "Your list is empty" (a VoiceOver header), "Add what you need, or pull in a recipe's ingredients in one go.", then two actions stacked: **Add an item** (a soft pill with a `plus` glyph; it opens the add bar, focused, the same as the tab bar's +) and **Add from a recipe** (a plain green button with `fork.knife`; it opens the Recipes tab). Spanish: "Tu lista está vacía", "Agrega lo que necesitas o trae los ingredientes de una receta de una vez.", **Agregar un artículo**, **Agregar de una receta**. It scrolls under large Dynamic Type. While the list has no items, the view menu (4.6) is hidden, as the Recipes tab hides its sort button with no recipes; ⋯ stays. A list with only checked items isn't empty: it shows its Checked section and the view menu as usual.
- **The + hint**: people rarely find a long-press on their own, so the app points it out once. The screen and the tabs dim, a faint ghost of the long-press menu shows above +, a "Press and hold" pill (es: "Mantén presionado") sits to the left of +, and rings ripple out from + over and over; + itself stays bright. It shows at most once per device. **When**: after the user's third tap on + (taps on any tab count; long-presses don't), the next time add mode ends on a list (4.2: the add field loses focus and the keyboard goes down). That way it comes up after an add, when the tab bar is visible again, and never under the keyboard. It doesn't show while a sheet is over the list, in Select mode, or with VoiceOver on: VoiceOver already offers "More ways to add" as a custom action on +, so the hint is marked seen without showing. **Never** once the user has opened the menu (long-press, or the VoiceOver action), even before the third tap. **Dismissing**: a tap on the callout or anywhere else (that tap still does what it does), a long-press on + (which opens the menu as usual), or 8 seconds. All of these mark it seen. It fades in and out; under Reduce Motion one ring holds still around + instead of rippling. It's device state, not synced: `tabBar.addTapCount` (a number, capped at the threshold) and `tabBar.addHintSeen` (true). Settings > Developer (dev builds only) has **Show the + hint again**, which clears the seen flag and sets the count to 3, so the hint shows the next time add mode ends.
- **Switching lists**: the list's name in the header is a button with a small chevron after it, on one row with ⋯ (VoiceOver: "Groceries, switch list", hint "Shows your other lists"); it opens the **Your lists sheet**. While an invite is waiting (4.7) the chevron carries a dot, and the label adds ", 1 invite waiting".
- **Your lists sheet** (a native form sheet): "Your lists", with **Edit** and **New list** in its toolbar (New list asks which household in a native menu when there is more than one). Waiting invites come first (4.7), then all lists the user can see. With more than one household, lists are grouped per household under its name (VoiceOver: a header): the primary (oldest) household first, then the rest by name; a household with no lists still shows, with "No lists". With one household there is no household UI at all. The grouping follows joining or leaving a household live. Each row shows the list's colour dot, its name (with a mic mark on the Siri default list), the to-get count ("Done" when nothing is left), and a checkmark on the current list, whose row is tinted. Tapping a row makes it the current list and closes the sheet. Long-press: Rename, Make default for Siri, Archive, Delete (with confirm). Edit reorders by drag, within a household. At the bottom, "Archived lists (N)", when there are any, unfolds faded rows of the archived lists; tapping one offers Unarchive or Delete.
- **Opening a list from elsewhere** (a recipe's "Add to a list", a recipe import, moving an item to another list, the import notification, a `/list/<id>` link) makes that list current and switches to the List tab; nothing is pushed on top.
- The list's ⋯ menu has **Select items** (4.5), **Share list** (plain text via the system share sheet), then, after a divider, **Collapse all** and **Expand all** in By aisle and By recipe (4.6), then, after another divider, **Edit list…** (and **Reorder aisles…** in By aisle). Edit list is a sheet with Cancel and Done: the list's **Name**, its **Colour** (above), a **Default list for Siri** switch (on for the current default; turning it on for another list moves the default there), and a red **Delete list** in its own group, which asks first. Done saves the name, colour and Siri choice; Cancel drops them. **Uncheck all** and **Clear** are on the Checked section's header (4.6). Clearing, from either place, deletes every checked item at once with no confirmation; instead the list shows "Cleared 3 items" with **Undo** for 5 seconds, which brings exactly those items back, still checked, with their amounts. An item whose deletion changed in the meantime (deleted again on another device) stays deleted. Uncheck all works the same way: no confirmation, and "Unchecked 6 items" with **Undo** for 5 seconds, which checks exactly those items again with their original check time and checker. An item that merged into an open item of the same ingredient when it was unchecked is split back out (its amounts back on it, checked again; the open item keeps only what it had). An item changed in the meantime (checked, unchecked, edited or deleted, here or on another device) is left as it is. Nothing to uncheck shows no toast.
- Each user has a **default list** [Default: the first list, "Groceries"], changeable in Settings and in a list's Edit list sheet ("Default list for Siri"). If the default list is deleted or archived, the first remaining list becomes the default.
- **Launch**: a signed-in launch opens the List tab on the current list (above). Returning from the background keeps whatever screen was open, and the add field is not focused on launch (the keyboard would cover the list).
- **Keep screen on** (shopping with a cart): while a list is on screen, the app is in the foreground and the list has at least one unchecked item, the screen doesn't auto-lock (`expo-keep-awake`, one tag per list screen so two mounted screens can't release each other's lock). "On screen" is the List tab or a sheet over it (this list's own sheets, such as edit item and Edit list, and the Your lists sheet), since you're still shopping. When the last item is checked off or the list is empty, on another tab, a pushed screen or another sheet (e.g. Import from link), in the background, or with the setting off, it's released and the system's normal auto-lock applies; coming back to the foreground takes it again. The setting is Settings > Shopping > **Keep screen on**, a switch with the detail "While a list has items to get" (VoiceOver: "Keep screen on", hint "Stops the screen locking while a list has items to get"), default **on**. It's a device setting (`list.keepScreenOn`), not synced, since a phone in the store and an iPad on the counter want different things, and turning it off takes effect at once on an open list.
- **Since you looked** (board 1 A, https://claude.ai/artifact/7YVHMBerTkBufhm8CcVv8C). Each list has a device setting `list.seenAt.<listId>`, set to now whenever the list stops being on screen after being shown in the foreground (the Keep screen on rule above, so its own sheets count as on screen; another tab, a pushed screen, the background, switching lists, the list deleted or archived all move it). A list shown for the first time on this device gets the marker at once, so it shows nothing. When a list comes on screen with a marker, the app first pulls the list's household (sharing a pull already running, such as the foreground's; offline it goes on with what the device has), so adds made while the app was closed or in the background are on the device, then works out once what other members added since (`listNewsSince`): live unchecked items with a contribution another member added after the marker less 2 minutes of clock skew, by the server `created_at` (a Combine into an existing item counts; an item counts once; adds by you on any device, by Siri on your account or with no `added_by` never count). An item whose live contributions are all such adds (a new item, or a checked one re-added, which 4.4 gives fresh amounts) wasn't on the list to get when you last looked: its row gets a `Badge` "NEW" (`primarySoft`) after its name, in every view mode. An item that was (it has a live contribution that isn't such an add: older, yours, or with no `added_by`) gets the change instead: the same badge just before its amount, reading what those adds total as one amount (`combineAmounts`, an add with no amount counting as one of the usual unit), so "+1 box", "+8 oz" (beside "2.5 lb") or "+1". When they don't make one amount (`+1 cup + 1 tsp`), there's no badge and the amount itself is tinted (`newBadge`, `onPrimarySoft` text) instead. Adds that change no amount (a recipe link with no quantity) show nothing on an item that was there. In By recipe, a row's change counts only its own section's adds, so another section's row of the same item may show none. Amounts that went down are never shown. There's no card or summary, and nothing on the row says who added it or when (its second line is only its own, e.g. "from Chicken tacos"). A new item checked off loses its badge. What's new on open is worked out once per visit; items arriving live get the same badge as arrivals (below). **The badge fades**: its clock starts when its row is first in sight on screen (on open for rows in view, on scroll for rows below, on unfolding for rows in a folded section), and 10 s (`motion.newBadgeMs`) later it fades out over `motion.duration`, or goes at once under Reduce Motion. It doesn't come back during the visit (not even for another add to that item). Once the list leaves the screen every badge is gone; since that moves the marker, a badge you left before it faded doesn't return, and coming back shows only what's new since. VoiceOver: a new row adds "new" after its name while its badge shows; a changed row adds "1 box added" after its amount ("more added" when the amount is tinted). The markers are purged with the household.
- **A row that arrives while you look** (board 2 E). While a list is on screen (the Keep screen on rule, in the foreground), an item to get that gains an amount from another member in its live query is an arrival: a new item, a checked item they re-add, or their Combine raising an item's amount. A visit starts from what's on the list as it comes on screen, so the first load never gets it; nor does pull catching up on adds made on the server before the list was shown (more than 2 minutes before, for clock skew) (those are the visit's "since you looked", above), your own adds from any device or Siri on your account, or an add with no `added_by`. A new row grows into place (its height opens over 250 ms while the rows below make room); under Reduce Motion it just appears. An arrived row that wasn't on the list to get as the visit began (a new item, or a checked one re-added) gets the NEW badge; another member's Combine into an item that was gets the change badge ("+1 box", or the tinted amount, by the same rules as above, counting every add of theirs this visit). Either fades as above, its clock starting when the row is first in sight; if it lands out of sight (below the visible rows, or in a folded section) it keeps the badge until you scroll or unfold to it. Nothing else marks it: no name tag, no flash (the add-merge tint on your own adds, 4.4, is unchanged) and no pill or scroll-to for rows out of sight. Leaving the list ends the visit and clears every badge: what came while away is the next visit's "since you looked". No haptic and no sound. VoiceOver announces arrivals, at most one announcement every 5 seconds: the first at once ("Alex added limes"), then what came in the meantime as one ("Alex added 3 items"; with several people, a sentence each, "Sam added milk. Alex added 2 items"); what's waiting when the list leaves the screen is dropped. An arrived row's label adds "new" (or what was added) while its badge shows, as above.
- **Alex is adding** (board 2 D, the prototype). While a list is on screen (the Keep screen on rule, so its own sheets count), the app is in the foreground and online, the app joins the list's private Realtime presence channel, topic `list:<listId>` (7.2), and tracks `{ user_id, adding }`. `adding` is true while the add field is focused and for 10 s after this person's last add (an item or a whole recipe); updates go out at most once a second, the latest winning. Presence never carries what's being typed. Leaving the list (another list, tab or pushed screen), the app going to the background or going offline leaves the channel, which drops this device from it. The list header shows a 32 pt pill under "9 to get · By aisle", on the green (a `HEADER_WASH` fill, `onHeader` text): the first adder's 24 pt avatar (`memberHue`) and "Alex is adding", "Alex and Sam are adding" or "3 people are adding" (short names, 4.7), then three dots that pulse in turn (still under Reduce Motion). It fades in and out over 200 ms. Your own presence, on any of your devices, never counts. In add mode, when the header is collapsed, the pill is hidden and the compact title's second line ("8 to get · As added") reads "Alex is adding" with the same pulsing dots instead, back to the count once no one else is adding. VoiceOver reads it as static text, with no announcement.
- **Checking off plays a light haptic**: checking an item, by tapping its check or swiping its row right, plays a selection tick (`Haptics.selectionAsync()`, a tick you can feel without looking, lighter than an impact), and Check all on a section (its header's swipe right or the recipe menu) plays the same tick once, however many items it checks. Unchecking plays nothing (it's a correction, not progress), nor do taps in Select mode (they select, not check). There's no in-app setting: iOS already silences it when System Haptics is off.

### 4.2 Adding items (req 1)
- A persistent add field sits at the bottom of the list screen. Typing shows autocomplete from (a) the household's recently used ingredients, then (b) the global ingredient catalog (filtered per 6.5).
- **Household history** (`ingredient_usage`, 5): one row per household and ingredient key with a use count, the last time it was used, and the most recent text typed for it. Adding an ingredient to a list counts one use (Combine included; undoing the add takes it back), and so does adding one to a recipe; adding that recipe to a list counts again. Editing doesn't count. History outlives list items and recipes: deleting an item doesn't forget that the household buys it. Existing data is backfilled once on upgrade.
- **Ranking**: history matches when the name or key starts with the typed text or has a word that does (`thi` matches `chicken thighs`), or when it is the typed name. The item chips, history and catalog together, are ordered: (1) a suggestion that is the typed name (both normalized as an add, so `coffees` counts too) comes first, from history or the catalog, so `coffee` shows Coffee before the more often bought Coffee filters; (2) then shorter names before longer ones, from either source (`chi`: Chives before Chicken thighs); (3) at equal length, a whole-name prefix match beats a word match, then higher frecency (`use_count * 0.5^(days since last use / 30)`), then history before the catalog, then alphabetical.
- **History first**: if history has 2 or more matches, the catalog is left out (up to the suggestion limit), except its entry for the typed name, which always shows, so `chi` suggests the chicken thighs this household buys, not catalog chicken salad. With fewer, catalog items fill the remaining slots, deduped by key. As the user keeps typing, history drops out on its own (`chickp`), so the catalog still finds chickpeas.
- **Remove from suggestions**: long-pressing a history suggestion offers "Remove from suggestions", which hides that row (`hidden_at`). Using the ingredient again unhides it. Catalog suggestions don't have this action.
- **Add mode and the chip row**: on a list, focusing the add field (the tab bar's + opens it, 4.1) enters add mode: the title in the header and the view menu give way, which shows the list's name over "8 to get · As added" (tapping it hides the keyboard); the add field stays above the keyboard with one horizontal, scrollable row of chips directly above it; the list fills the space between, its cards keeping the same inset and rounded corners as outside add mode. Hiding the keyboard leaves add mode and hides the bar with it, clearing any text in the field, so + next opens it empty (4.1). Scrolling or checking off rows keeps the keyboard up. The chip row shows only while there's something to offer: with text, while something matches. With the field empty (whitespace counts as empty) there's no chip row, on lists as on recipes. Matching nothing hides the row. The row grows upward into the list's space, so the field never moves. Autocorrect, spell check and autofill are off on the add field, so iOS's QuickType bar never shows above the keys; the chips are the only suggestions. While typing, the chips are autocomplete: household history, then the catalog, then recipe matches (below), with the typed part in green. Tapping a chip adds it exactly as typing its text and pressing Return would (Combine, highlight, toast, usage counted), clears the field and keeps the keyboard up, so several can be tapped in a row. Long-press on a history chip offers "Remove from suggestions". VoiceOver reads each chip as a button "Add <name>". While typing, each unchecked item whose ingredient name is the typed name or contains it as whole words (amount left out, normalized as an add, so plurals line up: `cumin` marks Cumin and Ground cumin, `pea` marks Peas, but `cum` marks nothing and `rice` doesn't mark Licorice) is tinted, with the matched word(s) in green and "already on list" in place of its amount. When the list's household has **Combine English and Spanish names** on (4.15), so is one whose family key is the typed name's family key or contains it as whole words: `pepino` marks an unchecked Cucumber (its name in green) and `cucumber` marks Pepino (tinted, no green, as the typed word isn't in its name); an unlinked pair (`tomate`, `tomato`) marks nothing, and a checked item is never marked. With it off (the default) only the item's own key counts, so `pepino` marks nothing beside Cucumber. Switching it changes the tint at once. An add then follows 4.4, which compares family keys too when the setting is on: adding `pepino` beside Cucumber adds to it. In By aisle and By recipe, an add scrolls its section into view.
- **Recipes in the add bar** (on a list only): autocomplete also matches the household's recipes by name, with the same prefix/word rule as history on the whole typed text (so `2 ta` matches no recipe), leaving out recipes with no ingredients. Recipe chips come after the item suggestions, so a typed ingredient is never pushed aside by long recipe names: a prefix match before a word match, then most recently used (the later of the recipe's last change and its last add to a list), then shorter name, alphabetical. There's no product cap on them, as typing more narrows them (up to 20 show, a guard on the row's length). They never take an item suggestion's slot. A recipe chip shows a fork-and-knife glyph and its name (the typed part in green) inside a green outline; VoiceOver reads it as a button "Add recipe Tacos, 8 items". Tapping it adds the whole recipe at 1× exactly as 4.4 bulk add does (every ingredient except the household's staples, 4.5 Always have; Combine; one "Added *Tacos* · 6 items" toast counting only what was added) with **Undo** and **Adjust**. When staples were left out the toast adds a second line, "Skipped salt, pepper" (more than three: "Skipped salt, pepper and 2 more"); when every ingredient is a staple nothing is added and the toast reads "You always have everything in *Tacos*" with only **Adjust**. Adjust opens the recipe's add sheet (4.5) on this list; adding from there replaces that add (it is undone, then the adjusted add is made), and Cancel leaves the first add as it is. Return always adds the typed text as a plain item, even when it exactly matches a recipe's name, so typing never surprises you.
- Return adds the item and keeps the keyboard up for the next one.
- **Where a new item appears** (4.6): in As added, at the top of the list (scrolled into view); in By aisle, at the bottom of its aisle's section, or in the Sorting… card at the top while its category is pending (4.3.1); in By recipe, in Other items. A Combine leaves the existing row where it is in every mode.
- Light quantity parsing on input: a leading number and optional unit are pulled out. `2 lbs chicken thighs` becomes quantity `2`, unit `lb`, name `chicken thighs`. `milk` has no quantity. Number words (`two avocados`) are parsed too, since Siri dictation produces them. The item row has an edit sheet for name, quantity, unit, note, and category.
- **The edit sheet**, top to bottom: the item's aisle icon, name and total, with a round **check** beside the name; for a checked item, the name struck through and "Checked <when> by you" under them (`checked_by`; another member's check names them, "Checked 5 min ago by Alex", by their short name (4.7); a check by someone whose name isn't on the device (removed, deleted, or not loaded yet) reads "by someone", and a check with no one recorded leaves the "by" off; *when* reads like the Added rows: "5 min ago", "yesterday", "Tuesday", "Mar 4"). The check (VoiceOver: "Got it", a checkbox) is the same write as the row's check, so sync behaves the same, and like the stepper it saves right away without saving the sheet's other edits; the sheet stays open either way (unchecking closes it if the item folds into the same ingredient already open on the list). Then the Name / Amount / Note card, the **Added** card (shown with more than one amount, or when one came from a recipe: 4.4), the aisle pills (4.3.2; the list household's aisles, the first six in the aisle order the user uses for that household (4.3.3), always including the current aisle, then **Show all**, which shows the rest and "+ New", which creates the aisle in the list's household). An aisle choice is the household's: when it differs from the catalog default, a `footnote` in `inkSecondary` under the pills reads "Changes it for everyone in <household>." ("…in your household." with no name), with a plain **Reset to default** button under it that selects the default aisle; VoiceOver reads the footnote after the pills. Then an **Always have** switch row (4.5; VoiceOver: a switch "Always have", hint "Left off when you add a recipe") with the `footnote` "Left off when you add a recipe. Changes it for everyone in <household>." ("…in your household." with no name) under it; switching it saves at once, not on Save. Then **Move to another list…** (4.4; left out when the household has no other open list) and Delete item. Save saves the edits; Cancel or swiping the sheet away drops them (amount changes made with the stepper, unit menu or a delete control are already saved).
- Supported units [Default]: count (none), `g`, `kg`, `oz`, `lb`, `ml`, `l`, `tsp`, `tbsp`, `cup`, `fl oz`, `pt`, `qt`, `gal`, `can`, `jar`, `bag`, `box`, `bunch`, `clove`, `pinch`, `dozen`. Unknown unit words stay part of the name.
- **Usual unit** (`ingredient_unit_usage`, 5): household history also counts the units each ingredient is bought in. Every add or recipe ingredient that has a quantity counts one use of its unit (count when it has none); undoing the add takes it back, and an add with no quantity counts no unit. The usual unit is the most-used one; ties go to the most recently used, then alphabetical. Until the household has bought an ingredient with an amount, its **default unit** stands in: how it's usually bought in a US supermarket (`ingredients.default_unit`: `gal` for milk, `dozen` for eggs, `lb` for chicken thighs, `count` for lemons), set by the seed (6.2) and by the categorizer (6.4). Household history always wins over it. An add with no amount counts as one of the usual unit when totals are computed (4.4). Existing data is backfilled once on upgrade.
- **Changing an amount**: the edit sheet's Amount row has a number field and a unit button side by side, with a − / + stepper beside them. The stepper and unit button save immediately, and the number field follows the new total. A typed number (decimal keypad; `1.5`, `1,5` and `1/2` all read as numbers) saves when the field loses focus or on Save, in the unit the button shows, and isn't rounded to the step; an empty field clears the amount, and text that isn't a positive number shows an error and saves nothing. An item with no amount shows an empty field and its usual unit (count when none is known) on the button. Step sizes: 1 for count, `can`, `jar`, `bag`, `box`, `bunch`, `clove`, `pinch`, `dozen`, `oz`, `fl oz`, `pt`, `qt`, `gal`; 0.5 for `lb`, `kg`, `l`, `tsp`, `tbsp`; 0.25 for `cup`; 100 for `g`, `ml`. Results are rounded to the step, and − stops at one step (it's disabled there). An item with no amount steps from one of its usual unit, so + on `milk` usually bought by the gallon gives `2 gal`. The unit button opens a native menu of the units above grouped as Count / Weight (`g`, `kg`, `oz`, `lb`) / Volume (`ml`, `l`, `tsp`, `tbsp`, `cup`, `fl oz`, `pt`, `qt`, `gal`) / Packages (`can`, `jar`, `bag`, `box`, `bunch`, `clove`, `pinch`, `dozen`), with the current unit ticked and the ingredient's usual unit (above; Count when a plain count is usual) listed first, on its own, marked "Usual". Picking one keeps the number and changes only the unit (`2 lb` becomes `2 kg`, not converted). Either change replaces the item's amounts with the single new amount; recipe links with no quantity stay so By recipe view keeps them. When the total isn't a single amount (`2 cans + 400 g`), the stepper and unit button are disabled and the amount is edited as text.
- Name normalization (produces the ingredient key): lowercase, trim, collapse whitespace, strip punctuation, simple English singularization (`tomatoes` to `tomato`, `berries` to `berry`, with an exception list such as `hummus`, `asparagus`, `molasses`, `grits`). Spanish adds accent folding and Spanish plurals (4.15). The user's original text is kept as the item's display name. The add field doesn't auto-capitalize. **Display name**: wherever an item or ingredient name is shown for reading (list rows in every view mode, the edit sheet's title, recipe ingredient rows, suggestion rows and chips, toasts, VoiceOver labels, Share as text), it's shown in sentence case by `displayItemName` in `src/shared/`: the first character is uppercased when it's a lowercase letter and the rest is left as stored (`chicken thighs` reads `Chicken thighs`; `2% milk` stays). A first word that already has a capital in it is left alone (`iPhone charger`). Display only: the stored name never changes, and whatever writes back (the edit sheet's Name field, the text a suggestion puts in the add field, the recipe's Edit ingredient prompt) keeps the stored text. The normalizer lives in `src/shared/` and is used unchanged by the app and the Edge Functions, so client and server always agree on the key.
- Tap an item's round check to check it off (its hit area is at least 44 pt). Tap anywhere else on the row (name, second line, amount) to open the item's edit sheet; tapping the name never checks it off. Long-press the row to enter Select mode (4.5) with that item selected (in As added, long-press and move drags the row instead, 4.6); in Select mode a tap anywhere on the row selects it instead. VoiceOver reads the check as a "Check off <name>" checkbox and the rest of the row as a button that "Opens details", with Edit, Delete, Check off / Uncheck and Select actions on it. Checked items move to a collapsed "Checked" section at the bottom of the list (they are not deleted). A just-checked row first stays in place, struck through (tapping its check again takes it back), until the list has gone 1.5 s without a check or a touch; then every row waiting moves at once. So checking off several items in a row never shifts the next one under the finger. Leaving the list or the app moves them right away. Swipe right to check an item off (or, in Checked, to uncheck it), as a tap on its check does. Swipe left to delete; undo toast for 5 seconds. Neither swipe works in Select mode.

### 4.3 Categories (req 4, 6, 9)

#### 4.3.1 Automatic categorization
- Every ingredient has a global default category, set by the LLM the first time anyone in the app adds it (section 6).
- If the ingredient is already known, the item drops into its section instantly, offline included (the catalog is cached on device).
- If it is new, it appears immediately in its own "Sorting..." state at the top of the list and moves into its section when categorization returns. If categorization fails, it goes to **Other** and is retried in the background.
- Without the plan's `categorize` (4.12, a base user), a new name never shows "Sorting...": it goes into **Other** at once with the default unit `count`, and isn't sent. The device still records it locally as `pending`, so it's sent once the plan has `categorize`; a household override or unit history works as for any name. The same goes for a list item synced from a household-mate whose name this device doesn't know.

#### 4.3.2 Recategorizing (req 6)
- From the item's edit sheet (tap the item's name), "Move to category..." opens a category picker (with "New category...").
- This writes a **household override** for that ingredient (`household_category_overrides`, keyed by household and the ingredient's family key, 4.15, so `leche` and `milk` move together), in the household of the list the item is on. It applies on every list of that household, for *every member*, on every device. Other households, and the global default, are unchanged.
- Someone in two households has a separate set of overrides in each; a list or recipe uses its own household's.
- "New category..." creates a **custom aisle in that household**: every member has it (it goes at the end of any aisle order that doesn't place it yet, 4.3.3). A name the household already has (a system aisle or one of its custom ones, in any case) picks that one instead.
- When the chosen aisle differs from the catalog default, the item sheet shows a footnote under the aisle pills, "Changes it for everyone in <household>." ("Changes it for everyone in your household." with no name), and a plain **Reset to default** button (VoiceOver hint "Uses the usual aisle for everyone") that selects the default aisle.
- "Reset to default" resets it for the whole household. It writes the override with a null category rather than deleting the row, so it syncs like any other change.
- When two members change the same ingredient, the later change wins (field-level LWW through `apply_mutations`, 7.3).
- Overrides are never fed back into the global catalog (Q4).
- **Client side**: `src/features/categories/categories.ts` (`setCategoryOverride` / `clearCategoryOverride` by household, `createCategory(userId, householdId, name)`, `categoriesQuery(userId, households)`: system aisles plus those households' custom ones, with the user's positions). The list items query joins overrides on the list's household; the item sheet, recipe screen and add bar's aisle dots use the list's or recipe's household.

#### 4.3.3 Category order (req 9)
- **Per household, with an optional personal override.** Each household has a **household aisle order**, and each member can keep **their own order** for that household. There is no order that spans households. For a list in household H, the By aisle and By recipe views (4.6) and the aisle pills (4.4) use, as a whole:
  1. the viewer's own order for H, if they have one;
  2. else H's household order, if one has been saved;
  3. else the default store walk (section 6.2 order).
  The whole order comes from one source: positions from two orders are never mixed. Someone's own order is a snapshot: even when it matches the household order, it doesn't follow later changes to it. Removing it (**Use household order**) means following the household order again, including later changes.
- **Which aisles an order holds**: the system aisles plus H's custom aisles (4.3.2). An aisle missing from the order in use (a custom aisle created since, or a system aisle added by an app update) goes after the ones it places: missing system aisles in default order, then missing custom aisles by `created_at` (then id). Nothing is borrowed from a lower source. Ids the order holds that are gone, or aren't H's, are skipped. A renamed aisle keeps its place (orders hold category ids).
- **The Aisle order screen** is per household: **Settings → Households → [Household] → Aisle order** (4.7), and, in By aisle, **Reorder aisles…** in the list's ⋯ menu opens it for the list's household. Settings has no top-level entry for it. It shows only that household's aisles (no household names under them).
  - At the top, under the title, the **source line** says what the user follows now: **Following household aisle order** or **Using your own aisle order** (`callout`, `inkSecondary`).
  - Under it, a segmented control **Household order | My order** sets which order the drags edit, before the first drag. It starts on Household order when the user follows the household, and on My order when they have their own. Choosing a side creates or deletes nothing. Under the control, for Household order: "Used by members who follow the household order." For My order: "Only you see this order." (`footnote`, `inkSecondary`).
  - The rows show the chosen side's order as it would apply: Household order shows the household order (or the default when there is none); My order shows the user's own order, or, when they have none, the order they use now, as the starting point for a new one.
  - Drags (and VoiceOver's Move up / Move down) change a draft on screen only. The save button under the rows names the side: **Save household order** or **Save my order** (off until something moved). Saving writes the whole order and leaves the user's other side alone: saving the household order never deletes the user's own order, so the source line still says "Using your own aisle order".
  - **Use household order** (a plain button, shown only while the user has their own order) deletes their own order for this household; the source line changes to "Following household aisle order". It doesn't confirm: the household order is still there.
  - Unsaved moves: switching sides or leaving (Done, swipe down, back) asks "Discard your changes?" with **Discard** (destructive) and **Keep editing**. Nothing is ever saved without its button.
  - No undo, and no notice when the household order changes while someone uses their own: the source line is enough.
- **Storage**: one row per order holding the whole order as an array of category ids (`household_category_order`, `user_household_category_order`, section 5), so a save always replaces the whole order and two saves never interleave. Any current member can change the household order; only the user changes their own. The household order syncs through `apply_mutations` as one field (7.3); the user's own syncs to their devices by direct writes, like `user_settings`. Leaving a household deletes the leaver's own order for it.

### 4.4 Duplicate handling (req 8)
A duplicate is an add whose normalized ingredient matches an **unchecked** item already on the same list.

- **Across a family** (4.15): when the list's household has **Combine English and Spanish names** on, an item matches when it has the add's key or the add's family key, on every path that combines (the add bar and its chips, a recipe's Add to a list, Siri adds, and sync's merges, 7.2 and 7.3). Adding `pepino` to a list with an unchecked Cucumber adds to Cucumber, and `cucumber` to one with Pepino adds to Pepino: the item keeps the name it was first added under, and the toast names it ("Added to *Cucumber*, now 3"). A checked twin is reopened as below. When an item with the add's own key and a twin are both open (they were on the list before the link), the add goes to its own. The same goes within a recipe: `pepino` and `cucumber` in one recipe fold into one item. Usage history and the usual unit stay per key (4.2): the `pepino` add counts a use of `pepino`, in its own unit, and its Undo takes that back. An unlinked pair (`tomate`, `tomato`) stays two items. A link cleared by `yarn ai:unlink` stops new combines; items already combined stay as they are. Unchecking or restoring an item beside an open twin folds it into the twin, as `apply_mutations` would (7.3). Moving an item to another list and editing its name still compare the item's own key. With the setting off (the default) every one of these paths compares the item's own key only, as before links existed: `pepino` beside Cucumber is a new item, on the device and in `apply_mutations` (which reads the list's household's setting) alike, and a pull doesn't merge a local twin. Turning it on merges nothing already on a list: open twins stay two items until one is added again (the add goes to its own key's item) or reopened (it folds into the other). Turning it off leaves combined items as they are. Aisle overrides use the family key whatever the setting (4.3.2).

- **Single add in the app**: no prompt, so the add field never blocks the next item (goal 1). The new quantity is added to the existing item as a new contribution (**Combine**), the existing row briefly highlights, and a toast appears for 5 seconds: "Added to *milk* (now 2 gal). **Undo**". The amount is the item's new total (4.4 quantity math, so `milk` added to `1 gal milk` reads `2 gal` when milk is usually bought by the gallon, and `1 gal + 1` when no unit is known yet) and is left off when there is none.
  - **Undo**: removes the add, leaving the item as it was. There is no "Keep existing" choice, for single adds or recipe adds.
  - Undo is also available later from the item's edit sheet, which lists its contributions ("Added": the amount, then who and when, e.g. "You, Tuesday" or "Alex, Tuesday" with the member's short name; "Someone else" only for a member whose name isn't on the device (removed, deleted, not loaded yet) or an old add with no one recorded; a recipe's add is the recipe's name, followed by who added it when that was another member, "Shakshuka · Alex, yesterday"; a Siri add is "Siri") with a delete control on each when there is more than one. A row from a recipe that still exists shows a chevron and opens that recipe when tapped (the sheet saves its edits and closes first); a deleted recipe's row reads "A recipe" and doesn't open. Each row's who-and-when text follows a 24 pt avatar (`sizes.addedAvatar`): the member's initial in their colour (`avatarColors(memberHue(id))`), yours too, "?" when their name isn't on the device, and grey for an old add with no one recorded; a recipe's add has its recipe's tile (`fork.knife` in `recipeHue`) instead, and a Siri add a grey `mic` tile. The avatar is hidden from VoiceOver, since the row's text already names who.
  - Under the item's name and amount on the sheet (above the "Checked … by" line), an "Added by" row says who put it on the list, from everyone with a live add: up to three overlapping 22 pt avatars (`sizes.addedByAvatar`, each ringed 2 pt in `background`, 6 pt overlap) and a footnote line with the names in bold. One person: "Added by Alex · Tuesday" (when = their earliest live add) or "Added by you · Tuesday"; two: "Added by Alex and you" (you last, no time); three or more: "Added by Alex, Sam and 2 others". A Siri add counts as whoever asked Siri (so yours is you), and a recipe add as whoever added the recipe to the list. A member whose name isn't on the device is "someone" with a "?" avatar. Adds with no one recorded are skipped; with none left, the row doesn't show. VoiceOver reads it as one element: "Added by Alex, Tuesday".
  - **The item's photo** (item-requests board G): one per item, so whoever shops knows exactly what to get, added, changed or removed by any member on the sheet and shown there for everyone in the household. Under the title block and above the Amount card: with no photo, a dashed **Add photo** tile (64 pt, `photo.badge.plus`) opening a native menu of **Take photo** and **Choose from library** (`expo-image-picker`, cropping to 4:3 allowed; the camera asks for access, and an alert says how to allow it when it's off); with one, the photo full width at 4:3 in a `Card`, with **Replace photo** (the same two sources) and **Remove photo** on a long-press or its **…** button, and a tap opening it full screen (pinch to zoom, pull down to close). It works like a recipe's own photo (4.5): shrunk on the device (`downscalePhoto`, 1600 px, JPEG), uploaded to the private `item-photos` bucket at a new path each time (`<household_id>/<list_item_id>/<uuid>.jpg`), then `photo_path` is set through the usual item update, with "Photo updated" (or "Photo removed") and **Undo**. While it uploads, the sheet shows it from the device with a spinner; a failed upload leaves the item as it was, with "Couldn't upload the photo" and **Try again**. It loads with the session's token and the path as `cacheKey` (one retry with a refreshed token when Storage turns it away), so once seen it shows offline. Uploading needs a connection: offline, the tile and Replace photo are disabled with "Needs a connection"; Remove still works (it's a synced edit). The photo stays when a checked item is reopened by a new add: it's the same product. Old objects are deleted on the server (`photo-cleanup`, 5), and leaving or losing a household clears the image caches. VoiceOver: "Photo of za'atar", with Take photo, Choose from library and Remove photo as custom actions. Client side: `src/features/lists/item-photos.ts` (`uploadItemPhoto`, `removeItemPhoto`, the source) and `item-photo.tsx`.
- **Move to another list…** (edit sheet): picks another open list in the same household (a native action sheet of their names, in the user's order) and moves the item there with all its contributions (recipe ones keep their recipe). The sheet saves its edits first, closes, and opens the destination list. An unchecked item meets that list exactly as a single add would: an open match gets its contributions (**Combine**, and the moved item goes), a checked match is unchecked with its amounts replaced, otherwise the item itself moves (keeping its note). A checked item moves as it is. A move isn't a use, so household history (4.2) is unchanged. The destination list shows a toast for 5 seconds with **Undo**: a Combine reads as a single add does ("Added to *milk*, now 2 gal") and tints the row it joined; otherwise "Moved *milk* from Groceries". Undo puts the item back on its list with its contributions and returns the item it joined to its earlier amounts (checked again, if the move reopened it); if the same ingredient has since been opened on the first list, the amounts go onto that item.
- **Adds with no UI always Combine silently**: Siri adds (4.9), adds that arrive by sync from a household member, and offline-conflict resolution (7.3). The toast only shows on the device doing an in-app add.
- **Quantity math**: if both quantities share a unit, or convertible units within the same dimension (mass with mass, volume with volume, metric and US customary both allowed), sum them.
  - **Display rule** [Default]: show the sum in the unit of the larger contribution, rounded to a friendly value (nearest 1/4 for cup/tsp/tbsp, 0.1 for lb/kg/l, whole numbers for g/ml/count). If rounding would hide a contribution (e.g. `1 cup + 1 tsp` rounds to `1 cup`), show both instead: `1 cup + 1 tsp`.
  - **Adds with no amount count as one** of the ingredient's usual unit (4.2) when that's a unit things are bought in (`gal`, `qt`, `pt`, `l`, `lb`, `kg`, `dozen`, `can`, `jar`, `bag`, `box`, `bunch`), otherwise a unitless one (no usual unit known, a count, or a cooking measure such as `tsp`, `tbsp`, `cup`, `oz`, `g`, `ml`), and sum like any other contribution. So `milk` + `milk` reads `2`, and `1 gal` + `milk` reads `2 gal` when milk is usually bought by the gallon (`1 gal + 1` with no history). `pistachios` + `2 tbsp pistachios` reads `1 + 2 tbsp`, even when tbsp is the usual unit (a unit from recipes, not one it's bought in). Stored quantities stay null; the rule applies only when totals are computed. A lone add with no amount still shows no amount. A recipe link with no quantity (a recipe ingredient with no amount, or an item linked by "Add to recipe") records the recipe, not an amount, and doesn't count. The exception is a recipe ingredient with no amount added to a list when the ingredient is usually bought by count (its usual unit, 4.2, is a count): it's saved as 1, so `1 red onion` plus a recipe that just says `red onion` reads `2 red onion`.
  - If units are incompatible (`2 cans` + `400 g`), the row shows them joined (`2 cans + 400 g`). No error.
- **If the matching item is checked**: no prompt. The item is unchecked and its contributions are replaced with the new one (you bought it before; you need it again).
- **Bulk add** (adding a recipe to a list): no clash step. Each chosen ingredient behaves exactly like a single add: an open match Combines (as a recipe contribution, so the item also groups under the recipe), a checked match is unchecked with its amounts replaced, and the same ingredient twice in the recipe folds into one item. The list then shows one toast for 5 seconds: "Added *Tacos* · 8 items" (the number of ingredients added) with **Undo**, which removes every contribution that add made in one step: new items go, Combined items return to their earlier amounts, and reopened items are checked again with their old amounts. Uses counted by the add (4.2) are taken back too. No row highlights; the items appear wherever the current view puts them (4.6).

### 4.5 Recipes (req 2)
- Recipes belong to the household. A recipe has a name, optional notes, optional source URL (set by recipe import, 4.10), and a list of recipe ingredients (ingredient, quantity, unit, note).
- **The recipe link**: on the recipe screen, a link that opens (`http`/`https`, host with a dot) shows as a button, host without `www.` then path, truncated in the middle; tapping it opens Safari (not an in-app browser, so sign-ins and paywall access carry over). A pencil ("Edit link") turns it into the text field; ending editing saves (trimmed, `https://` added when there's no scheme; an empty field removes the link). Long press: **Open**, **Copy link**, **Edit link**, **Remove link** (no confirm), also VoiceOver custom actions. Text that isn't a link is kept as typed and shown as plain text that starts editing on tap. No link: the empty field, "Add a link to the recipe". A recipe with a link that opens also has **Visit recipe on web** in its Recipes tab card's long-press menu (before Rename and Delete) and in its By recipe header's menu (4.6), opening Safari the same way.
- **The source credit**: a recipe whose link opens says where it came from, next to the site's own steps and photo: "From seriouseats.com" (the host without `www.`, `footnote` `inkSecondary`) at the foot of the Method card, under the last step, and at the foot of Cook mode's last step. When the recipe shows its photo from the site, the same credit sits on the photo's bottom-right corner as a small caption (`caption` `inkSecondary` on a `surface` chip, so it reads over any photo). Tapping it opens the original in Safari, as the link row does; VoiceOver reads "Recipe from seriouseats.com, link". It follows the link: editing the link changes the credit, and removing it removes the credit (the recipe is then the user's own). A recipe without steps shows none under the Method card, and one without a link that opens (typed by hand, or plain text in the link field) shows none anywhere. A recipe imported from photos (4.10) that keeps its pages (`source_pages`) and has no link that opens ends its Method card instead with "From your photos · **View pages**" (a `doc.text` glyph, `footnote` `inkSecondary`, "View pages" in `primary`; board C, `docs/design/photo-import-pages/c-method-card-credit.dc.html`): one button at least 44 pt tall that opens **Original pages** (below); VoiceOver "From your photos, 2 pages, button", hint "Opens the pages". A link credit wins over it, and Cook mode shows neither.
- **Original pages** (board B, `docs/design/photo-import-pages/b-pages-full-screen.dc.html`): a recipe with `source_pages` has **See original pages** (`doc.text`) in the ⋯ menu, after Rename (and Edit servings) and before Change photo… and Delete (board A); a recipe without has none. It and the Method card's credit open a full-screen modal (`src/app/recipe/[id]/pages.tsx`) with no native header on `photoViewer.background` (black): a top bar under the safe area with a 44 pt round ✕ on the left (VoiceOver "Done"; the escape gesture also closes), the recipe's name in `headline` over "Page 1 of 2" in `footnote` (white and 72% white, `photoViewer.text` / `textSecondary`), and a 44 pt round ⋯ on the right (VoiceOver "More"), both on `photoViewer.button`. The pages sit in a horizontal paging scroll, each fitted to the screen (`contentFit="contain"`) in its own zooming scroll view (pinch, or double-tap to zoom in on the point and again to zoom out, up to 4×); page dots (8 pt, `photoViewer.dot` / `dotInactive`) under them when there's more than one. VoiceOver reads each page as "Page 1 of 2", and swiping moves between them. Pages load as own photos do (`recipePhotoSource` with the `recipe-pages` bucket, through `useRecipePage`): the session's header, cached on disk by path (so they show offline once seen), one retry after refreshing the session. A page that can't load shows "Couldn't load this page" in its place, or "Needs a connection" offline. The ⋯ menu: **Share page** (`square.and.arrow.up`) shares the current page through the system share sheet from `expo-image`'s cached file (`Image.getCachePathAsync`, copied to a `.jpg` in the cache directory; a page not in the cache says "Couldn't share this page"), and **Delete pages** (`trash`, destructive) asks "Delete the original pages?" / "The recipe stays. Everyone in the household loses the pages." (Cancel, Delete); Delete clears `source_pages` through the usual recipe update (so it syncs and the server deletes the files, 5), closes the screen and shows "Pages deleted" on the recipe screen, with no Undo.
- **Choosing the photo**: a recipe with no photo (`photo_path` and `image_url` both empty) shows a dashed **Add photo** tile (2 pt dashed `controlBorder`, radius `md`, a `photo.badge.plus` glyph and the label in `inkSecondary`) at the top of the header band, where the photo would be (VoiceOver "Add photo, button"); one with a photo has **Change photo…** in the recipe's menu, between Rename (then Edit servings and See original pages, when shown) and Delete (also when the photo failed to load). Either opens the **Choose a photo** sheet. **From the page**, only when the recipe's link opens: loaded on open from `recipe-photos` (6.9), the page's photos (up to 12) in a two-column grid of 4:3 tiles (radius `md`, cropped), the current photo outlined in `primary` (`focusRing`); VoiceOver reads "Photo 3 of 8 from the page" as a button, the current one selected. While loading, placeholder tiles (`surfaceMuted`); offline, "Connect to see photos from the page"; a page that can't be fetched or has no photos, "No photos found on the page"; any other failure, "Couldn't load the page's photos. Try again later." A page photo that fails to load leaves the grid. Then **Generate with AI**, only in the Add photo sheet (the recipe has no photo, not in Change photo…) and only when the recipe's household has `recipe_photo_ai` (4.12, through `useFeature`): a row in its own card (`sparkles` on a `plum` tile; VoiceOver "Generate a photo with AI, button") with "Makes one photo from the recipe. One per recipe." under the card (`footnote` `inkSecondary`). The user types nothing. Tapping it closes the sheet and starts `generateRecipePhoto(recipeId)` (`src/features/recipes/photo-upload.ts`, beside `uploadRecipePhoto`), a runner outside the sheet and the screen, so leaving the recipe screen doesn't drop it. It sends only the recipe's id to `generate-recipe-photo` (6.11). While it runs (up to a minute), the recipe screen's photo area (where the Add photo tile was, at the top of the band) shows a 16:9 `surfaceMuted` placeholder (radius `md`) with a spinner over "Generating photo…" (`footnote` `inkSecondary`; VoiceOver reads it as a progress bar), and the Add photo tile is hidden. On success it sets `photo_path` to the answered path through the usual recipe update, with "Photo updated" and **Undo** (which clears it again; the recipe has still had its one AI photo). A failure shows a toast on the recipe screen if it's showing that recipe, and is dropped otherwise: `not_food`, "Couldn't make a photo for this recipe"; `already_generated`, "This recipe already had its AI photo"; `ai_photos_blocked` (the user's AI photos are blocked after repeated refused prompts, 6.11), "AI photos are turned off for your account." with no Try again, and it drops `recipe_photo_ai` as `not_entitled` does, so the Generate with AI row goes away until the features reload (the app isn't told about a block up front, so the row shows until the user tries); `daily_limit` (the user's AI photos today, 4.12), "You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow."; `limit_reached`, "You've used this month's AI photos. They reset on Nov 1." (6.11); anything else (the model failing, no connection, signed out), "Couldn't make a photo. Try again later." with **Try again**, since the server released the slot. `not_entitled` also drops `recipe_photo_ai` for the recipe's household until the plan reloads. Offline, the row is disabled with "Needs a connection". Then **Photo library** and **Take photo** (below). **Remove photo** (a soft destructive button) when there is one: it clears `photo_path` when one is set (so the site's photo, if any, shows again), else `image_url`. Picking a page photo sets `recipes.image_url` and clears `photo_path` through the usual recipe update (so it syncs and the household sees it), closes the sheet and shows "Photo updated" with **Undo** on the recipe screen; Remove does the same with "Photo removed". Undo puts the previous photo back (`image_url` and `photo_path`); the toast goes when it times out or the screen closes. Picking the current photo just closes the sheet. A page photo is the site's photo, so the source credit shows on it as above.
- **The household's own photo**: **Photo library** and **Take photo** pick an image (`expo-image-picker`, cropping to 4:3 allowed; the camera asks for access, and a refusal says so in an alert), shrink it on the device with `downscalePhoto(uri, { longEdge: 1600, quality: 0.8 })` (`src/features/recipes/downscale-photo.ts`, JPEG; future photo import reuses it), upload it to the private `recipe-photos` bucket (5) at `<household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg`, a new object every time, and then set `recipes.photo_path` through the usual recipe update, with "Photo updated" and **Undo**. `uploadRecipePhoto(recipeId, localUri)` does the upload and the update. The sheet closes once a photo is picked; while it uploads, the photo area shows it from the device with a spinner. A failed upload leaves the recipe as it was and shows "Couldn't upload the photo" with **Try again**. Offline, both rows are disabled with "Needs a connection". The photo shown everywhere (recipe screen, Recipes tab, plan grid and plan sheets) is `photo_path` when set, else `image_url`: `recipePhotoSource(recipe, session)` (`src/features/recipes/recipe-photo-source.ts`) gives `expo-image` Storage's authenticated URL with the session's token as an `Authorization` header (expo-image sends `headers` on iOS and Android) and the path as `cacheKey`, so the disk copy survives token refreshes and shows offline. An own photo the server turns away (400, 401 or 403) gets one retry after refreshing the session, then falls back to the icon tile. The site's source credit never shows on an own photo. Leaving or losing a household, and `wipeLocalData`, clear `expo-image`'s disk and memory caches, so a former member's phone doesn't keep the photos. Needs a development build, not Expo Go.
- **Start from the recipe**: the round **+** in the Recipes tab's header (the empty state's **New recipe** and **Add recipe** in the tab bar's long-press menu, 4.1, do the same) opens the **Add recipe** sheet (`docs/design/add-recipe-sheet/`, boards B1 and B2): under the sheet title, one field and one button. The field always gets focus; its placeholder is "Paste a link or type a name" with recipe import (4.12) and "Recipe name" without (VoiceOver "Recipe name or link" / "Recipe name"). The button, a `PrimaryButton`, is **Create** (disabled while empty; Return does the same), which makes a blank recipe in the household and opens it. A link typed or pasted into the field, with recipe import, puts a `link` glyph at the field's start and "We'll bring in the ingredients, steps and photo." (`footnote` `inkSecondary`) under it, and the button reads **Import recipe** (with a `link` icon; Return does the same), which hands the link to the import sheet (4.10). With recipe import, a **Paste link** chip (`Pill soft`: `primarySoft` / `onPrimarySoft`, a `doc.on.clipboard` glyph, a 44 pt hit area) sits above the field only while the clipboard holds a link (`Clipboard.hasUrlAsync()`, which shows no paste prompt); tapping it reads the clipboard, puts the link in the field (taken out of shared text) and hides the chip. With recipe import (`recipe_import` in any of the user's households, which covers photo import too; hidden otherwise), under the button an "or" divider (two `border` hairlines around `footnote` `inkTertiary` "or") and then a **From photo…** row in a card of its own: a `primarySoft` icon well with a `camera`, "From photo…" in `body` over "A cookbook page or a screenshot" (`footnote` `inkSecondary`) and an `inkTertiary` chevron; the whole row is one button at least 44 pt tall (VoiceOver "From photo, button"). It opens **Take photo** / **Choose from library** / Cancel, and the pages go to the import sheet (4.10, **From a photo**). A link isn't made into a recipe named after it: without import, Create stays disabled with "That's a link. Type the recipe's name instead." Rename still uses the plain prompt. Then add ingredients with the same add field, parser and chip row (4.2) as lists, minus recipe chips.
- **The recipe screen**: the header band (name, link, one line of tags, notes), kept small so the ingredients card shows on the first screen. The link's line has no "Serves N": the servings show on the Ingredients card's stepper, and the recipe's own servings are edited from **Edit servings** in the ⋯ menu (a number prompt, 1–100, empty clears). Without servings, a dashed **Add servings** pill sits beside the link and opens the same prompt. The tags are one tappable line (4.14). The notes are edited in place: with none, a small **+ Add notes** (`footnote` `inkSecondary`, plus glyph; VoiceOver "Add notes, button") that turns into the notes field, focused; with notes, the text in `subhead` `ink`, two lines at most (VoiceOver "Notes: <text>, button"), and tapping it turns it into the field; either saves when the field loses focus. The band runs straight into the switch (or, without steps, the Ingredients card). The recipe's actions float in a dock at the bottom of the screen, drawn like the tab bar (4.1): **Add to a list** takes the room (disabled with no ingredients; it opens the add sheet at the stepper's servings), then two compact buttons, **Cook** when there are steps (VoiceOver "Start cooking"; it opens the cook screen at the stepper's servings) and **Plan** (4.16). The rows slide under it through a short fade, the screen's content ends above it so the last row and the Method card's last line scroll clear, and the screen's toasts float above it. A recipe with steps then has an **Ingredients | Method · 6** switch (the segmented control; VoiceOver reads tabs, "Method, 6 steps", with the selected one announced) showing one card at a time: the Ingredients card with its servings stepper, or the Method card. The Ingredients card's header has the ingredient count right after the title ("Ingredients  12", `sectionTitle` then the count, `spacing.sm` apart on one baseline), so the stepper (and Reset) stand alone on the right and "12" doesn't read as servings; with no servings the count still sits beside the title. VoiceOver reads the header "Ingredients, 12 ingredients". Ingredients are added from the card's last row, **Add ingredient** (a `plus` and the label in `primary`; VoiceOver "Add an ingredient, button"), there with or without steps and under "No ingredients yet" on an empty card. There's no add field at rest: the row opens the add bar (4.2's, with its chips) docked above the keyboard, focused, and the action dock hides while it shows. It stays open after each add, so several go in a row, and the new row scrolls into view. It hides in two steps (unlike a list's add bar, 4.1, which goes in one): losing focus while empty hides it at once; with text, the first tap out only drops the keyboard and the bar stays showing the text, and a second tap out hides it, keeping the text, so **Add ingredient** brings it back as it was. The dock comes back once the bar is hidden. It opens on Ingredients every visit and isn't saved; the stepper's servings stay as chosen across switches. Switching while the switch is scrolled off the top scrolls it back into view. Without steps there's no switch: the Ingredients card, then the Method card's "No steps yet." and **Add steps**. Anything below the cards stays below both tabs.
- **Recipe view trial** (`src/features/recipes/view-style.ts`): a device setting `recipe.viewStyle`, Settings > Design trials > **Recipe view** (after Fonts), a segmented control (VoiceOver "Recipe view style") of **Standard** (the recipe screen above, the default and the fallback for any unknown value, never-set and the retired `indexCard` included) and **Title on photo** (board E, `docs/design/recipe-view/e-title-on-photo.png`). An open recipe screen follows a change at once. Title on photo replaces only the photo header and the band; the switch, the cards, the ⋯ menu, the dock, the add bar and the toasts are the standard view's. The native header is always transparent. **Title photo** (`RecipeTitlePhoto`): full width, at least 4:3, up under the header: the photo (the household's own, else the site's; the device's copy with a spinner while uploading) over the recipe's tile colour (`aisles[hue].soft`), which shows alone with no photo, while it loads and if it fails, with a `photo` glyph in the hue's `strong` in the middle; then the colour is a button (VoiceOver "Add photo") opening the Choose a photo sheet, or a spinner while Generate with AI runs. The name (`screenTitle`, a header, wrapping) is in `photoScrim.text` on a `photoScrim` band across the bottom, 20 pt in. No credit chip: the byline carries the link. **Byline**, in the tile colour, 20 pt in: the plan badges; one line of the link (a `link` glyph and the host without `www.` in the hue's `strong` `amount`, at most half the line, opening Safari; none when there's no link that opens) then the tag line (as the band's); a `border` hairline; the notes on one line after a `note.text` glyph (`subhead`, truncated), a tap editing them as the band's, or "+ Add notes". With no link field, the ⋯ menu has **Add link** (or **Edit link**), a prompt saving as the band's field does. With no servings, the Ingredients card's header has the dashed **Add servings** pill where the stepper goes.
- **Step matching in the background** (`ensureStepMatch`, `src/features/recipes/ensure-step-match.ts`, on every plan, 4.12): a recipe with steps and ingredients whose match isn't fresh is sent to `match-recipe-steps` (6.8) after an import saves, after the Steps sheet's Save, 2 s after the last ingredient edit on the recipe screen, and when the recipe or cook screen opens (how older recipes get matched; there's no launch backfill). Nothing is sent offline or while a call for that recipe is in flight. The answer is saved as `step_ingredients` only if the recipe's basis is unchanged when it comes back. Errors are silent; `unavailable` and `rate_limited` pause all matching for an hour and `daily_limit` (the user's matches today, 4.12) stops it for the session (in memory). Two household devices may both match; the last writer wins.
- **Cook mode's steps, all open** (board 6 "All steps open" of the cook screen canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/CoSfyu4eDD9BvbYgMP37k2; it replaces board B's cards): the top bar keeps ✕ and the recipe's name over "2 of 5 steps done" ("For 6 · 2 of 5 steps done" when the recipe has servings). The ingredients (or All ingredients, below) come first, then the steps on a timeline, 12 pt apart, then Also needed; the source credit sits under the last step. Every unticked step is open, so the page reads as the whole recipe: its full text and its ingredients. Each step's number sits on a rail that joins it to the next (`primary` below a done step), and the number is the step's checkbox: tapping it ticks or unticks the step. The step's text is never a tap target, so a stray tap ticks nothing. A step is **current** (the first unticked step: a `primary` ring round its number, a `surface` panel with **NOW** over the text in `cookStep` at 600, its ingredients as pills, then a full-width **Mark step done** button), **upcoming** (an outlined `controlBorder` number, the text in `body`, its ingredients as pills) or **done** (a filled `primary` check, folded to its text on one line in `callout` `inkSecondary` with a chevron). Tapping a folded step reopens it to show its whole text and its ingredients (ticked with it), and tapping again folds it; the reopening is screen state like the ticks. Ticking a step, from Mark step done or its number, folds it; unticking opens it. Mark step done ticks the current step (and so its ingredients) and scrolls the next current step into view, instantly under Reduce Motion. VoiceOver reads each step's number as a checkbox ("Step 3 of 5, …"), a folded step's line as a button with the step's text and its expanded state, and the button as "Mark step 3 done"; an open step's text is hidden from it, since the checkbox's label reads it. With no step match, steps have no pills, otherwise the same.
- **Cook mode with ingredients under each step** (board 3 G and I, board 4 L, then board B): when the recipe's `stepMatch` isn't `none` (a `stale` match is still shown), the cook screen changes in four ways. (1) The Ingredients card becomes **All ingredients**, folded by default: one row with "9 · 5 ticked" (`footnote`) and a chevron, unfolding to the usual checklist; the fold stays as set for the visit. (2) Every open step lists its ingredients under its text as wrapping pills: each a rounded (`full`) pill at least 44 pt tall (`surfaceMuted` in the current step's panel, `surface` on the ground) with a 22 pt check and the use, amount first and scaled to the servings ("2 tbsp butter"). A share reads as its own scaled amount "of" the whole ("1 of 3 limes"); an ingredient in more than one step with no share shows its whole amount followed by "part" ("3 cloves garlic, part"). Tapping a pill ticks or unticks that use; a ticked pill has a filled check and `inkSecondary` text. At the largest text sizes the pills go one per line. A folded done step shows none until reopened; a step with no ingredients shows none. (3) **Also needed** ("Not in a step") after the steps lists the ingredients in no step. (4) Ticks are per use of an ingredient (a step's, or Also needed's): ticking a step ticks its ingredients (unticking it leaves them); an All ingredients row is ticked when every one of its uses is, and ticking it there ticks (or, all ticked, unticks) all its uses. Steps still count as today. VoiceOver: a step reads "Step 1 of 5, …, uses chicken thighs, chipotle in adobo, limes, garlic"; an ingredient under a step "Limes, 1 of 3 limes, for step 1" (or "Garlic, 3 cloves, part, for step 1"); the fold row is a button, "All ingredients, 9, 5 ticked", with its expanded state. With no match the screen is exactly as before. Ticks are the screen's state only (`src/features/recipes/cook-match.ts`).
- **Step sections** (`docs/design/step-sections/`, boards A to D; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/Q3doqmcDNtijg8oCatgxhV): a recipe's method can have named sections, and some sections are ways to do the same part ("Marinate the chicken", then Grilling, Baking, Pan-frying or Broiling; familystylefood.com/chicken-thigh-marinade). `recipes.steps` (5) holds them as heading entries between the step strings: `{ "section": "Grilling" }`, with `"choice": true` on a section that is one way of several. A section runs from its heading to the next; steps before the first heading are an unnamed section. Two or more `choice` sections next to each other are one **choice group**; a lone `choice` section reads as an ordinary one. The helpers are pure, in `src/shared/recipe-steps.ts`: `stepTexts(entries)` (the step strings, in order, the only thing anything counts, numbers or matches), `recipeSections(entries)` (each section's title, `choice` and the indexes of its steps in `stepTexts`) and `stepPath(entries, choices)` (the steps shown for one pick per choice group, the first option when a group has no pick). A recipe without headings reads exactly as before.
  - **Step indexes are step strings only.** `step_ingredients` (5) has one entry per step string, `match-recipe-steps` (6.8) gets `stepTexts`, and `stepIngredientsBasis` hashes `stepTexts`, so renaming a heading or ticking a section as a choice doesn't make the match stale.
  - **Method card** (board A): each section's title sits over its steps (`sectionLabel` `inkSecondary`, with the card's step padding). A choice group starts after a hairline (`border`) with "Then cook it one way" ("Cook it one way" when the group comes first; es: "Luego cocínalo de una forma" / "Cocínalo de una forma") and "4 ways" (`footnote` `inkSecondary`), then one pill per option (the selected one `primary` with `onPrimary` text, the others `surfaceMuted`; VoiceOver: a radio group named by the label), then the picked option's steps only. The first option is picked on every visit; the pick isn't saved. Numbers run through the path shown (1–7 for marinate then grill), and the header's count and the switch's "Method · 7" are that path's. VoiceOver reads a section title as a header.
  - **Cook mode** (boards B and C): the steps come in their sections, each title (`sectionLabel` `inkSecondary`) over its steps, the rail breaking at each title. When a choice group is reached, a **How are you cooking it?** card stands in for it (es: "¿Cómo lo vas a cocinar?", under it "The recipe gives 4 ways. You can change this later." / "La receta da 4 formas. Puedes cambiarla después."): the current card's border when every step before it is ticked, upcoming otherwise, with one row per option (the section's title, "4 steps" in `inkSecondary`, a chevron; at least 56 pt tall; VoiceOver "Grilling, 4 steps, button").
