Picking one replaces the card with that section: its title and a **Change method** button (es "Cambiar método") on the right of the title, which brings the card back; ticks in an option are kept while the screen is open. The top bar counts the shared steps plus the picked options' ("For 4 · 4 of 7 steps done"); while a group has no pick it reads "3 steps done · pick a method" (es: "3 pasos hechos · elige un método"). A named section whose steps are all ticked, once the current step's card is past it (not while the picker is current, so the steps just done stay in view over it, board B), folds to one row (its title, "3 done", a chevron; VoiceOver "Marinate the chicken, 3 steps done, button", collapsed), which unfolds on a tap. Picks and folds are the screen's state only, like ticks (`src/features/recipes/cook-match.ts`). Ingredients under each step work as above; Also needed still lists only ingredients in no step at all, and All ingredients lists every one.
  - **Steps sheet** (board D): one field, a step per line; a line starting with `#` is a section heading (`# Grilling`; the `#`s and spaces after them are dropped, an empty heading is dropped). The label above the field reads "One step per line · # starts a section" (es: "Un paso por línea · # inicia una sección"). With two or more headings in the text, a **Choose one when cooking** card (es "Elige una al cocinar") under the field lists them live, one checkbox row each, ticked where the heading is a `choice`; its footnote "Ticked sections that sit next to each other are ways to do the same part. Cook mode asks which one you're using." (es: "Las secciones marcadas que están juntas son formas de hacer la misma parte. El modo cocina te pregunta cuál usas."). Ticks follow the heading's text, so editing a heading's name keeps its tick only if the name is unchanged. Save writes the headings and their ticks with the steps.
  - **Imports** (6.6, 6.10): JSON-LD's `HowToSection` names become headings, and the import's model says which sections are ways to do the same part. A page without JSON-LD, or a photo, gets headings when the page shows them.
- **Start from ingredients**: on any list, long-press an item (light haptic; VoiceOver: the row's "Select" action) and lift without moving to enter Select mode with it already selected (in As added, moving before lifting drags the row instead, 4.6), or choose "Select items" from the list's menu; tap the others, then **Add to recipe**. That opens a sheet titled "Add N items to…": a **New recipe** row at the top with an inline name field (Return creates the recipe, in the list's household, from the selection), then the household's recipes, each with its tinted icon tile and ingredient count, most recently used first (the later of the recipe's last change and the last time it was added to a list). Only recipes in the list's household are offered. Under the New recipe row, a search field ("Search N recipes") and the **Filters** pill with its active chips search and filter them exactly as the Recipes tab does (4.14: names and ingredients, with "Has *chicken thighs*" for an ingredient match; the Filters pill shows the filter sheet's pills in place, and Show N recipes comes back), keeping the most-recently-used order; nothing matching reads "No recipes match", with **Clear filters** when filters are on. The New recipe row stays at the top whatever the search. The sheet's search and filters are its own: they neither start from nor change the Recipes tab's, and they reset when the sheet closes. Picking one appends the items to it (skipping ingredients it already has). Either way the selected list items also get that recipe added as a contribution, so they immediately group under it; the sheet closes, Select mode ends, the list switches to By recipe and a toast says "Added 3 items to Tacos" with **View recipe**. Swiping the sheet away or Cancel keeps the selection.
- **Select mode's bar**: under "N selected" (or "Tap items to select them") on the left sits **Select All** on the right, a plain text button that selects every unchecked item on the list, rows in folded sections and the Sorting… card too; once all are selected it reads **Deselect All**, which clears the selection. Checked items can't be selected (they have Clear, 4.6). Below that, a round `dangerSoft` trash button (54 pt, `danger` glyph; VoiceOver "Delete 3 items") sits left of the full-width **Add to recipe**, smaller and off to the side so it's the harder one to hit; both are disabled with nothing selected. **Delete** asks first, the one bulk delete that does (Select All can empty a shared list, and a 5-second Undo is easy to miss): a native action sheet with no title, just **Delete 3 items** (destructive) and Cancel, which keeps Select mode and the selection. Confirming deletes the selected items at once, ends Select mode, and shows "Deleted 3 items" with **Undo** for 5 seconds, which brings exactly those items back as Clear's Undo does (4.1): unchecked, with their amounts and recipe contributions; an item deleted again on another device in the meantime stays deleted. So clearing a whole list is ⋯ → Select items → Select All → Delete.
- The Recipes tab groups recipes by household the same way the Your lists sheet groups lists (4.1).
- **The Recipes tab** matches the List tab (4.6): a forest-green header (light status bar) with "Recipes" (`largeTitle`; with the Plan tab off, the title switches views, 4.16) and, on the right of the title, the round **sort button** (below) and a round **+** (44 pt, `onHeader` on `header` in both schemes, a `header` plus; VoiceOver "Add recipe") that opens the Add recipe sheet, then the search field on the green with **Filters** at its end (4.14). That's the whole header: the recipe count is in the search field's placeholder, not a row of its own. The recipes sit on the tab's ground, a sheet with rounded top corners over the green. Each household's recipes are rows in one card, its name above the card only when the user's recipes span more than one household. A row is the recipe's photo tile, the name on one line, and under it the first `meal` tag (in facet order) and the total time ("Dinner · 35 min", "1 hr 20 min"), either alone, or nothing when both are missing; there's no ingredient count. Grouped by cuisine, the first known cuisine (facet order) takes the meal's place ("Thai · 30 min"); every other grouping keeps the meal. VoiceOver reads "Tacos, Dinner, 35 min, on Groceries". The row keeps the card's "On <list>" badge, long-press menu, swipe to delete and toast outline.
- **Shelves** (`docs/design/recipes-header/` boards B2 and B3): with 8 or more recipes and no search or filters on, the sheet opens (under the imports card, which stays first) on up to three shelves, each a header row and a sideways row of photo tiles, then an **All recipes** heading with the recipe count after it over the household cards, which follow the sort and grouping as above. **This week** (`calendar`): the recipes planned from today through Saturday, across households, by day then meal, each once at its first slot and captioned with it ("Thu dinner"); See all counts the planned recipe slots. **Recently added** (`clock`): the 10 newest, captioned with the time; See all counts every recipe. **Favorites** (`heart.fill` in `primary`): up to 10 favorites A–Z, a favorite of any current member of the recipe's household, the user included (4.14), captioned with the time; See all counts every one. A shelf with nothing on it is left out (`recipeShelves` in `src/features/recipes/recipe-shelves.ts`). A tile opens the recipe and keeps the card's long-press menu and "On <list>" badge, and VoiceOver reads it as "Tacos, Thursday dinner" or "Tacos, 35 min" (", on Groceries" when it's on a list). **See all**: This week opens the week (Recipes > This week, or the Plan tab when it's on, 4.16); Recently added sets the sort to Recently added and scrolls to the All recipes heading; Favorites turns on the Favorites filter, so the shelves give way to its results. Typing a search (not just spaces) or setting any filter hides the shelves and the All recipes heading, leaving the results under the filter chips as before; clearing the search and filters brings them back. Below 8 recipes there are neither shelves nor the heading. **While grouped** (any Group by but None), the shelves fold into one card, a row each (the glyph, the title and the See all count, with a chevron), so the grouped sections show under the search field; tapping a row opens that shelf in place (See all on its right, the tiles under it) and tapping again folds it. Shelves opened stay open for the app session, whatever the grouping (`useOpenShelves` in `recipe-fold-store.ts`), and Group by None shows them all as above. Photo size changes only the list under All recipes.
- **Favorites and Want to try** (4.14): after the name, a small (`iconSmall`) heart when the recipe is a favorite, `heart.fill` in `primary` (the accent) when it's one of the user's own, else an outlined `heart` in `inkSecondary` when only other current members of its household have it; and a bookmark (`bookmark`, `inkSecondary`) when it's Want to try; on rows and large tiles alike. They're decorative, and VoiceOver reads them after the name instead: "Your favorite" (also when others have it too), "Sam's favorite", "Favorite of Sam and Ana", "Favorite of Sam and 2 others" (members' names as the household shows them, 4.7), then "Want to try" ("Tacos, Your favorite, Dinner, 35 min, on Groceries"). The card's long-press menu has **Favorite** (`heart`) or, once the user has it, **Unfavorite** (`heart.slash`), which set only the user's own favorite (`setMyFavorite`, 4.14) whatever other members have, and **Want to try** (`bookmark`) or, once set, **Tried it** (`checkmark`, which removes `want-to-try`), after Visit recipe on web and before Rename. Want to try stays a shared Saved tag: it adds or removes only its own slug and writes `tags` and `tags_edited: true` in one update, keeping every other facet's stored slugs (unknown ones too) as the Tags sheet's Save does (`setRecipeSaved`); on an untagged recipe it creates the tags with only `saved` set.
- **Sort and group**: the sort button is a 44 pt round white-wash button with an `arrow.up.arrow.down` glyph between the title and the +, hidden on This week and while there are no recipes (VoiceOver "Sort: Recently added, small photos", or "Sort: A–Z, grouped by cuisine, large photos"). It opens a native "Sort and group" menu with three sections, **Sort**, **Group by** and **Photo size** (below), the current choice ticked in each; picking in one keeps the others. **Sort**: **A–Z** (by name in the app's language, ignoring case and accents; the default, so All recipes reads as a collection under the shelves) and **Recently added** (newest change first). A sort already stored on the device is kept. **Group by**: **None** (the default: each household's recipes in one card), **Meal**, **Cuisine**, **Protein**, **Method**, **Time** and **Saved**. A grouping splits each household's recipes into folding cards like the list's aisle sections, each header a band in its own fixed hue with the count and chevron on the right (VoiceOver "Dinner, 3 recipes"): Meal, one per `meal` tag in facet order (Breakfast, Lunch, Dinner, Side, Dessert, Snack, Drink), then **No meal**; Cuisine, one per region (4.14: American, Latin, European, Middle Eastern, Asian, African), then **No cuisine**; Protein and Method, one per value in facet order, then **No protein** / **No method**; Time, Under 30 min, Under 1 hour, Over 1 hour, then **No time**; Saved, **Favorites** (a favorite of any current member, the user included) and **Want to try**, then **Not saved**. A recipe with several values in a facet shows in each of their sections, empty sections are hidden, and recipes in a section follow the sort. Search and filters (4.14) apply first. Households keep their order whatever the choice. Both are device settings (`recipes.sort`, `recipes.group`; an older `recipes.sort` of By meal opens as A–Z grouped by meal); folded sections are kept for the app session, per household and grouping, not saved.
- **Photo size**: the sort button's third section, **Small photos** (`list.bullet`; rows, the default) and **Large photos** (`photo`), the current one ticked. Large photos lay the same recipes out as a two-column grid, one column at the largest accessibility text sizes. Each tile is its own small card on the screen's background, so there's no household card around them and an odd last tile simply sits in the first column; when grouped, the tiles sit under each section's header band. A tile is the photo across the column at 4:3 (the recipe's tile colour when there's none or it hasn't loaded), the name on up to two lines and the total time (or "Has *chicken thighs*" for an ingredient-only search match). Tiles keep the long-press menu, the "On <list>" badge (over the photo's top corner), the toast outline and the VoiceOver label; swipe to delete is rows only, so a tile's Delete is in its menu and a VoiceOver action. The choice is a device setting (`recipes.photoSize`).
- **Copy to household...** (not built yet; `future/copy-to-household-tasks.md`): a member of several households can copy a recipe into another of their households (a new, independent recipe owned by that household). This is how a recipe gets from one household to another; nothing is ever shared across households.
- **Add a recipe to a list**: from the recipe screen, "Add to list...", from **Add to a list** in the recipe's Recipes tab card long-press menu (the same sheet; left out when the recipe has no ingredients, as the recipe screen's button is disabled then; the menu reads Add to Groceries, Add to a list, Visit recipe on web, Favorite, Want to try, Rename, Delete), or by picking the recipe in a list's add bar (4.2), which adds it at 1× with all ingredients but the household's staples (Always have, below) and offers **Adjust** to open this sheet instead. Only lists in the recipe's household are offered (once built, the "Copy to household..." action is the way to reach others). Pick the list, then a checklist of the recipe's ingredients (all but the household's staples selected by default, so the user can uncheck what they already have). Optional servings multiplier (0.5x, 1x, 2x, 3x) scales quantities. "Add N to Groceries" adds them straight away, with duplicates handled per 4.4 bulk add (Combine, no question), and opens the list with the "Added Tacos · 8 items" toast and **Undo**.
  - **Scaled amounts read like kitchen measures** (`kitchenRound`, `src/domain/kitchen-round.ts`): wherever servings scale a recipe (the recipe screen's stepper, the cook screen, Add to list's servings card and multipliers, and what's added to the list), each scaled amount is rounded, then formatted as usual. Cups round to the nearest ¼, ⅓, ½, ⅔ or ¾; under ¼ cup becomes tablespoons (16 to a cup). Tablespoons round to the nearest ½; under 1 tbsp becomes teaspoons (3 to a tbsp), and 3 tbsp or more becomes cups only when that's a clean cup fraction (4 tbsp → ¼ cup, 6 tbsp stays). Teaspoons round to the nearest ¼, ⅛ the smallest. Grams round to 5 (10 from 100 up), ounces and pounds to the nearest ¼, ml to 5. A count with no unit rounds to the nearest ½, never below ½; every other unit (cans, kg, gal…) to 2 significant figures. Nothing scales to 0. The recipe's own amounts at its base servings (1×) are shown and added as written, never rounded.
  - **Quick add to the current list**: the card menu's first item, **Add to <list name>** (`cart.badge.plus`), adds the recipe to the current list (4.1: the one last opened on this device, else the default) in one step, exactly as picking it in the add bar does (1×, the household's staples left out). It's there only when that list is open (not archived) and in the recipe's household, and the recipe has ingredients. The Recipes tab stays put and shows the toast "Added *Tacos* to Groceries · 6 items", with "Skipped salt, pepper" on a second line when staples were left out, **Undo** and **Open list**; with every ingredient a staple nothing is added and it reads "You always have everything in *Tacos*" (no Undo).
  - **One ingredient**: on the recipe screen, long-press an ingredient row (light haptic; VoiceOver: the row's **Add to a list** action, beside Delete) for a native action sheet titled with the ingredient's name, offering **Add to <list name>** for each open list in the recipe's household, the current list (4.1) first, then **Always have** (or, for one of the household's staples, **Don't always have**; below), then Cancel. Picking a list adds just that ingredient at the amount its row shows (the stepper's servings, `kitchenRound`ed), as a recipe contribution (so it groups under the recipe), handled per 4.4 bulk add (Combine, a checked match reopened). A staple is added anyway: it was picked. The recipe screen stays put and shows "Added *Limes* to Groceries" with **Undo** and **Open list**. With no open list in the household the sheet offers only the Always have choice.
- **Always have** (household staples, `household_staples`): each household keeps a short list of ingredients it always has (water, salt, pepper, olive oil), matched by ingredient key. Only recipe adds leave them out: this sheet starts with them unticked, each with an "Always have" tag in `inkSecondary` after its name (ticking one adds it this time and doesn't change the staple), and the add bar's 1× add (4.2) and its **Adjust** start from the same ticks. Typing a name, a chip, Siri and Move to another list always add. Staples are household data like aisle choices (4.3.2): any member marks or unmarks one, from the item edit sheet (4.2), Settings, the recipe screen or this sheet, and it syncs to everyone in the household.
  - **Marking from a recipe**: on the recipe screen, the ingredient's long-press sheet (One ingredient, above) has **Always have**, or **Don't always have** when it's already a staple (VoiceOver: the row's action of the same name, after Add to a list). It marks or unmarks the ingredient for the recipe's household at once (`setStaple`) and shows "Added *Salt* to Always have" (or "Removed *Salt* from Always have") with "For everyone in <household>" on a second line ("…in your household" with no name) and **Undo**, which puts it back. In this sheet's checklist, long-press a row (light haptic; VoiceOver: the row's **Always have** / **Don't always have** action) for a native action sheet titled with the ingredient's name: **Always have** (or **Don't always have**) and Cancel. Marking unticks the row and shows its "Always have" tag; unmarking ticks it and drops the tag. Every row naming the same ingredient follows. There's no toast in the sheet: the tag is the feedback, and a second long-press is the way back. A tap still only ticks or unticks.
- Editing a recipe does not retroactively change list items already added from it.
- Deleting a recipe keeps its list items; their contributions become "no recipe".
- **Swipe to delete**: on the Recipes tab, swipe a card left to reveal a `danger` **Delete** (trash). Tapping it, or a full swipe (past 60% of the card), asks the same "Delete “Tacos”?" as the card menu's Delete; Cancel closes the card. Only one card is open at a time: opening another or scrolling closes it, and a tap on an open card closes it rather than opening the recipe. VoiceOver: a Delete action on the card asks the same.

### 4.6 List view modes (req 11)
The **view menu**, a header button (`arrow.up.arrow.down`) beside ⋯, opens a native menu: **As added** (clock) | **By aisle** (cart) | **By recipe** (fork and knife), the current one ticked. VoiceOver: a button labelled "View: <view>" with the hint "Changes how the list is grouped". The last choice per list is remembered on the device (`list.<id>.viewMode`, stored as `newest`, `category` or `recipe`); a list with no saved choice opens in As added, and choices saved before As added existed are kept.

In every view, an item with a photo (4.4) shows a small `photo` glyph in `inkSecondary` after its name, where the NEW badge sits (both can show, the glyph first), and VoiceOver reads "has a photo" after the name.

- **As added** (default): one card with no section header, latest add at the top. An item's place is the earliest `created_at` among its current contributions, so a Combine onto an open item keeps it where it is (it highlights there), while re-adding a checked item, whose contributions 4.4 replaces, moves it to the top like a new add. The adds in one recipe add share a timestamp and keep the recipe's ingredient order as a block (ties go by contribution id, which is UUIDv7 and so counts up in add order, then by item id). An item with no contributions goes by its own `created_at`. Each row shows its aisle as the small aisle dot beside the name (VoiceOver reads the aisle's name), or the dashed amber check and "Sorting…" while its category is pending, so the item stays in place when it's sorted. Check, tap-to-open, swipe-to-delete, the add highlight and the undo toast work as in By aisle. After an add that puts an item at the top, the list scrolls to the top if it isn't on screen.
  - **Your own order**: in As added (not in Select mode, add mode or the Checked section), long-press a row (light haptic) and move without lifting to drag it: the row lifts (a little bigger, with a shadow, as on the Aisle order screen), the others make room, and the list scrolls near its top and bottom edges. Dropping saves the order (light impact); dropping in the same place saves nothing. Long-press and lift without moving still enters Select mode (4.5); a tap or swipe is unchanged. VoiceOver: each row also has **Move up** and **Move down** actions (left out at the ends), which save the same way and announce the new place ("Milk, 3 of 8"). The order is a device setting (`list.<id>.manualOrder`), not synced or shared: a list of item ids, each with the `created_at` its place was based on (the one As added sorts by), so it survives switching views, leaving the list and quitting the app. New items, and items whose place changed since (a checked item re-added, 4.4), go above the ordered ones, newest first, so an add still lands at the top; a Combine keeps the item where the user put it. A checked item keeps its place for when it's unchecked. Each drop saves every row on screen (plus the checked items' places) and forgets items that are gone. While the list has a saved order, the view menu has **Reset order** (`arrow.uturn.backward`) in its own group, which goes back to plain As added with no confirm and shows "Order reset" with **Undo** for 5 seconds. As added stays ticked in the view menu. Signing out or deleting the account clears it with the rest of the device's user data (4.8).
- **By aisle** (the grouped-by-category view of 4.3.3): sections in the aisle order the viewer uses for the list's household (4.3.3: their own, else the household's, else the default), using the household's overrides. Empty sections are hidden. Within a section, items sort by when they were added [Default] (manual drag within a section is a later enhancement).
- **By recipe**: one section per recipe that has a contribution on this list, ordered by most recently added. Each recipe's header leads with its photo at the icon well's size (`sizes.iconWell`, its own photo, else the site's, 4.5), or the recipe's fork-and-knife icon tile without one. A final **Other items** section holds items with only manual or Siri contributions. Inside each section (Other items too), items needing a category ("Sorting…", with the dashed amber check) come first, then items follow the aisle order the viewer uses for the list's household (4.3.3, the same section order By aisle uses, with the household's overrides; an unknown aisle counts as Other), then by when the item was added. Each row shows its aisle's small dot beside the name, as in As added, so the order reads.
  - An item with contributions from several recipes appears under *each* of those recipes, showing that recipe's contribution quantity, with a small "also in: Tacos" hint. Checking it in any section checks the single underlying item everywhere.
  - Long-pressing a recipe header (light haptic) opens its menu: "Check all", "Visit recipe on web" (only when the recipe has a link that opens, 4.5), "Remove recipe from list" (destructive; removes that recipe's contributions; items left with no contributions are deleted). VoiceOver offers the same as actions on the header. Other items has no menu.
- **Swiping a section header** (By aisle and By recipe): a full swipe right checks every item in the section (Check all; the check-off tick, 4.1; swiping left to remove plays a light impact), and on a recipe header a full swipe left removes the recipe from the list. Aisle headers and Other items don't swipe left: deleting a whole aisle in one gesture is too easy to do by mistake. Both work on folded sections. Check all and Remove recipe, from a swipe or the menu, show a toast for 5 seconds with **Undo**: "Checked 6 items" (Undo unchecks exactly those, leaving any checked or unchecked since) or "Removed *Tacos* from the list" (Undo brings back its amounts and the items it deleted). VoiceOver offers Check all as an action on an aisle header. The Checked header and the Sorting… card don't swipe, and headers don't swipe in Select mode.
- **Collapsing sections** (By aisle and By recipe): tapping a section's header folds its rows away, leaving its card with just the header and a chevron (`chevron.down` open, `chevron.right` folded); tapping again opens it. The whole header is the target (≥ 44 pt). A folded recipe header shows its item count, so you can see what's hidden. VoiceOver: the header is a button with an expanded state, labelled with the section's name and item count. The Sorting… card has no header and never folds, and As added has no sections. Folded sections are remembered on the device per list and per view mode (`list.<id>.collapsed.<category|recipe>`, keyed `c-<categoryId>` / `r-<recipeId|other>`); keys for sections that aren't showing are ignored, so a section that comes back is still folded. An add or Combine that lands in a folded section opens it, so its highlight shows. Check all, Remove recipe and Select mode work the same on folded sections (Check all checks the hidden rows too). The list's ⋯ menu has **Collapse all** (`rectangle.compress.vertical`, off when nothing is open) and **Expand all** (`rectangle.expand.vertical`, off when nothing is folded), in their own group after Select items and Share list, in these two modes; they're left out in As added and when the view has no sections. The view menu only changes the view. Opening a section fades its rows in, with no animation under Reduce Motion.
- Checked items behave the same in all three modes (a "Checked" section at the bottom). It's folded while there's something to get, and opens by itself once nothing is: when the last item is checked (after its row's linger, 4.1) or when a list with nothing to get opens. It folds again by itself when something is to get again (an add, an uncheck, a sync). A tap on its header is the user's choice and wins: a fold by hand lasts until the list next becomes all done or stops being, and an open by hand stays open. Settings > Shopping > **Always show checked items** (a switch with the detail "Otherwise they show once everything's checked"; VoiceOver hint "Keeps the Checked section open on your lists"), default **off**, keeps it open while shopping too; a tap still folds it. It's a device setting (`list.alwaysShowChecked`), not synced, read when the list comes into focus. While nothing is to get and something is checked, the header line reads "All done" in place of "N to get" (and "All done · As added" in add mode). Its "Checked · N" header folds and unfolds the section (VoiceOver: a button with an expanded state) and has two text buttons at its trailing end (44 pt hit area): **Uncheck all**, which shows "Unchecked N items" with Undo (4.1), and **Clear** (VoiceOver: "Clear 3 checked items"), which clears the checked items with Undo (4.1). Long-pressing the header (light haptic) opens a menu with **Clear N checked items** (destructive) and **Uncheck all**. The section, and so the button and menu, shows only while something is checked. Select mode (4.5) works from any mode; making or adding to a recipe from it switches to By recipe.

### 4.7 Households and sharing (req 7, 7.1)

**Principle: the household owns the data.** Every list and recipe belongs to one household. Users get access by being members, and lose it by leaving. No action ever moves data between households or hands it to a user; the only way data crosses households is an explicit copy (recipes, 4.5).

**Membership**
- Sign-up gives the user a **personal household** ("Personal", renameable) with a starter list, made on the device after the sign-in pull and invites fetch (`ensureStarterData`). **Unless an invite to their email is waiting**: then nothing is made until they decline every waiting invite or tap **Start my own list** (Invites waiting, below), so someone joining a household doesn't also get a spare empty one. Offline at first sign-in the invites can't be fetched, so the starter is made anyway, and a waiting invite shows on the list once it's fetched. Joining adds a membership and makes no household. It removes nothing either, except when the user's only other household has no other member (typically the personal one): then Join first asks "Keep <name>?" ("You'll share <household>'s lists and recipes. You can keep <name> too, or delete it with its lists and recipes.") with **Join and keep it**, **Join and delete <name>** (destructive; the household is deleted after the join, and a failure says "Joined, but <name> couldn't be deleted. You can delete it in Settings.") and Cancel. The Your lists sheet and the List tab's invite cards ask the same way (`useInviteActions`).
- A user can belong to any number of households (Q6). They must always belong to at least one, except a new user while their invites are waiting (above).
- **A user makes at most one household themselves.** `create_household` refuses a caller who is already a member of any household (`already in a household`, `P0001`), so nobody can make extra households to multiply a plan's allowances (4.12). The app only calls it when the user has none (`ensureStarterData` at sign-up, **Start my own list**); more households come from invites. The server makes a new personal household when a user leaves their only one (below). Each new household gets its `household_plans` row with the default plan (4.12).
- Settings > Households, the first card under the account card, lists the user's households: one row each with its name and counts ("2 lists · 5 recipes"), opening its screen, and the footnote "Members, invites, Always have, aisle order and name matching are in each household." Nothing household-specific is set in Settings itself. Each household screen (mockups: `docs/design/household-settings.html`) shows its name, members, open invites, a **Household settings** card (Always have, 4.4; Combine English and Spanish names, 4.15; footnote "These change it for everyone in <household>."), an **Aisle order** row under that card for every member (value "Household" or "Your own", by the source the user follows; it opens the Aisle order screen for this household, 4.3.3, and has its own footnote, "Shared with the household unless you keep your own.", since a personal order doesn't change it for everyone), and actions: Invite, Leave, and (owners only) Rename, Remove member, Make owner, Delete household. A new per-household setting goes in that card.
- The user can reorder their households (this sets section order on the Lists and Recipes tabs). Per user. *Not built yet* (`future/wishlist.md`): today the primary (oldest) household comes first, then the rest by name.
- **Always have** (4.5): the household screen has an **Always have** row with the count ("None" when empty), for every member. It opens a screen titled "Always have" listing the household's staples by display name in sentence case, live (another member's change shows without leaving). An "Add an ingredient" field at the top marks a name as a staple, read as the add bar reads it (amount ignored; one already there changes nothing), with the add bar's history and catalog chips. Swipe left, or **Edit** and a row's delete button, removes one with no confirm and a "Removed *Salt*" toast with **Undo**. Empty: "Nothing yet. Ingredients you always have are left off when you add a recipe." VoiceOver reads each row as text with a Delete action.

**Roles** (Q1)
- `owner` or `member`. A household can have several owners and always has at least one.
- Everyone has equal edit rights over the household's lists and recipes (create, edit, check, delete).
- Owners additionally manage the household: rename it (`rename_household`: trimmed, not blank, at most 50 characters; other members' devices get the new name through the usual pull or realtime update), remove members, make another member an owner, and delete the household. An owner can also step another owner, or themselves, back to member while another owner remains.
- Server side: `set_member_role(household_id, user_id, role)` (owners only, `not an owner` otherwise; the target must be a member, `not a member`; `role` is `owner` or `member`, `invalid role` otherwise). Demoting the household's last owner, themselves included, raises `last owner`. Setting the role someone already has does nothing. It locks the household row first, as the leaving rules do, so two owners demoting each other at once can't leave none.
- Any member can invite (Q16).

**Members' names on the device**
- The device keeps each household's members' names (`household_member_names`, 5), filled from `get_household_members` after each full pull and when Realtime reports someone joining or leaving a household (each household's channel listens to its `household_members` rows and loads the names again, without a pull). `src/features/households/member-names.ts`: `memberName(householdId, userId)` and the live `useMemberNames(householdId)`.
- A member is named by their first name as it is ("Alex"; a two-word first name like "Mary Ann" stays whole), `shortMemberNames`. When another member of the household has the same first name (ignoring case), a member with a last name gets its first letter too, uppercased ("Alex R.", "Alex M."); one without keeps just the first name. A blank first name has no name, so it reads as unknown. The current user is "You" (*Tú*). An unknown, removed or null id is "Someone" (*Alguien*).
- The household screen uses each member's full name (the first name, plus " " and the last name when there is one: "Alex Rivera"): member rows ("Alex Rivera (You)"), the member action sheet's title, the remove and role-change confirms and failure alerts, the heir in the Leave confirm, and the inviter passed to the invite sheet. A member's avatar letter is their first name's initial. `get_household_members` returns `first_name` and `last_name` (owners first, then by first name, then last name with none first, then user id).
- A member's avatar colour is `memberHue(userId)` (with `avatarColors`), the same everywhere the app shows them.
- **Your name** (Settings > Name, `src/app/(tabs)/settings/name.tsx`, `src/features/settings/name.ts`): names are set here only, never prompted. Tapping the account card (4.8) opens it: **First name** and **Last name** fields (`textContentType` given name / family name; Last name's placeholder "Optional"), prefilled from the user's own row in `household_member_names` (any household; empty before the first pull), and the footnote "People in your households see this name." **Save** (the header's `done` button) is enabled when the trimmed first name is 1–50 characters, the trimmed last name at most 50, and either differs from the cached name. It needs a connection: it updates the user's own `profiles` row directly (`first_name`, `last_name` trimmed, a blank last name null; not the outbox), then reloads the names of each of the user's households (`refreshAllMemberNames`) and goes back. A spinner replaces Save while it runs. Offline ("You're offline. Connect to the internet to save your name.") or a failure ("Couldn't save your name. Try again.") shows in `footnote` `danger` under the fields and keeps the edits; editing clears it.

**Invites**
- "Invite someone" on the household screen opens the invite sheet, which generates an invite link for that household (Universal Link, single use, expires in 7 days). *For now* the link uses the app scheme (`perfectbite://invite/<token>`); Universal Links need a hosted domain (follow-up). Only the token's SHA-256 is stored on the server.
- Opening the link (signed in) shows "Join *John's household*? You'll share its lists and recipes. Your other households aren't affected." Accepting adds the user as a member. Nothing of theirs moves (Q3).
- Already a member: the link says so and opens the household (the invite stays unused).
- A used, expired or unknown link says so ("Ask for a new one"). Opening a link signed out goes to sign-in; the link isn't kept for after sign-in. For an email invite that doesn't matter: signing in with that email shows the invite in the Your lists sheet (below). Keeping a link-only invite's link through sign-in is a follow-up.
- **The invite sheet** (designs S2, S3). Step 1: "Invite to <household>", "They'll share its lists and recipes. Their other households aren't affected.", an email field (email keyboard and autofill) with "Use the email they sign in with (their Google account).", and Next, enabled only for a valid email. Next creates the invite for that email (with progress on the button); a failure ("<email> is already in <household>.", offline) shows under the field. "Don't know their email? Share a link instead" creates a link-only invite and opens the share sheet. Step 2: "Let <name> know" (the first word of the email's local part, e.g. "Sam" for sam.lee@…), the email with "Invite expires in 7 days", then Messages (body filled in), Mail (To, subject and body filled in), Copy link ("Link copied" toast) and More… (the share sheet); Messages and Mail are hidden when the device can't send them. A note says they can skip this: signing in with that email shows the invite in the app. Done closes the sheet. Back returns to step 1 with the email kept; Next again re-invites, which revokes the earlier link.
- **Pending invites** (design S4). Under Members, the household screen has an "Invited" section listing the household's open, unexpired invites, loaded from the server with the members and again whenever the screen regains focus (e.g. after the invite sheet closes). It's hidden when there are none or they can't be fetched (offline). Each row shows the email (or "Invite link" for a link-only invite) over "Sent by <name> · expires <date>" ("Oct 18"; with the year when it isn't this year) and a Pending badge. Tapping a row opens an action sheet: **Send again** (email invites only) re-creates the invite, which revokes the old link, and opens the invite sheet on step 2 for that email; **Cancel invite** (destructive) revokes it and removes the row; Cancel. A failure shows in an alert and keeps the row. Every member sees and can act on every invite.
- **Invites waiting in the app** (mockup H). After sign-in, on each return to the foreground and on reconnect (skipped while offline), the app fetches the invites sent to the user's email (`my_invites`). They're kept in memory only, not in SQLite, and cleared on sign-out; a failed fetch keeps the invites already shown. While any are waiting, the list switcher's chevron has a dot (4.1), and the Your lists sheet shows them first, under an "Invite" label, one compact row each: the inviter's initial in a round avatar, "<inviter> invited you" over "to <household> · N members", a round Decline (✕, VoiceOver "Decline invite to <household>") and a Join pill. **Join** accepts it (`accept_invite_by_id`), pulls (as the join screen does) and removes the row, so the household's lists appear in the same sheet. **Decline** asks first in an alert ("Decline the invite to <household>?" / "<inviter> can send a new one."), then declines it and removes the row. The busy button shows a spinner. A failure shows in a toast and keeps the row, except for an invite that can't be used any more: one revoked, used or declined meanwhile (or gone) removes the row with "This invite was cancelled.", and an expired one with the expired message. VoiceOver reads the row as "<inviter> invited you to <household>", then the two buttons.
  - **The invite card** (mockups: https://claude.ai/artifact/D2T8hVTUt4o38F3SFNA57C, boards A and "A, two invites"). With no lists and an invite waiting (an invited new user, Membership above), the List tab shows the invites instead of "No lists yet.". One invite: a "You're invited" label over a card with the inviter's initial in a round avatar, "<inviter> invited you to", the household's name (`screenTitle`), "N members", "Share one grocery list, their recipes and the meal plan. Check something off and everyone sees it.", **Join <household>** (primary, full width) and **Decline** (plain). Under the card, "or", then **Start my own list** (a dashed outline pill with `plus`) and "You can join later from Your lists.". Two or more: the title "N invites waiting" over "Join one or both. Each keeps its own lists.", then a card each (avatar, the household's name, "From <inviter> · N members", **Decline** and **Join**), then **Start my own list instead**. Join and Decline work as in the sheet (above: the Keep prompt, the confirm alert, spinners, failures in a toast). After **Join** the tab opens the household's first open list, with the **You're in** card (below); other waiting invites then show on that list. **Start my own list** makes the personal household with its Groceries list (`startOwnList`: `ensureStarterData`, or just a Groceries list as the default when the user already has a household with no open list) and opens it; invites still waiting show on the list (below). Declining the last waiting invite makes the starter too (Membership above).
  - **On a list** (mockup board C). While an invite is waiting and the List tab shows a list, the first waiting invite (newest first, as the sheet lists them) is pinned above the rows, scrolling with them: a `surface` card outlined in `primary`, the inviter's avatar, "<inviter> invited you to <household>" over "N members · their lists join yours", then **Not now** (plain), **Decline** (`danger` text) and **Join** (a primary pill). Join and Decline work as in the sheet, a failure showing in the list's toast; after Join the tab opens the household's first open list with the You're in card. **Not now** hides the card for that invite on this device (device setting `invites.hiddenIds`), and the next waiting invite, if any, takes its place; a hidden invite stays in the Your lists sheet and keeps the chevron's dot. VoiceOver reads the card as the sheet's row does, then its three buttons.
  - **You're in** (mockup "After Join"). After a join from the List tab's cards or the Your lists sheet, the household's lists show a `primarySoft` card above the rows (above a pinned invite, if any): the inviter's initial in a round avatar, "You're in <household>" over "<inviter>'s lists and recipes are yours now. Tap the list's name to switch." ("Its lists and recipes…" with no inviter's name), in `onPrimarySoft`, and a ✕ (VoiceOver "Dismiss") that closes it. It's kept in memory only, for the last household joined: a restart or sign-out ends it.
- **Email invites** (server side, invite sheet, pending list and in-app invites done). An invite can name the email it's for (stored trimmed and lower-cased; null for a link-only invite). Inviting the email of someone already in the household fails ("already a member"); inviting an email with no account works the same as any other, so the inviter never learns whether an address has an account. Inviting the same email to the same household again revokes the earlier invite and makes a new link ("Send again"; only the token's hash is stored, so the old link can't be re-shown). The email decides who sees the invite in the app, but doesn't lock the link: whoever opens it first can join with it. The app checks an email with the server's pattern before sending it, and says "<email> is already in <household>." for a member's email. A Mail invite has the subject "Join <household> on The Perfect Bite" and a body naming who invited them, what joining means, the link, and "Sign in with <email> and the invite will be waiting."; Messages and More… send the same short message as a link-only invite.
- An invite is **open** until it's used, revoked or declined; expiry is checked when it's read. Any member can see the household's open, unexpired invites (email or link-only, who sent it, when, when it expires) and revoke any of them. Someone signed in with an invite's email sees it in the app (household, inviter, member count) unless they're already in that household, and can join or decline it there without the link. Revoked and declined links read as used.
- On accept, the new membership syncs the household in (7.2) and the app opens the List tab.

**Leaving and removal** (Q2)
- Leaving removes access; the data stays with the household. The user keeps no copy.
- The last owner leaving promotes the member who joined earliest to owner.
- The last member leaving deletes the household and its data, after a confirm that says so.
- If the user is leaving their only household, a new empty personal household is created for them, so they always have one.
- Server side: `leave_household(household_id)` (any member; `not a member` otherwise) and `remove_member(household_id, user_id)` (owners only, `not an owner` otherwise; not on yourself, who must leave instead) both clear the leaver's default list if it's in that household, run the leaving rules (`private.remove_member_from_household`, 4.12) and, if it was the leaver's only household, make them a new one with `private.create_personal_household`: "Personal", with them as owner, a "Groceries" list at position 1 as their default list (the same as the device's `ensureStarterData`). Both return `{ outcome: 'left' | 'promoted' | 'deleted', new_household_id }` (null when no household was made).
- **In the app** (household screen). **Leave household** is a `danger` row at the bottom, for every member. It confirms first, worded for the case: "Leave <household>? You'll lose access to its lists and recipes. They stay with the household." (a plain member or one of several owners); the same plus "<earliest-joined member> will become the owner." (the last owner with other members); "Leave and delete <household>? You're the only member, so its N lists and M recipes will be deleted." (the last member); plus "You'll get a new empty household." when it's the user's only household. On confirm: `leave_household`, then a pull, then back to Settings. The app never makes the new household itself: the server does, and one pull brings it down and purges the old one (the old one is purged only after the households still listed are in, so the device never has none and the offline starter step never makes a second). **Remove member** (owners, on another member's row): an action sheet ("Remove from <household>", Cancel), then "Remove <name>? They'll lose access to <household>'s lists and recipes.", `remove_member`, and the members reload. Offline, both say "You're offline" and change nothing; a failure shows in an alert and changes nothing. VoiceOver: the rows are buttons with hints.
- **Make owner and Remove as owner** (owners, in the app). Another member's row offers **Make owner** above Remove from <household> (**Remove as owner** for another owner); the owner's own row offers **Step down as owner** when another owner exists (otherwise it does nothing). A non-owner's rows do nothing. Make owner confirms "Make <name> an owner?" / "Owners can rename the household, remove members and delete it."; Remove as owner "Remove <name> as owner?" / "They'll stay a member."; Step down "Step down as owner?" / "You'll stay a member. Only owners can rename, remove members or delete the household." On confirm, `set_member_role`, then the members reload (the Owner/Member badges change); stepping down hides the owner-only controls at once. Offline: "You're offline"; `last owner` (a race) says "<household> needs at least one owner."; any failure shows in an alert and changes nothing. VoiceOver: the row's hint says what tapping offers ("Make owner or remove.", "Remove as owner or remove.", "Step down as owner."). Other members' devices see the new role when they next load the household screen.
- When access is lost (leave or removal), each of the user's devices removes that household's data from local storage on its next sync, and any queued outbox mutations for it are dropped.
- **Delete household** (owners only): deletes it for every member, after a confirm naming how many members and lists are affected. Members left with no household get a new personal household.
- **In the app** (household screen, owners only): a `danger` row **Delete household** above Leave household. It confirms first: "Delete <household>?" / "Its N lists and M recipes will be deleted for all K members. This can't be undone." ("Its N lists and M recipes will be deleted. This can't be undone." when the user is the only member), plus "You'll get a new empty household." when it's the user's only household; Cancel and a destructive **Delete**. The counts come from the device's data and the loaded members. On confirm: `delete_household`, then a pull (which purges it and brings down any new personal household, as for Leave), then back to Settings with a toast "Deleted <household>". Offline: "You're offline"; a failure shows in an alert and changes nothing. Other members' devices purge it on their next sync, as after removal; a List tab showing one of its lists falls back to the default list, then the first (4.1), as when a list is deleted elsewhere. VoiceOver: the row is a button with a hint.
- Server side: `delete_household(household_id)` (owners only, `not an owner` otherwise) locks the household row, clears every member's Siri default list if it's in the household, deletes the household (its lists, items, recipes, overrides, custom aisles, invites and memberships cascade), and gives each member left with no household a new one with `private.create_personal_household`. It returns `{ new_household_id }` for the caller (null when they still have another). The cascaded membership deletes reach every member's devices through Realtime and pull, which purge the household as after removal (7.2). Like leaving, it writes no `admin.admin_actions` row.

**Per-user settings stay per user**, across all households: category order, household order, and the Siri default list (which can be in any household). Category overrides and custom categories are no longer per user: they belong to a household and every member shares them (4.3.2). The order stays the user's own, including where each household's custom aisles go.

All changes sync live to all members' devices.

### 4.8 Accounts
- **Google sign-in only**, through Supabase Auth, the same way as stash (native Google Sign-In SDK, then `signInWithIdToken`).
- **During development**: dev builds (`__DEV__`) also show stash's dev sign-in stub (email + 6-digit code; local Supabase delivers the code to Inbucket). Release builds show Google only. The OAuth clients are in their own Google Cloud project (Q13). Nothing else in the app may depend on which sign-in method is used.
- **The account card** tops Settings (board 10): the 52 pt avatar, then the user's full name (`headline`) over their email (`footnote` `inkSecondary`), the sync badge (7.5) and a chevron. The name is read from the user's own row in `household_member_names` (any household, so it shows offline); the avatar letter is the first name's initial. With no name cached yet (before the first pull) it shows the email over "Signed in" and the email's initial. VoiceOver reads it as one button, "Signed in as Sam Lee, sam@example.com, Synced" ("Signed in as sam@example.com, Synced" without a name), hint "Edits your name"; it opens Settings > Name (4.7, Your name).
- A first-run user gets a personal household (4.7) with a starter list named "Groceries", which is their default list. A user invited before they sign in gets it only if they don't join (4.7, Membership).
- Account deletion (App Store requirement): leaves each of the user's households by the leaving rules (4.7, `private.remove_member_from_household`: owner promotion; households where they were the last member are deleted with their data), then deletes the user with their profile, aisle order and settings. Category overrides and custom aisles are household data (4.3.2) and stay with the household, like its lists and recipes. Items, check-offs and invites they made in shared households stay, with the reference to them cleared. Users delete their own account with `delete_account()` (no argument: it acts only on `auth.uid()`; raises `not signed in` without one; returns `{ households: [{ id, outcome }] }`, and an empty array on a retry once the account is gone). It writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`action` `delete_account`, the user id, and per household its id and outcome, with `promoted_user_id` for `promoted`) with no email, display name or household name: the user asked to be forgotten. `admin.delete_user` (4.12) stays for support requests. Both run the same core, `private.delete_user_core`, which, last of all (so the account's own deletes leave none), deletes the user's `private.user_activity` entries (4.12 User activity) and scrubs the user from other users' entries: `member.removed` and `member.role_changed` entries whose `data.member_id` is the user lose `member` and `member_id`, and the portal says "a member".
- **Sign out** (Settings, `src/features/auth/sign-out.ts`) pushes the outbox (best effort, up to 3 seconds), signs out of Supabase and Google, then wipes the device as Delete account does (`wipeLocalData`, below), so the next account to sign in starts from its own households, not the last user's. If Supabase refuses the sign-out, nothing local changes. As a backstop, before an account becomes ready the app checks which user the device's data was last synced for (the pull state's `userId`) and wipes it first if that's someone else. Changes still unpushed after 3 seconds (e.g. offline) are lost. Signing out also revokes the device's share credential (when online) and always deletes it from the Keychain, keeping the installation id (6.12).
- **Delete account** is in Settings, under Sign out (`src/features/auth/delete-account.ts`). It needs a connection: offline it says "You're offline" / "Connect to the internet to delete your account." and does nothing else. Online it fetches each household's member count (`get_household_members`) and confirms "Delete your account?": the account, aisle order and settings go; each household where the user is the only member is named with its list and recipe counts as deleted; the shared ones are named on one line as staying with their other members (if any member count can't be fetched, only the first sentence). A second "Are you sure?" follows, so one mis-tap can't do it. Then, behind a full-screen "Deleting your account…" overlay that can't be dismissed, it pushes the outbox (best effort, up to 3 seconds, so shared households get the user's last changes), calls `delete_account`, wipes the device, signs out of Supabase locally (the server session is gone; its error is ignored) and of Google, and lands on sign-in with the toast "Your account was deleted.". A failure says "Couldn't delete your account" / "Nothing was deleted. Try again later." and changes nothing on the device.
- After deleting, the device keeps nothing of the user's (`wipeLocalData`, `src/features/auth/local-wipe.ts`): every user and household row in SQLite goes in one transaction (households, lists, items, contributions, recipes and their ingredients, custom aisles, overrides, usage, user settings, household and personal aisle orders, failed imports, the outbox, sync cursors and the HLC clock), then the App Group list snapshot (as on sign-out, 4.9), the Siri and share queues, the plans store and its cached answer (4.12), the in-memory invites (4.7) and notifications (4.11). Kept: the global catalog (system aisles, categorized ingredients; names still pending are the user's and go) and device-only preferences that hold no user data (aisle colours, item rows, keep screen on, the import sheet's switch) with the catalog and one-time backfill markers. Every table in `src/db/schema.ts` must have a wipe step; a test fails otherwise.
- Note: App Store Guideline 4.8 expects a privacy-focused login (e.g. Sign in with Apple) alongside Google for a *public* release. Fine to ignore for personal/TestFlight use; revisit only if this goes to the public App Store.

### 4.9 Siri (req 12)

**Platform constraint** (learned in stash): App Shortcut phrases can only contain `AppEnum`/`AppEntity` parameters, not free-form text. "Hey Siri, add milk to The Perfect Bite" in one breath is not expected to work. The interaction is two turns.

**Interaction**
1. User: "Hey Siri, **add to The Perfect Bite**."
2. Siri: "What do you want to add?"
3. User: "Two avocados."
4. Siri: "Added two avocados to Groceries." The app does not open.

**Naming a list**: lists are exposed as an `AppEntity`, so a phrase can include one: "Hey Siri, add to **Costco** in The Perfect Bite." Without one, the item goes to the user's default list (4.1).

**Behavior**
- The spoken text goes through the same parser and normalizer as a typed add (4.2). One utterance is one item in v1 (multi-item splitting is Later, 11.3).
- Duplicates always Combine, with no prompt (4.4). Siri's reply doesn't mention it.
- Works offline and while the phone is locked (after first unlock since boot), using stash's App Group queue design.
- Unknown ingredients show "Sorting..." on every device until categorized, as with any add.
- If the user isn't signed in, the item waits in the queue and is added after sign-in.
- Also available, for free, through the Shortcuts app and the Action Button.

**Implementation**
- A Swift `AppIntent` named `AddToPerfectBiteIntent` in an App Intents extension under `targets/`, added with `@bacons/apple-targets` (as stash's `targets/save-intent`).
  - `@Parameter(title: "Item", requestValueDialog: "What do you want to add?") var text: String`
  - `@Parameter(title: "List") var list: ListEntity?`
  - `openAppWhenRun = false`
- `ListEntity` values come from a **list snapshot** (`siri/lists.json`: id, name, household id and household name per list across all the user's households; `households`, every household the user belongs to as `{ id, name }`, oldest first, including any with no live lists, so the share sheet can save to one; plus the default list id and `lastOpenedListId`, the list screen last opened on this device or null; and `userId`, the signed-in user's id, which the share extension copies into each envelope and matches against the device credential (4.10); Siri ignores the household and user fields) that the app writes to the App Group container on launch and whenever lists, memberships or the default list change, and deletes on sign-out. Archived lists are left out. Settings > Siri adds to picks the default list. Settings > Siri adds to names each list's household ("*Groceries* in *Smith home*") when the user's lists span several households. Siri itself shows the list name only, so two lists with the same name aren't told apart there yet. The app also rewrites the snapshot whenever a list screen opens, so `lastOpenedListId` stays current; the share extension (4.10) reads the same file for its list picker.
- The App Group container holds, besides `siri/lists.json` and the Siri queue (`siri/pending/`), the **share queue** `share/pending/<uuidv7>.json` (4.10), and for each share being uploaded in the background its request body `share/upload/<id>.json` (the file the upload task sends) and `share/upload-state/<id>.json` (`{ startedAt, attempts }`, marked accepted when the upload got a 2xx). A share envelope is `{ id: uuidv7, v?: 2, userId?: uuid, url, listId | null, householdId?: uuid | null, addToList?: boolean, page?: { title?, jsonLd?: string[], text? }, createdAt, appVersion, source: "share" }`, with the same caps as the import request's `page` (6.6). `userId` is the snapshot's `userId` when the share was saved and `v` is 2; an envelope without them was written by an older extension (version 1) and counts as the signed-in user's (4.10). Like the Siri envelope it has one Zod schema in `src/shared/` (`schemas/share.ts`), a matching Swift `Codable` in `targets/_shared/`, and a contract test; files are written atomically (`.tmp`, then rename).
- The App Group is shared with the share extension only for files. The **device credential** (4.10, 6.12) lives in a dedicated shared Keychain access group (`keychain-access-groups` on the app and the share extension, in `app.json` `ios.entitlements`, `targets/share-recipe/expo-target.config.js` and the EAS `appExtensions` entitlements), written by the app with `expo-secure-store`, `AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`.
- `perform()`:
  1. Writes an envelope to the App Group queue: `{ id: uuid, source: "siri", text, listId?, createdAt, locale?, appVersion }` (`src/shared/schemas/siri.ts`).
  2. *Not built yet* (steps 2 and the `siri-add` function below; `future/siri-tasks.md`; today a Siri add reaches the household only when the app next drains the queue): makes a **best-effort upload** (timeout ~3 s) to the `siri-add` Edge Function, using the device credential in the shared Keychain access group (6.12, with a `siri.add` scope added for it), never the app's session (`docs/spikes.md` §2). This matters more than in stash: a household member at the store should see the item without the speaker opening the app.
  3. Replies with the dialog. If the upload failed, the reply is the same; the item is uploaded later.
- `siri-add` Edge Function (not built yet): parses and normalizes with the shared code, resolves the list, applies auto-Combine, inserts the list item and contribution with ids derived from the envelope id (so a repeat upload is a no-op), and categorizes unknown names inline by calling the shared `categorize()` from `categorize-ingredients` (same limits and LLM boundaries, 6.4). No other process categorizes them later.
- The app drains the queue on launch, foreground and sign-in (as stash does; signed out, adds wait), one drain at a time, oldest first. Each envelope is parsed like a typed add and goes to its list, or the default list if that list is gone or archived, as a Siri contribution that Combines silently (4.4), dated when it was spoken. The item and contribution ids are UUIDv5s of the envelope id (`siriAddIds`), so an envelope applied twice (a crash before its file is deleted, or already applied by `siri-add`) is a no-op. Applied files are deleted; unreadable ones, and text with nothing to add, are logged and deleted. New names go to categorization in one batch, and a list screen that's showing gets one toast, "Siri added 2 items".
- Envelope format: one Zod schema in `src/shared/` and a matching Swift `Codable`, with a contract test (as stash).
- **App name**: the App Shortcut phrase must include the app's display name, so the bundle display name must be exactly what users say. Stash had to use phrases like "Add to my Stash"; test early whether "add to The Perfect Bite" is recognized reliably, and register a few variants ("Add to Perfect Bite", "Add something to The Perfect Bite"). **[SPIKE]**

### 4.10 Recipe import (req 14)

On a recipe page, the user picks a list and taps **Add**. The recipe is saved to the household (its name, source URL and every ingredient), and all its ingredients go onto that list, grouped under the recipe in By recipe view (4.6). The user never types an ingredient. Extraction happens on the server (6.6).

**From the share sheet**
1. In Safari (or any app that shares a link): Share → **The Perfect Bite**. The extension accepts one web URL, one web page, or text containing a URL (apps like Instagram and Messages share a URL inside text; the first `http(s)` URL is used). It doesn't take images: photos of a recipe come in through the app only (**From photo…**, below).
2. The sheet shows the page title (or the host when there's none) with the host on a second line; a **Save to** household picker (hidden with one household), defaulting to the household of the list the list picker would pick (below); a row **Also add ingredients to a list** with a switch; and Cancel. Off, there's no list picker and the button reads **Save recipe**. On, a picker of that household's lists shows (same default rule within the household) and the button reads **Save & add to <list>**. A household with no lists disables the switch, with "This household has no lists" under it. Changing household resets the list to that household's default. The switch starts off the first time and then remembers its last state on the device (`share/prefs.json` in the App Group); the household isn't remembered. With no households in the snapshot yet it says "Open The Perfect Bite once to set up" instead. The rules live in `src/features/recipes/save-choice.ts` (tested) and are mirrored in Swift (`ShareChoice.swift`).
3. Saving writes a share envelope to the App Group queue (4.9: `householdId`, `addToList`, `listId` only when on, `userId` from the list snapshot and `v: 2`) and then, when the shared Keychain holds a device credential (6.12) for the same `userId` that hasn't expired, **starts a background upload** to `share-ingest` (6.12): it writes the request body to `share/upload/<id>.json`, starts a `URLSessionUploadTask` from that file on a background `URLSession` (identifier `com.jgp.perfectbite.share.upload`, `sharedContainerIdentifier` `group.com.jgp.perfectbite`) with `Authorization: Bearer <credential>` and `Idempotency-Key: <envelope id>`, to the fixed `share-ingest` URL in the extension's Info.plist (set from the app config by `targets/share-recipe/expo-target.config.js`), and records `share/upload-state/<id>.json`. With no credential, an expired one or one for another user, it skips the upload and the app submits the share later (step 5). Either way the sheet shows "Saved. We'll prepare it in the background." (both switch states) for about a second, then closes. The extension runs no React Native and never uses the app's Supabase session (`docs/spikes.md` §2). In Safari, the extension also captures the page's title, its `application/ld+json` blocks and its readable text, so pages behind a sign-in or paywall import from what the user sees; other apps only give the URL.
   - When the upload finishes after the extension has exited, iOS wakes the app (`application(_:handleEventsForBackgroundURLSession:completionHandler:)`). A local Expo module in `modules/` (an `ExpoAppDelegateSubscriber`; `ios/` is never edited) recreates the session for that identifier, takes its delegate events (a 2xx deletes `share/upload/<id>.json` and marks the upload state accepted; anything else leaves both) and calls the completion handler. No answer is treated as final: the envelope stays until reconciliation (step 5) sees that the server has it. How soon the upload runs, offline, or after the user force-quits the app isn't promised; the envelope covers every gap.
4. **The server imports it** (6.12). `share-ingest` records the operation in `share_imports` (5) under the envelope id, and `share-import-worker` extracts the recipe (6.6), then saves it to the household and adds its ingredients to the list in one transaction (**Which recipe** and **What goes on the list**, below), so it reaches every household device through ordinary sync (7.2) without anyone opening the app. The envelope id is the import id, so a share submitted twice (a background upload and the app) is one operation and creates one recipe. The sharer gets a push when it's ready or has failed (4.13).
5. The app **reconciles with the server** on launch, foreground, reconnect and sign-in, instead of draining: it reads `share/pending/*.json` and keeps the envelopes whose `userId` is the signed-in user (one without `userId`, from an older extension, counts as theirs; another user's are left alone and never submitted); asks `share_import_status(ids)`; for ids the server has, deletes the envelope and its `share/upload/` and `share/upload-state/` files; and submits the rest to `share-ingest` with the user's session and the same id, deleting the local files once the server accepts them. Whichever of the background upload and the app's submit arrives second gets the existing operation back. Signed out, envelopes wait. An unreadable envelope is logged and deleted. The app never extracts or saves a share itself.

**From inside the app**
- In-app imports (a link or a photo) stay on the device: the user is watching, so the app calls `import-recipe` (or `import-recipe-photo`) and saves the result itself, through the same list rules the server uses (`src/shared/domain/`). Only shares import on the server.
- The sheet offers the share sheet's choices: a **Save to** household picker (pills; hidden with one household), then **Also add ingredients to a list** with a switch that shows that household's list picker when on; the button reads **Save recipe** or **Save & add to <list>**, and a household with no lists disables the switch with "This household has no lists". Changing household resets the list to that household's default. With the switch off it imports recipe-only (as a recipe-only share).
- **From the Recipes tab** (a link in the Add recipe sheet's one field, 4.5, which the empty state's **Import from link** also opens; the sheet is replaced by this one, with the link filled in): the household follows the share sheet's rule (the household of the last opened list, then of the default list, then the first), and the switch starts off and remembers its last state on the device (device setting `importAddToList`, separate from the extension's). Opened with a list (`list` param; the list's menu no longer offers it, but the sheet still takes one): that list's household, the switch on with that list chosen (the user can still turn it off or change list; that isn't remembered). The rules are `initialSaveChoice` in `src/features/recipes/save-choice.ts`.
- A small sheet has a URL field and a **Paste** button. The clipboard is read only when Paste is tapped, so iOS shows no paste prompt when the sheet opens.
- **Save recipe** / **Save & add to <list>** first runs the checks that fail at once, and shows them inline with the sheet open: "That doesn't look like a link" and "You're offline" (from expo-network). Otherwise it starts the import, closes the sheet straight away and posts a `recipeImport` notification (4.11): the recipe's icon tile (`recipeHue`, fork and knife), title "Importing your recipe", body "We'll let you know when it's ready.", with no tap action beyond dismissing. The result comes later as a notification with the same id (**When the import finishes**, below), whether the sheet was opened from the Recipes tab or a list.
- **Device fallback.** Many recipe sites answer a server fetch with a bot check (Cloudflare's "Just a moment...", "Security Verification", Vercel Security Checkpoint, AllRecipes' JS-only "Simple Page"). When the server answers `fetch_failed` for a pasted link, the app loads the link in a hidden WebView, waits up to 20 s for the real page (a challenge page often reloads into it), captures the page's title, JSON-LD blocks and readable text with the same caps as the share extension, and imports again with that page (6.6). Nothing shows on screen. Only `fetch_failed` falls back, never "not a recipe", a rate limit or an invalid URL.
- **From a photo** (`recipe_import`, the same feature and monthly allowance as a link, 4.12; the Add recipe sheet's **From photo…**, 4.5): **Choose from library** picks up to 3 images in the order tapped (`allowsMultipleSelection`, `selectionLimit: 3`, `orderedSelection`); **Take photo** takes one (asking for camera access; a refusal says so in an alert) and then asks "Is there another page?" with **Add another page** (up to 3) or **Use photos**. Each page is shrunk on the device with `downscalePhoto(uri, { longEdge: 2000, quality: 0.8 })` (`IMPORT_PHOTO`, `src/features/recipes/photo-import.ts`). The Add recipe sheet is replaced by the import sheet, titled "Add recipe from photo", with the same Save to picker, switch (same defaults and `importAddToList`), list picker and buttons, and the pages as 3:4 thumbnails (`importPageThumb`, VoiceOver "Page 1 of 2") in place of the link field, with a note under them: a small page glyph (`doc.text`, `inkSecondary`, `iconSmall`) and "The pages are kept with the recipe, so everyone in the household can see the original." (`footnote` `inkSecondary`). Saving sends the JPEGs as base64 to `import-recipe-photo` (6.10); as for a link, offline says so inline, and otherwise the sheet closes at once with the "Importing your recipe" notification and the result replaces it. Success always creates a new recipe (there's no URL to reuse one by), with no `source_url` and no photo (so no source credit), with the response's tags, time, steps and step match, and adds to the list as a link import does, with the same notification, toast and Undo; the recipe then shows the Add photo tile (without From the page). The recipe keeps its pages: once it's saved, `importRecipeFromPhotos` uploads each page's downscaled JPEG (the files it sent) to the private `recipe-pages` bucket (5) at a new `<household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg` each, one at a time and in order (`uploadRecipePages`, beside `uploadRecipePhoto` in `src/features/recipes/photo-upload.ts`), then sets `recipes.source_pages` to their paths through the usual recipe update, before the result's notification. This is best effort: if a page fails to upload, the ones that went up are removed, `source_pages` stays null and the import still succeeds. The page files in the cache are deleted after. Undo deletes the recipe as before, and the server removes its pages (5). A failure's notification gives the photo wording: "That photo doesn't look like a recipe" (`not_a_recipe`), "Try again in a minute" (the per-minute limit or unavailable), "You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow." (`daily_limit`, 4.12), "You've used this month's recipe imports. They reset on Nov 1." (`limit_reached`, the household's monthly limit, shared with link imports, 4.12; the date is the answer's `resets_at`), "Recipe import isn't included in your plan." (`not_entitled`, which also drops `recipe_import` for that household until the plan reloads), and "You're offline" (the connection went during the request; it queues nothing). Tapping it opens the failed import's review sheet (**Failed photo imports**, below). A failed photo import uploads no pages; it's recorded in `failed_imports` with its pages kept on the phone, so it can be tried again without picking them again.

**Which list.** The picker defaults to the list last opened on this device (`lastOpenedListId`, 4.1), then the user's default list (4.1), then the first list; archived and deleted lists don't count. If a share's list is gone or archived (or no longer in the share's household) by the time the server commits it, it goes to the user's default list. With no lists left at all, the recipe is saved recipe-only into the share's household, nothing is added, and the receipt says so (`list_gone`, 5): the "ready" push (4.13) reads "Saved *Lemon pasta*. Its list was gone, so nothing was added."

**Recipe only.** A share can save the recipe without adding anything to a list: its envelope has `addToList: false`, `listId: null` and the `householdId` to save it to. An envelope without `addToList` (queued by an older extension) adds to a list as above. The server imports it into that household with the same import id and the same reuse by canonical URL, and adds no list items. A sharer who is no longer in the share's household (left, removed, or it was deleted) when the server processes it gets a permanent failure with nothing written, whether or not the share adds to a list. In-app, Undo deletes the recipe only if this import created it. In the imports card a waiting recipe-only share reads "host · to *household*".

**Which recipe.** A recipe's `source_url` is stored in canonical form: lowercase host, no `www.`, no fragment, no tracking parameters (`utm_*`, `fbclid`, `gclid`, `ref`, `mc_*`), no trailing slash. If the list's household already has a recipe with the same canonical `source_url`, the import reuses it: no duplicate recipe is created, and the existing recipe is left as it is. Otherwise a new recipe is created in that household with the page's recipe name, the source URL, notes "Serves N" when the page says, and its ingredients in page order (name, quantity, unit, note, as 4.5). When the response has a step match (`stepIngredients`, 6.6), the new recipe saves it as `step_ingredients` (4.5), each index mapped to the `recipe_ingredients` row it created, with the basis worked out from the saved steps and ingredients, so the match is fresh and `ensureStepMatch` makes no call. A match that doesn't line up with the saved steps or ingredients, or none (null, or an older server), leaves the recipe unmatched for `ensureStepMatch`. A reused recipe keeps its own match, as it keeps its own tags (4.14).

**What goes on the list.** Every ingredient, at 1×, with no checklist step, as the recipe's contributions, exactly like 4.4 bulk add with Combine-silently duplicate handling: an open match Combines, a checked match is unchecked with its amounts replaced, and the same ingredient twice folds into one item. Household usage counts as a recipe add (4.2). Categories for new names come back with the import, so nothing shows "Sorting…" unless categorizing them failed (then 6.3's catch-up retries them as usual). The device and the server plan the same writes from the same rules (`planRecipeImport` in `src/shared/domain/`). On the server the recipe, its ingredients, the list adds and the usage counts commit in one transaction (6.12): an item checked or changed between planning and committing makes it plan again, so a just-checked item reopens with the recipe's amounts (4.4) rather than gaining a contribution while checked, and an amount someone edits at the same moment keeps the import's amount on top, as two phones would.

**The toast.** On the list screen: "Added *Chicken tikka masala* · 12 items" for 5 seconds with **Undo** and **View recipe**. Undo removes every contribution the import added to the list (as bulk-add Undo does) and deletes the recipe if this import created it; a reused recipe stays. View recipe opens the recipe. When it shows on the Recipes tab instead (tapping the success notification while the tab is showing), it sits above the tab bar: "Added *Chicken tikka masala* · 12 items on Groceries" with **Undo** and **Open list**, and the recipe's card is outlined in `primary` while it shows. A recipe-only import shows no list toast; on the Recipes tab it says "Saved *Chicken tikka masala*" (or "*Chicken tikka masala* is already in your recipes" when it was reused) with **Undo** (only when the import created the recipe) and **View recipe**, the card outlined as above.
