A share imported on the server shows no toast and has no Undo: its recipe and items arrive through sync like another member's, and the sharer hears from the push (4.13).

**Not a recipe, or the import fails**
- In the app, an invalid link and being offline show inline and the sheet stays open; everything else is known only after the sheet has closed, so it comes as the failure notification (below) in the same plain words. The words: "That page doesn't look like a recipe" (anything that isn't a single recipe: an article, a page listing many recipes), "Couldn't reach that site" (the page couldn't be fetched or isn't HTML, on the server or the device), "This site blocked the import. Open it in Safari and tap Share → The Perfect Bite." (the device fallback never got past the bot check), "Try again in a minute" (the per-minute limit or the service unavailable), "You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow." (the function answered `daily_limit`, the user's daily safety limit, 4.12), "You've used this month's recipe imports. They reset on Nov 1." (the function answered `limit_reached`, the household's monthly limit, 4.12; the date is its `resets_at` as a short date, never "Try again in a minute"), "That doesn't look like a link" (an invalid URL) [Default], "You're offline" [Default], and "Recipe import isn't included in your plan." (the function answered `not_entitled`, 4.12). Nothing is written.
- For a share, failures are **server state** (`share_imports`, read through `my_share_imports`, 5), so they show on all of the sharer's devices and never on other members'. They come in the same plain words. Failing at once, with no retries: `not_a_recipe`, `invalid_url`, `not_entitled` (the extension can't know the plan, so shares are accepted on any plan and fail with that reason), the sharer no longer in the share's household ("You're no longer in that household." [Default]), and `daily_limit` and `limit_reached`, which **fail with their reason rather than wait** ("You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow.", "You've used this month's recipe imports. They reset on Nov 1.", the date from the kept `resets_at`): nothing retries on its own, and the user taps Try again after the reset. `rate_limited` (the per-minute limit) is a short throttle: the job waits for the next minute without counting an attempt. Other failures are temporary (network, 5xx, `unavailable`, model errors, `fetch_failed`, a commit conflict, a lost lease) and retry on the server with backoff (30 s, 2 min, 10 min, 1 h, 6 h, with jitter); after 8 attempts the share fails with the last reason ("Couldn't reach that site" for `fetch_failed`, else "Something went wrong."). A failed share is pushed to the sharer (4.13).
- **Failed in-app imports** are kept on the device only (`failed_imports`: its `kind` (`link` or `photo`), the url (links only), title, reason, when, and where it was going: the household, and the list when there was one; for a photo, its `pages`; not synced). Failed shares aren't recorded there.
- **Failed photo imports** (`docs/design/photo-import-retry/`, boards A–E) keep their pages on the phone. When a photo import fails (any reason in its notification but "You're offline" from the inline check, which keeps the sheet open), `importRecipeFromPhotos` moves each page's downscaled JPEG (the file it sent) out of the cache into `failed-imports/<id>/<n>.jpg` under the app's document directory (iOS may clear the cache) and records a `failed_imports` row with `kind` `photo`, no url, `pages` (the file names, in page order) and where it was going. The pages are never uploaded, synced or seen by anyone else. They're deleted with their row: when a Try again succeeds (after `uploadRecipePages` sends them up as the recipe's pages, as for any photo import), on Dismiss, by `wipeLocalData`, and 30 days after the failure (`created_at`), pruned at launch. A row whose files have gone is deleted quietly. Try again (on the card or in the review sheet) sends the kept pages to `import-recipe-photo` again, where it was going (the same fallbacks as a link's Try again), with the "Importing your recipe" notification and its result as for a new photo import; success creates the recipe and removes the row, another failure keeps the row (and its files) with the new reason.
  - **The review sheet** (board D): tapping a photo row's pages (VoiceOver "2 pages from a photo, button") or its failure notification opens the "Add recipe from photo" import sheet filled from the row. It has a `dangerSoft` banner (an `exclamationmark.circle` and the text in `danger`) saying when it failed and why ("Couldn't import this yesterday at 6:42 PM. Try again in a minute."), then the pages as 3:4 thumbnails, each with a round remove button (VoiceOver "Remove page 1 of 2"; hidden when only one page is left), and a dashed **Add page** tile while there are fewer than 3 (Take photo / Choose from library, as in the import sheet). The note under them reads "Kept on this phone for 30 days, or until the recipe imports." in place of the pages note. The Save to picker, switch and list picker are set to where it was going (falling back as Try again does). The button is **Try again** / **Try again & add to <list>** with an `arrow.clockwise`, and under it is **Dismiss and delete photos** (a soft destructive button), which deletes the row and its files and closes the sheet. Try again saves the sheet's pages and target to the row first, so another failure keeps the edits; a removed page's file is deleted then. Closing the sheet any other way leaves the row as it was.
  - **When the same pages can't succeed** (board E): `limit_reached` shows Try again disabled, reading "Try again Nov 1" (the 1st of the month after the failure, in UTC), until that date. `not_a_recipe` swaps Try again for **Use other photos** (`Pill soft`, `camera`), which offers Take photo / Choose from library and then opens the review sheet with the new pages in place of the kept ones (they replace the row's pages only when its Try again is tapped). Every other reason keeps Try again. Link rows don't change.

**The imports card.** While anything is importing, waiting or failed, the Recipes tab shows one card at the top. A share counts as waiting while its envelope hasn't reached the server or its `my_share_imports` row is `received`, `extracting`, `extracted` or `committing`, and as failed when the row is `failed`; an in-app import counts while it runs, and as failed while its `failed_imports` row exists. Its heading says what matters most: failures first ("Couldn't import 1 recipe", with "1 more waiting" or "Importing 1 of 2…" under it), then an in-app import running ("Importing 1 of 2…" over the recipe and its list), then shares waiting ("2 recipes waiting" over "We'll prepare them in the background.", or "You're offline. They'll import when you're back." while offline with envelopes the server doesn't have yet). Tapping it expands a row per import, in two labelled groups when there are both: **Couldn't import**, each with its title (or host), the reason and **Try again**: for a share, `retry_share_import(id)` puts the same operation back on the server (same id, never charged again, 6.12) and it shows as waiting; for an in-app import, it imports again where it was going (recipe-only into its household, or onto the same list, falling back to the default list when that's gone; rows recorded before targets were kept go to the default list; success removes the row, another failure keeps it with the new reason), with **Dismiss** beside it (in-app rows only; dismissing a failed share is open, Q17). A failed photo import's row (board B) shows its pages in place of the icon tile, as a small stack (the first page as a 3:4 thumbnail, a second behind it turned 6°, and a count badge with more than one page), "From a photo" as its title and "2 pages · to *list*" (or "· to *household*" when recipe-only) over the reason. With one failed import and nothing else in the card, the collapsed card shows it directly (board A): its pages (or the link's icon tile), "Couldn't import 1 recipe" over the reason (followed by "Your photos are kept." for a photo) and **Try again**, without expanding. While a photo import's Try again runs, its pages carry a spinner on a `photoScrim` (board C) and the heading reads "Importing 1 of 1…" over "From a photo · to *list*". **Waiting**, each share with its title (or host) over "host · to *list*", and **Remove** while its envelope hasn't reached the server (deletes the envelope and its upload files); once the server has it, it shows a spinner instead and can't be removed. The Recipes tab's icon has a badge counting waiting shares and failed imports. The card's state comes from the App Group's envelopes and `my_share_imports` (the sharer's own rows), read again at each reconcile and when a recipe-import push (4.13) arrives; it is never stored.

**When the import finishes.** The sheet has always closed by then, so the result is always posted as a `recipeImport` notification (4.11), with the id of the "Importing your recipe" one: it replaces that banner in place if it's still showing, and queues after whatever is showing otherwise. Nothing waits on the list for later; the list or Recipes tab toast (with Undo) comes from tapping the success notification.
- Success: the recipe's icon tile (`recipeHue`, fork and knife), title "Recipe added", body "Added *Chicken tikka masala* · 12 items to Groceries". Tapping it opens that list with the usual import toast (Undo, View recipe); if that list is already showing, the toast shows there.
- Recipe-only success: the same tile, title "Recipe saved", body "Saved *Chicken tikka masala* to *Smith home*". Tapping it opens the recipe.
- Failure: a rose warning tile, title "Couldn't import recipe", body the sheet's plain-words reason (above). Tapping it reopens the sheet with the same household, switch state, list and link. The device fallback still runs first.
- A share's result comes as the recipe-import push (4.13), shown as a `recipeImport` notification while the app is open; there's no "Importing your recipe" banner for a share.

**Offline.** A share always succeeds in the extension, online or not: the envelope waits in the App Group until the background upload or the app (open, signed in, online) gets it to the server, which imports it. A signed-out app keeps it until after sign-in. The in-app sheet needs a connection; offline it says so and queues nothing.

### 4.11 In-app notifications (req 13)

A banner that drops in at the top of the screen, over whatever is showing (sheets too), to say that something the user started has finished while they were doing something else. This is the base each feature adds its own notifications to. Push notifications (4.13) are separate; one that arrives while the app is open becomes one of these.

- **Kinds.** A feature posts a notification with a `kind`, one per use case, so per-kind settings can switch them off later without touching the features. The kinds are `recipeImport` (4.10: "Importing your recipe" as the import sheet closes, then its result in place of it), and `itemsAdded` and `shopping` for a push that arrives while the app is open (4.13).
- **The banner.** A `surface` card 8 pt below the top safe area and 16 pt from the sides, with radius `card` and the floating shadow in light mode. It holds an icon tile, a title (`headline`) and up to two lines of body (`footnote`, `inkSecondary`), with an optional part of the body in bold. Tapping the banner runs its action and dismisses it. Swiping it up dismisses it. It times out after 6 seconds (`motion.notificationTimeoutMs`). It slides down on arrival and up on leaving, and only fades under Reduce Motion. A success or warning haptic plays when it appears. VoiceOver announces "title. body", and the banner is a button whose hint says what tapping does.
- **One at a time.** Notifications queue in the order they're posted, and the next one drops in when the one showing goes. Posting with the id of a notification that is already queued or showing replaces it in place, and a replaced banner that's showing restarts its timer.
- **Only while the app is in the foreground.** The timer starts when the banner shows. A notification posted while the app is in the background waits until the app comes back. A banner showing when the app goes to the background stays up and restarts its timer when the app returns.
- **Not kept.** Notifications live in memory only. There's no inbox, and nothing survives the app quitting or signing out (sign-out clears the queue).

### 4.12 Admin and plans

**The dashboard and the admin portal are the admin tools.** The app is run from the Supabase dashboard (Authentication → Users, the Table Editor, the SQL Editor, the Edge Function logs) and from a small admin portal, a web app in `admin/` run locally against hosted Supabase, for the jobs the dashboard makes awkward: finding a household and changing its plan, editing what each plan includes and its monthly limits, seeing how much of each AI feature people use, and following what a user did (User activity, below). Everything else (deleting a user, reading logs) stays on the dashboard. How to do common jobs there is in [`docs/admin.md`](admin.md).

- **Admins** are the users with a row in `private.admins` (5), added and removed in the Table Editor; a table rather than a role in the JWT, so removing one takes effect on their next call. `public.is_admin()` says whether the caller is one (false with no user). The owner's account (jgpasch@gmail.com) is made an admin by the migration, and the local dev user by `supabase/seed.sql`.
- **The server checks every portal action.** The portal holds only the project URL and publishable key and signs in like the app does (its login screen is only UX). Each action is a `public.admin_*` function (`security definer`, executable by `authenticated` only) whose first step is `private.require_admin()`, which raises `not authorized` (`42501`) for anyone else. Portal functions write `admin.admin_actions` like the dashboard's do, with `performed_by` set to the admin's user id (the column's default, `session_user`, would be `authenticator` from the API), and still require a note.
- **Signing in to the portal**: `/sign-in` has **Continue with Google** (Supabase OAuth, back through `/auth/callback`, which exchanges the code for a session); against local Supabase (a `127.0.0.1` or `localhost` URL) it also has the dev email-and-code form, the code going to the local mail viewer. `proxy.ts` refreshes the session and sends anyone signed out to `/sign-in`. The signed-in layout asks `is_admin()`: a non-admin sees only **Not authorized** ("This account isn't an admin.") with their email and **Sign out**, and so does an admin whose later call answers `42501` (removed meanwhile). The sidebar's foot shows the admin's email and **Sign out**.
- The dashboard runs as `postgres` and doesn't know the app's rules. So a change that has rules goes through a tested SQL function in the `admin` schema, run as a saved SQL snippet, never by editing or deleting rows by hand. A change with no rules beyond the table's own constraints is made in the Table Editor.
- `admin` isn't one of the API's exposed schemas (`supabase/config.toml`), and its schema usage and every function's execute are revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`, so only the dashboard can call them.
- Each admin function writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (5) in the same transaction. `performed_by` is always `postgres` from the dashboard, so each function takes a `p_note`, and the snippets always fill it in: it's the only record of who asked and why.

**`admin.delete_user(p_user_id, p_note)`**: account deletion (4.8) for a support request; users delete their own with `delete_account()` (4.8). Both run `private.delete_user_core(p_user_id)`, which does the steps below except the audit row and returns the households array.
- Raises `user not found` for an unknown id, changing nothing.
- For each of the user's households (in their household order) it runs the leaving rules, `private.remove_member_from_household` (4.7): the last member leaving deletes the household and its data (`deleted`); the last owner leaving promotes the member who joined earliest (`promoted`); otherwise the user just leaves (`left`). The helper doesn't create a personal household; that's for callers whose user goes on using the app (`leave_household`, `remove_member`).
- Then it deletes the `auth.users` row, which cascades their memberships, profile, aisle order and settings. Rows they made in households that remain (items added or checked, contributions, recipes, invites) stay, with `added_by`, `checked_by`, `created_by` and `used_by` set to null.
- Last, it deletes the user's activity entries and scrubs them from other users' `member.removed` and `member.role_changed` entries (4.8, User activity below).
- Its audit row has `action` `delete_user`, the user's id, the note, and `details`: the email, the full name (key `display_name`) and, per household, its id, name and outcome (with `promoted_user_id` for `promoted`). It returns the same `details`.

**Plans and features.** A feature is a text key; a plan is a named set of features (`plans`, `plan_features`, `household_plans`, 5). A plan belongs to a household, not a user: everyone in the household gets its features and shares its monthly allowance, so inviting people doesn't multiply it. Plans cover only the two AI features that cost real money. Everything else the AI does is for everyone on every plan with no monthly limit: **categorize** (6.1; it fills the global catalog, so it helps everyone, and nobody gains from overusing it), **step matching** for recipes typed or edited by hand (6.8) and **Suggest tags** (6.7). Those keep per-user per-minute limits and per-user daily ceilings (**Safety limits**, below), which stop a scripted account rather than a person. Every number (the plans' monthly limits and the safety limits) is a table row an admin edits in the portal, never a constant in code.
- **The features** are two: `recipe_import` (link import and photo import alike, one allowance, 4.10, 6.6, 6.10; checked by `import-recipe`, `import-recipe-photo` and, for shares, `share-import-worker` (6.12); the categorize, tags and step match an import makes in the same request are part of it) and `recipe_photo_ai` (the AI makes a recipe's one photo, 4.5, 6.11; checked by `generate-recipe-photo`). `categorize`, `step_ingredients`, `recipe_tags` and `recipe_import_photo` were features until the move to households; `recipe_import_photo` folded into `recipe_import` (a plan that had it has `recipe_import`), and the others stopped being plan features. With them went the import rule (a plan with an import needed `categorize`, the `plan_features_import_needs_categorize` trigger): an import still categorizes inline, under categorize's safety limits, whatever the plan.
- **Seeded plans**: **founding** ("Founding": `recipe_import` 50 a month and `recipe_photo_ai` 20 a month; the default until paid plans launch), **unlimited** ("Unlimited": both, with no monthly limit) and **free** ("Free": neither; categorize, step matching and Suggest tags still work). Founding replaced **plus** and Free replaced **none**, ids included (plan ids are referenced by `household_plans` and the audit log, so the move to households was the last cheap moment to rename them; old `admin.admin_actions` rows keep `plus` and `none` as written). **basic** (only `categorize` and step matching, which everyone now has) was deleted, its households moved to free. Older still, base and standard were renamed none and basic (`20261111130000_rename_plans.sql`). At most one plan is the default (a partial unique index). Plans are made and edited in the portal (below).
- **Founding is frozen once paid plans launch.** Every household made before paid plans launch is a founding household: it stays on founding, free of charge, for good, through its explicit `household_plans` row (below). Until the launch, new features may be added to founding; after it, never: a new feature goes into a new plan, so founders get exactly what founding had at launch, and a paid plan never ends up offering less than the free one founders kept. Changing founding's limits is still allowed and applies to every founder. How to launch: `docs/admin.md`, Launching paid plans.
- **The feature keys stay fixed in code**: the `check` constraints on `plan_features` and `plan_limits` (5) and `private.feature_keys()`, which gives their fixed order (`recipe_import`, `recipe_photo_ai`). A new feature is a migration, since an Edge Function has to check it; the portal only turns existing keys on and off per plan.
- **Editing plans** is done in the portal, through these functions. Like the user ones, each starts with `require_admin()`, raises and changes nothing on any error (`plan not found` `P0002` for an unknown plan, `note required` `22023` for a null or blank note), and otherwise writes one `admin.admin_actions` row: `action` the function's name without `admin_`, `target_plan_id` the plan, the note, `performed_by` the admin's id, and `details` `{ before, after, households }`, the plan as `admin_list_plans` shows it before and after (null before a create and after a delete) and how many households are on it. Each returns those `details`.
  - `admin_list_plans()` (jsonb): every plan by id, each with `id`, `name`, `is_default`, `households` (the count of its `household_plans` rows; the default counts no one extra, since every household has a row) and `features`, the ones it has in the fixed order, each with its `monthly_limit` (null for none). Read-only, no audit row.
  - `admin_create_plan(p_id, p_name, p_copy_from default null, p_note default null)`: a new plan, never the default, with a copy of `p_copy_from`'s `plan_features` and `plan_limits` (none without it). The id matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{1,30}$` (`invalid plan id`, `22023`) and is unused (`plan exists`, `23505`); a blank name is `name required` (`22023`). `p_note` has a default only because it follows `p_copy_from`; it's still required.
  - `admin_rename_plan(p_id, p_name, p_note)`: the name, never the id (ids are referenced by `household_plans` and the audit log).
  - `admin_set_default_plan(p_id, p_note)`: clears the old default first, for the partial unique index, then sets this one. It decides only the plan new households start on: no existing household moves. `details` also has `previous_default`. The portal confirms "New households will start on <plan>. Existing households keep their plan."
  - `admin_set_plan_feature(p_plan_id, p_feature, p_enabled, p_monthly_limit default null, p_note default null)`: enabling adds the feature and an explicit `plan_limits` row with the limit (null for no limit; replacing the row if the plan already has it); disabling removes both. After paid plans launch, never enable a new feature on founding (above); nothing enforces it.
  - `admin_set_plan_limit(p_plan_id, p_feature, p_monthly_limit, p_note)`: upserts the `plan_limits` row of a feature the plan has (`feature not in plan`, `P0002`, otherwise); null for no limit. It shows in `private.monthly_limit` on the next call.
  - `admin_delete_plan(p_id, p_note)`: refuses the default plan (`default plan`) and a plan with `household_plans` rows (`plan in use`), both `23503`; otherwise deletes it, its `plan_features` and `plan_limits` cascading. Its audit row keeps the last state in `details.before`.
  - Unknown feature keys and negative limits are refused by the tables' `check` constraints (`23514`).
  - The Table Editor still works on `plans`, `plan_features` and `plan_limits`, unaudited.
- **Per household.** A household's plan is its `household_plans` row and nothing else: there's no "unassigned, so the default". Every household has a row: creating one (`create_household`, `private.create_personal_household`, 4.7) writes it with the plan that is the default at that moment, and the migration that moved plans to households gave every existing household one (an owner's per-user plan, the plan with more features winning when two owners' plans met, ties by plan id; else the default; every household of jgpasch@gmail.com on unlimited). **"Default" only means the plan new households start on**: changing it never moves an existing household. A household with no row (which shouldn't exist; with no default plan a new household gets none) has no features. Every member gets the household's features while acting in it (importing into it, making its recipe's AI photo), so a user in two households can have a feature in one and not the other. The plan stays with the household when members join or leave (4.7) and goes when it's deleted.
  - **Founding households.** Every household made before paid plans launch is on founding through its own row, so making a paid plan the default later moves none of them (above).
  - **One household per new user.** `create_household` refuses a caller who is already a member of any household (`already in a household`, `P0001`, 4.7), so a user can't make extra households to multiply allowances. The app only ever creates one when the user has none (`ensureStarterData` at sign-up, **Start my own list**), and the server makes one when a user leaves their only household. Leaving your only household (deleting it, as its last member) and getting a fresh one, with a fresh allowance and first month, is allowed: it costs every list and recipe, so it isn't worth farming.
- **Monthly limits.** A plan sets how much of each feature a household may use per calendar month (UTC, resetting on the 1st), shared by its members, in `plan_limits` (5), one row per plan and feature. Seeded: **founding** `recipe_import` 50 (link and photo imports together), `recipe_photo_ai` 20; **unlimited** a row per feature with a null limit; **free** no rows. A null `monthly_limit`, or no row for a feature the plan has, means no monthly limit, so deleting a row lifts it. Changing a row takes effect on the next call. Categorize, step matching and Suggest tags have no monthly limit on any plan, only safety limits (below).
  - **First month.** A household younger than 30 days (`households.created_at`) gets twice its plan's limits, so importing a collection in its first days doesn't hit the wall. It goes by the household's age only (the account's age plays no part, so there's no per-user first month). The 30 days is a named constant in `private.first_month_ends_at(household)` (shown in the portal), and the 2× one in `private.monthly_limit(feature, household)`, the only place a monthly limit is resolved.
  - **Checking.** `check_recipe_import_limit` and `check_recipe_photo_limit` (both counting the household's `recipe_import`) and `reserve_recipe_photo_ai` (`recipe_photo_ai`, for the recipe's household) check in order (the two import checks require the household, `household required` `22023` for null, before anything else is read or written): the per-minute safety limit (`user_limit`), the daily one (`daily_limit`), then the month: the household's usage for the current month (`private.monthly_usage(household, feature)`, from `private.household_ai_usage`, 5) against `private.monthly_limit(feature, household)`, under a per-household advisory lock. All before anything is written, so a refused request uses no budget; a request that passes is counted on the user's own usage table (for the safety limits) and on the household's. A null limit skips that check, but usage is still counted so the numbers stay visible. Past the monthly limit it answers `monthly_limit`, which the functions answer **429 `limit_reached`** with `resets_at` (the first instant of next month, UTC, ISO 8601). The functions don't know any limit number.
  - **Unlimited** means no monthly limits. The safety limits and one AI photo per recipe (6.11) still apply.
  - **In the app**, `limit_reached` is never "Try again in a minute": link and photo import alike say "You've used this month's recipe imports. They reset on Nov 1." (they share the allowance) and AI photos "…this month's AI photos…", the date being `resets_at` as a short date (read in UTC, so it's always the 1st). Shares fail with that reason rather than wait, and the user taps Try again after the reset (4.10).
- **Safety limits.** Per user, the same on every plan, in `public.ai_safety_limits` (5): one row per key with `per_minute` and `per_day` (null is no limit). They stop a client stuck in a loop (a minute) or a scripted account (a day), not a person. `private.safety_limit(key)` reads a row, and every `check_*_limit` function and `reserve_recipe_photo_ai` reads its numbers from there on each call, so a change applies at once. A day is the user's UTC day on the per-user usage tables (both import tables for `recipe_import`). Seeded:

  | key | per minute | per day | checked by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `categorize` | 60 requests | 500 LLM-bound names | `check_categorize_limit` (6.4) |
  | `step_match` | 30 | 100 | `check_step_match_limit` (6.8) |
  | `recipe_tags` | 20 | 50 | `check_recipe_tags_limit` (6.7) |
  | `recipe_import_link` | 10 | none | `check_recipe_import_limit` (6.6) |
  | `recipe_import_photo` | 5 | none | `check_recipe_photo_limit` (6.10) |
  | `recipe_import` | none | 100 (link and photo together) | both import checks |
  | `recipe_photo_ai` | none | 40 | `reserve_recipe_photo_ai` (6.11) |
  | `share_ingest` | 30 (per device credential) | none | `share-ingest` (6.12), not AI: it only bounds what a leaked credential can queue, which import metering then limits |

  Founding can't reach the `recipe_import` and `recipe_photo_ai` rows (its first month allows 100 imports and 40 photos in the whole month); they bound plans with no monthly limit. A per-minute refusal is `user_limit`, answered **429 `rate_limited`** as before; a daily one is `daily_limit` (after `user_limit`, before the monthly check; nothing counted), answered **429 `daily_limit`** with `{ error, message, resets_at }`, `resets_at` the next UTC midnight (ISO 8601). In the app: where a person started the request (the import sheet and its shares, photo import, Suggest tags, AI photos), `daily_limit` says "You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow." ("Llegaste al límite de hoy. Inténtalo mañana."); `rate_limited` says "Try again in a minute" as before. Background work stays silent: categorize leaves the names `pending` for the catch-up on either, and step matching pauses for an hour on `rate_limited` and stops for the session on `daily_limit`. Page photos (6.9) aren't AI and keep their own hard-coded limits. Admins edit the table in the portal: `admin_list_safety_limits()` (read-only) and `admin_set_safety_limit(p_key, p_per_minute, p_per_day, p_note)` (`require_admin()` first; `note required` as for plans; `unknown key` `P0002`; audited `set_safety_limit` with `details` `{ before, after }`), on a **Safety limits** card on the Plans page listing each key with its two numbers (blank for none), their units ("requests a minute", "names a day" for categorize's daily, and so on) and an edit form with the required note. Activity names the action.
- **Changing a household's plan** is done in the portal, through `admin_set_household_plan(p_household_id, p_plan_id, p_note)`: it upserts the household's `household_plans` row. A plan is required (`plan required`, `22023`, for null): a household's plan can be changed but never cleared, and the portal's form has no "default" choice. It raises `household not found` or `plan not found` (`P0002`), or `note required` (`22023`, a null or blank note), changing nothing, and otherwise writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`action` `set_household_plan`, `target_household_id`, `target_plan_id`, `details` `{ from, to }` with plan ids, the note, `performed_by` the admin's id), even when the plan doesn't change, and returns `details`. The portal finds households with `admin_list_households(p_search, p_limit, p_offset)` (the household's name or a member's email or first, last or full name, case-insensitively, or the household id exactly when the search is a uuid; newest first; at most 200 a page; each row with its name, member count, plan and `total_count`) and reads one with `admin_household_detail(p_household_id)` (name, created, the first month, members with role and email, the plan, this month's `in_plan`, `used`, `limit` and `resets_at` for `recipe_import` and `recipe_photo_ai`, and the `set_household_plan` history, newest first, with the admin's email). Users stay findable as before (`admin_list_users`, which has no plan columns, and `admin_user_detail`), each user's households listed with their plan id and name and linking to the household; a user has no plan of their own. The Table Editor still works on `household_plans` (a `before update` trigger sets `assigned_at`), unaudited; don't delete a row there, since a household with none has no features. The owner's households (jgpasch@gmail.com) are on unlimited on hosted, put there by the migration that moved plans to households.
- **AI usage** in the portal comes from `admin_ai_usage()`: per AI feature (the two plan features in the fixed order, then categorize, step matching and tags), this calendar month's `used` across everyone and `users` (how many used any), a `daily` series of the last 40 UTC days (oldest first, zeros included) and, for `recipe_import` and `recipe_photo_ai`, `top_households` (the 10 heaviest households this month, with name, plan id, `used` and their limit); categorize, step matching and tags, which have no monthly limit, get only those totals and `top_users` (with email, no limit); all in the units the limits use (`private.daily_usage`, per user, keyed `recipe_import` (link and photo imports together), `recipe_photo_ai`, `categorize`, `step_match` and `recipe_tags`, in that order, `private.ai_usage_keys()`; `top_households` reads `private.household_ai_usage`, as `private.monthly_usage` does); and `calls`, the last 30 days of `ai_calls` per `task` as stored (not mapped to features), with `total`, a count per `status` (every status, `refused` included, zeros too), `fallbacks` (not counting image or moderation rows, whose `fallback_of` links their description, 6.4) and the four token sums, and the same per `model`. Counts and sums only, never a call's instructions, prompt or output. Read-only, so no audit row.
- **Flagged AI calls.** One SQL function decides what's flagged, `private.ai_call_flag(public.ai_calls)` (immutable, text or null), so the portal, its partial index (`ai_calls_flagged_idx`) and retention agree: `not_food` for a `recipePhotoDescription` row whose output's `isFood` is false (an `ok` call, so it's flagged on purpose, 6.11), `refused` for a `refused` row (the image call's content-policy refusal, or a flagged moderation check), `content_filter` for any task's row with finish reason `content-filter` (a safety block, 6.4), else null. A flagged call, and the calls linked to it by `fallback_of` either way, are kept 180 days instead of 30 (5).
- **Reading AI calls.** Admins can read any call's instructions, prompt and response, and who made it, and see the AI photos made, in the portal. `admin_list_ai_calls(p_kind default 'failed', p_task, p_status, p_flag, p_user_id, p_limit default 50, p_offset default 0)` (jsonb): `{ calls, total_count }`, newest first, `p_kind` `failed` (status isn't `ok`), `flagged` (`private.ai_call_flag` isn't null) or `photos` (`ok` `recipePhotoImage` rows, since a harmful photo the image model allowed is never flagged), anything else `invalid kind` (`22023`); each call with `id`, `created_at`, `task`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `status`, `flag`, `error`, `http_status`, `finish_reason`, `duration_ms`, `fallback_of`, `recovered` (an attempt whose `fallback_of` is this one ended `ok`; an image or moderation row isn't a fallback, so it doesn't count), `user_id` and `user_email` (while the user exists), `user_ai_photos_blocked` (the user's AI photos are blocked now, below), `household_id`, `recipe_id` and `photo_path` (`output->>'path'`, image rows only); the four filters combine, `p_limit` is capped at 200 and `total_count` is counted before paging. `admin_ai_call_flag_count(p_days default 7)` is the sidebar's badge, flagged calls in the last `p_days` days made after the caller's `private.admins.ai_flags_seen_at`. Both are read-only, so no audit row. Opening the Flagged tab clears the badge: `admin_mark_ai_flags_seen()` sets the caller's `ai_flags_seen_at` to now and returns whether the badge had anything to clear (not audited). `admin_ai_call(p_id)` returns one call, any status, with every column, `flag`, `user_email`, `photo_path`, `fallback` (the attempt that followed it, newest if several: a fallback, or a description's image call, or its moderation check when that stopped it; the portal labels them Fallback, Image call and Moderation) and `primary` (the one it followed), each `{ id, task, model, status, error }` or null; an unknown id raises `not found` (`P0002`). Opening a call is audited: it writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`view_ai_call`, the call's user, `details` `{ ai_call_id, task, flag }`, no note, `performed_by` the admin's id). `admin_user_detail` has `ai_flags`, the user's flagged calls in the last 30 days, a count per flag (`{}` when none). Admins can read every `recipe-photos` object (`recipe_photos_select_admin`, 5), so the portal can sign URLs for the AI photos; those reads aren't audited.
- **Activity** in the portal comes from `admin_list_actions(p_action default null, p_limit default 50, p_offset default 0)` (jsonb): `{ actions, total_count }`, every `admin.admin_actions` row (the portal's plan and safety-limit changes, `view_ai_call` reads and `unblock_ai_photos`, the trigger's `block_ai_photos`, the dashboard snippets' `delete_user`, the app's `delete_account`) newest first, or only `p_action`'s, `p_limit` capped at 200 and `total_count` counted before paging. Each row has its columns plus `target_user_email` (while that user exists) and `performed_by_email` (as in `admin_user_detail`, so null for `postgres`). Read-only, so no audit row. The page names the two block actions **Blocked AI photos** (details "3 refusals") and **Unblocked AI photos** (details "Blocked Oct 21, 2026 after 3 refusals", and the note), and shows the block's admin, a null `performed_by`, as **Automatic**; both are in its action filter.
- **User activity.** An admin opening a user in the portal sees everything that user did, newest first, and can filter it: every change of theirs that reached the server, their AI requests and what admins did to their account. Only server writes are logged: nothing the app does without writing (opening a screen, searching, viewing a recipe), and an offline edit shows when it syncs. Today nothing else records who changed a row after it was made, so this is the only record.
  - **Logging is by table triggers.** Every write reaches a table whichever path it took (`apply_mutations`, the household functions, direct writes to `user_settings`, `user_household_category_order` and `notification_settings`, the push token functions), so one shared `after insert or update or delete … for each row` trigger, `private.log_user_activity()`, can't be missed by a path added later. It calls the table's own function (`private.activity_list_items(old, new)` and so on; `security definer`, `search_path = ''`, execute revoked from every client role), which returns the area, action, entity id and `data` per the catalog below, or nothing, and inserts one `private.user_activity` row (5) for the actor, `coalesce(auth.uid(), nullif(current_setting('app.actor_uid', true), '')::uuid)`: the signed-in caller, or the user a server writer acts for (`commit_share_import` sets the transaction-local `app.actor_uid` to the sharer, 6.12). It's on `lists`, `list_items`, `list_item_contributions`, `recipes`, `recipe_ingredients`, `meal_plan_entries`, `categories` (rows with a `household_id`), `household_category_overrides`, `household_category_order`, `household_staples`, `recipe_favorites`, `user_settings`, `user_household_category_order`, `notification_settings`, `households`, `household_members`, `household_invites` and `push_tokens`; not on `ingredient_usage` / `ingredient_unit_usage` (counters). It logs nothing when there's no actor (pg_cron, Edge Functions on the service role that set no `app.actor_uid`, the dashboard), nor for an update whose only changed columns are `updated_at`, `field_clock` or `created_at`. Names in `data` are read from the row or its parent by primary key (a list item's list, a contribution's item and list, an ingredient's recipe, a plan entry's recipe). Like the `household_events` triggers (8.5), logging never fails the write: an error raises a warning.
  - **One gesture, several rows.** The trigger sees rows, not what the user meant, so one gesture that writes many rows (a recipe with 20 ingredients) logs many entries sharing a `txid`, which the portal folds. The household functions instead set a transaction-local `app.activity_action` (`set_config('app.activity_action', …, true)`) with their action and `data`; while it's set, the trigger logs that one entry on the first write and nothing for the rest. `settings.aisle_order` is logged once per transaction the same way, however many aisles moved.
  - **The catalog.** Names are snapshots, as they were then, so a later rename doesn't change old entries; every `data` also has the household's name as `household` when there is one. Never free text the user wrote (notes, recipe text, steps, a plan note) and never an invited person's email.

    | area | action | logged when | `data` |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `lists` | `list.created`, `list.archived`, `list.unarchived`, `list.deleted`, `list.restored` | a list is inserted, archived or unarchived, soft-deleted or restored | `{ list }` (the name) |
    | `lists` | `list.renamed` | `name` changed | `{ list, from }` |
    | `lists` | `list.edited` | emoji or colour changed | `{ list, fields }` |
    | `lists` | `list.reordered` | only `position` changed | `{ list }` |
    | `lists` | `item.added` | a contribution is inserted | `{ list, list_id, item, item_id, source, quantity, unit }`, plus `recipe, recipe_id` when `source` is `recipe` |
    | `lists` | `item.add_undone` | a contribution's `deleted_at` is set | `{ list, item }` |
    | `lists` | `item.checked`, `item.unchecked`, `item.deleted`, `item.restored` | `checked_at` set or cleared, `deleted_at` set or cleared | `{ list, item }` |
    | `lists` | `item.moved` | `list_id` changed | `{ item, from, to }` (list names) |
    | `lists` | `item.renamed` | `display_name` changed | `{ item, from }` |
    | `lists` | `item.edited` | the note, or a contribution's quantity | `{ list, item, fields }` |
    | `lists` | `item.photo_added`, `item.photo_removed` | `photo_path` set or cleared | `{ list, item }` |
    | `lists` | `item.merged` | `merged_into` set (a Combine, 4.4, 7.3) | `{ list, item, into }` |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.created` | a recipe is inserted | `{ recipe, source }`, `source` the `source_url`'s host, `photo` or `manual` |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.imported_from_share` | `commit_share_import` (6.12), one entry for the whole commit (`app.activity_action`, above) | `{ recipe, recipe_id, list, items_added, items_combined }` (`list` null for recipe-only) |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.renamed` | `name` changed | `{ recipe, from }` |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.edited` | notes, servings, times, steps or another field changed | `{ recipe, fields }` (field names only) |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.tagged` | `tags` changed | `{ recipe }` |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.favorited`, `recipe.unfavorited` | the user's `recipe_favorites` row inserted or restored, or its `deleted_at` set | `{ recipe }` |
    | `recipes` | `recipe.photo_added`, `recipe.photo_removed`, `recipe.deleted`, `recipe.restored` | `photo_path` set or cleared, `deleted_at` set or cleared | `{ recipe }` |
    | `recipes` | `ingredient.added`, `ingredient.removed`, `ingredient.edited`, `ingredient.reordered` | a recipe ingredient inserted, deleted, edited, or only its position changed | `{ recipe, recipe_id, ingredient, fields }` |
    | `plan` | `plan.added`, `plan.removed`, `plan.restored` | a meal plan entry inserted, soft-deleted or restored | `{ date, meal, kind, recipe, recipe_id }`; `{ date, meal, kind }` for leftovers, eating out and notes (a note's text isn't kept) |
    | `plan` | `plan.moved` | `plan_date` or `meal` changed | `{ from: { date, meal }, to: { date, meal }, recipe }` |
    | `plan` | `plan.reordered` | only `position` changed | `{ date, meal, recipe }` |
    | `aisles` | `aisle.created`, `aisle.deleted` | a custom aisle inserted or deleted | `{ aisle }` |
    | `aisles` | `aisle.renamed` | a custom aisle's `name` changed | `{ aisle, from }` |
    | `aisles` | `aisle.assigned` | a `household_category_overrides` row written | `{ ingredient, aisle }`, `aisle` null for Reset to default |
    | `aisles` | `aisle_order.saved` | a `household_category_order` row written | `{}` |
    | `aisles` | `staple.added`, `staple.removed` | a staple inserted or restored, or its `deleted_at` set | `{ ingredient }` |
    | `household` | `household.created`, `household.deleted`, `household.left` | `create_household`, `delete_household`, `leave_household` | `{ household }` |
    | `household` | `household.renamed` | `rename_household` | `{ household, from }` |
    | `household` | `household.combine_languages` | `set_combine_languages` | `{ on }` |
    | `household` | `invite.created` | `create_invite` | `{ invite_id }`, never the invited email (someone who never signs up has no account to delete it with) |
    | `household` | `invite.revoked`, `invite.accepted`, `invite.declined` | `revoke_invite`, `accept_invite` / `accept_invite_by_id`, `decline_invite` | `{ invite_id }` |
    | `household` | `member.removed`, `member.role_changed` | `remove_member`, `set_member_role` | `{ member, member_id, role }` (`member` the full name) |
    | `settings` | `settings.default_list` | `user_settings.default_list_id` changed | `{ list }` |
    | `settings` | `settings.aisle_order` | `user_household_category_order` written or deleted | `{ household, removed }` (`removed` true for Use household order) |
    | `settings` | `settings.notifications` | `notification_settings` changed | `{ fields }` |
    | `devices` | `device.registered`, `device.unregistered` | `register_push_token`, `unregister_push_token` | `{ platform }` |

    The household rows are one entry per function call, from the function (`app.activity_action`, above); the rows it writes log nothing more. A `list_items` insert logs nothing when its first contribution comes in the same transaction (the contribution's `item.added` covers it); a path that inserts an item without one logs `item.added` from the item.
  - **Retention and deletion.** A daily pg_cron job, `user-activity-retention`, deletes entries older than 90 days. `delete_account` and `admin.delete_user` delete the user's entries last (so the account's own deletes leave none) and scrub the user from other users' entries: in `member.removed` and `member.role_changed` entries whose `data.member_id` is the user, `member` and `member_id` are removed from `data` (4.8).
  - **Reading it**: `admin_user_activity(p_user_id, p_areas text[] default null, p_actions text[] default null, p_household_id default null, p_from timestamptz default null, p_to timestamptz default null, p_before timestamptz default null, p_limit default 100)` (jsonb, `security definer`, `require_admin()` first). It returns `{ entries, next_before, counts }`, newest first, at most 200 entries a page, keyset paged on `p_before` (`next_before` null at the end), the filters combining. It merges three sources, each entry `{ at, source, area, action, entity_id, household_id, household, txid, data }`: `private.user_activity` (`source` `app`); the user's `ai_calls` (`source` `ai`, `area` `ai`, `action` `ai.<task>`, `data` `{ id, status, flag, recipe_id }`, never the prompt or output); and the `admin.admin_actions` rows with the user as `target_user_id` (`source` `admin`, `area` `admin`, `action` `admin.<action>`, `data` `{ details, note, performed_by_email }`). `p_areas` takes `ai` and `admin` too. An `invite.created` entry gets `invited_email` from `household_invites` while that invite exists (null once it's gone), so the email is never copied into the log. `counts` is per area over the same user, households and dates but not `p_areas` or `p_actions`, for the filter chips. An unknown user returns no entries, not an error (a deleted user's admin rows are still listed). **Opening a user's activity is audited**, like opening an AI call: a call with no `p_before` (a first page, whatever the filters) writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`view_user_activity`, the user as `target_user_id`, `details` the filters, no note, `performed_by` the admin's id); later pages write nothing. `admin_user_detail` has `last_active_at`, the newest `app` or `ai` entry (null for none).
  - **In the portal**, the user page has an **Activity** card after Households: area chips with counts (All, Lists, Recipes, Plan, Aisles, Household, Settings, Devices, AI, Admin), an action select for the chosen areas, a household select and a date range, all kept in the URL's search params so a filtered view can be linked. Entries are grouped by day (Today, Yesterday, then the date), each with its time, an area icon and a sentence built from the action and `data` (`admin/lib/user-activity.ts`: "Checked *Cucumber* on *Groceries*", "Invited *a@b.com* to *Home*", "Invited someone" once the invite is gone, "Removed a member" for a scrubbed entry), the household as a muted suffix; entries with the same action in one `txid` fold into one expandable line with a count ("Added 12 ingredients to *Lasagna*"). An unknown action still shows its name and `data`. **Load older** fetches the next page; the header shows "Last active"; with nothing, "Nothing in the last 90 days". Activity names `view_user_activity` "Viewed a user's activity".
- **AI photo blocks** (6.11). A user with 3 refused AI photo prompts in 30 days loses AI photos until an admin unblocks them (`private.ai_photo_blocks`, 5). `admin_user_detail` has `ai_photo_block` (null, or `{ blocked_at, refusals, last_call_id }`) and `ai_photo_refusals_30d` (`private.ai_photo_refusals` over the block window, so since the last unblock). `admin_list_users` has `ai_photos_blocked` per row and takes `p_blocked` (null everyone, true only blocked users, false the others), after the search, before paging. `admin_unblock_ai_photos(p_user_id, p_note)` raises `note required` (`22023`) or `not blocked` (`P0002`, also for an unknown user), changing nothing; otherwise it deletes the block and writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`unblock_ai_photos`, the user, the note, `performed_by` the admin's id, `details` `{ refusals, blocked_at }`, the block's), which it returns. **Unblocking starts the count over**: a refusal counts only after the user's latest `unblock_ai_photos` (or 30 days back, whichever is later), so the refusals that blocked them don't block them again on the next one; that's in `private.ai_photo_refusals`, the one place the rule is counted. In the portal, the user page has an **AI photos** card: blocked, "Blocked since <date> after <n> refused prompts" in `danger`, **See the refused prompts** (the AI calls page's Flagged tab filtered by the user and flag `refused`, which lists their moderation and image refusals alike) and **Unblock**, a dialog with a required note like Change plan's ("Unblocked AI photos for <email>" once saved); not blocked, "Not blocked", with "<n> refused prompts in the last 30 days (blocks at 3)" linking to the same list when n > 0. A blocked user has a `danger` **AI photos blocked** badge in the page's header, on their row in the Users list (whose filter, **All users** or **AI photos blocked**, is `?blocked=1`, kept with the search and paging) and on their `refused` rows on the AI calls page.
- **Device credentials** (6.12). A lost or stolen phone is handled in the portal only: there's no device list or "Sign out of other devices" in the app. `admin_revoke_device_credentials(p_user_id, p_note)` (`require_admin()` first; `note required` (`22023`) for a null or blank note) sets `revoked_at` and `revoked_reason` `admin` on every live credential of the user and writes one `admin.admin_actions` row (`revoke_device_credentials`, the user as `target_user_id`, the note, `performed_by` the admin's id, `details` `{ revoked }`, how many), which it returns, also when there were none. The user page has a **Revoke device credentials** button with a required note, like Unblock's dialog; a revoked device's background shares fall back to the app's submit with the user's session (4.10). Activity names it **Revoked device credentials**.
- **Resolution.** `has_feature(user, feature, household)` (for Edge Functions, `service_role` only) and `my_household_features()` (for the app) are the only places a plan is resolved. `has_feature` is false unless `user` is a member of `household`, then says whether the household's plan (its `household_plans` row; none, no features) has the feature. A null household is false. `my_household_features()` (`authenticated`, `security definer`) returns the caller's features per household, `{ households: { <household id>: [features, sorted in the "C" collation] } }`, for every household they're a member of; it's the app's only answer (there's no per-user one).
- **In the app.** Only `recipe_import` and `recipe_photo_ai` are gated; categorize, Suggest tags and step matching are always on, whatever the plan. A store in `src/features/plans/` holds the user's features per household, `{ households: Record<id, Feature[]> }`, `Feature` being `recipe_import` or `recipe_photo_ai`: `features.ts` (`useFeature(feature, householdId)` and `hasFeature(feature, householdId)`; `useAnyHouseholdFeature(feature)` for an entry point not yet tied to a household) and `load.ts`, which calls `my_household_features()` after sign-in, on each foreground and reconnect while signed in and online, and when the user's memberships change (after a join, leave or delete, or a new household), keeps the last answer in device settings (with the user's id; a cached answer in an older shape is ignored) so an offline launch has it, and clears both on sign-out. Until the first answer, and for a household the answer doesn't have yet (one just made or joined), it assumes the default plan (founding: both features), so nothing hides by mistake. A function's `not_entitled` answer drops that feature for that household at once (`lostFeature(feature, householdId)`) and reloads. **Recipe import**: the Recipes tab's two **Import from link** buttons, **Add recipe from link…** in a list's menu, and the Add recipe sheet's link handling and **From photo…** show when any of the user's households has `recipe_import`. In the import sheet, the **Save to** picker offers every household, and with one whose plan lacks `recipe_import` the button is disabled with "Recipe import isn't included in <household>'s plan." ("La importación de recetas no está incluida en el plan de <household>.") under it, for a link and a photo alike. An import answered `not_entitled` fails with "Recipe import isn't included in your plan." (4.10). Without `recipe_photo_ai` in the recipe's household the app hides **Generate with AI** in the Add photo sheet (4.5); a generation answered `not_entitled` fails with "Couldn't make a photo. Try again later." The plan isn't shown anywhere in the app.
- Edge Functions enforce entitlements; the app only hides what isn't enabled. Only `import-recipe`, `import-recipe-photo` and `share-import-worker` (all `recipe_import`, 6.6, 6.10, 6.12; the worker for the share's household and sharer, before reserving, so a share on a plan without it fails `not_entitled` uncharged) and `generate-recipe-photo` (`recipe_photo_ai`, 6.11) check a feature, through `requireFeature(admin, userId, feature, householdId)` (`supabase/functions/_shared/entitlements.ts`, which calls `has_feature` and throws `NotEntitledError`), answering **403** with the usual error body, `{ error: 'not_entitled', message }`, before any limit or model work and with nothing counted. `categorize-ingredients`, `suggest-recipe-tags` and `match-recipe-steps` check no feature: any signed-in user gets them, under their safety limits. An import's inline categorizing isn't checked again. **Requests name their household**: `import-recipe` and `import-recipe-photo` send `householdId`, the household the recipe is saved to (required: a request without one is `400 invalid_request`), which goes to `requireFeature`, so a caller who isn't a member of it gets 403 `not_entitled` before any work, like a plan without the feature; `generate-recipe-photo` looks up the live recipe's household (read-only, `404 not_found` when it's gone), checks the feature there, then reserves, so an unentitled call writes nothing. `suggest-recipe-tags` and `match-recipe-steps` still send the recipe's `householdId`, only so `ai_calls` records it (6.4): a household the caller isn't in is ignored (no `household_id` on the row). `categorize-ingredients` sends none.
- Local dev: `supabase/seed.sql` makes **unlimited** the default plan, since the dev user's households are made on the device after the seed runs, so manual testing against local Supabase has every feature. The `yarn ai:*` scripts (CLI and evals) run the handlers against in-memory stores with no database, and those stores grant every feature.

### 4.13 Push notifications (req 13)

Household members hear about each other's list activity when the app is closed: "Alex added milk, eggs and 4 more to Groceries" and "Alex is shopping at Groceries". iOS only for now (nothing rules out Android: the app keeps the Android channel and `push_tokens.platform`). Delivery is Expo Push (`expo-notifications`, which talks to APNs). The person who made the change never gets a household push about it; the recipe-import pushes (below) are the one kind that goes to the user themselves. Push needs a development build and a real device.

- **Events.** "Added" events come from `list_item_contributions` inserts (a Combine adds a contribution to an open item and inserts no item); "shopping" events from `list_items.checked_at` going from null to set. Triggers queue them on the pgmq queue `household_events` (8.5). A share imported on the server (4.10, 6.12) inserts its contributions with `added_by` the sharer when `commit_share_import` commits, so the household's "Alex added Tacos (8 items) to Groceries" goes out then, unchanged, and not to the sharer.
- **Recipe imports.** Two pushes go to the **sharer's own devices** only (every one of their push tokens, never other members'), sent at once rather than grouped, when one of their share imports finishes: "*Lemon pasta* is ready" (body "Added to *Groceries*", or "Saved to *Smith home*" for recipe-only, or "Saved *Lemon pasta*. Its list was gone, so nothing was added." with `list_gone`, 4.10; data `url` `/recipe/<id>`, opening the recipe), and "Couldn't import that recipe" with the reason in the body (4.10's plain words; data `url` `/recipes`, the Recipes tab, whose imports card shows the failed imports). They're queued by `commit_share_import` (done) and `release_share_import` (failed) and sent by `send-notifications` (8.5), respecting the user's `recipe_imports` setting and quiet hours like the other kinds (a push held by quiet hours isn't sent later; the imports card still shows the result).
- **Grouping.** A group is (household, actor, list, kind). It sends once its newest event is 60 s old or its oldest is 5 minutes old, whichever is first. At send time the rows are re-read, so an undone add or an unchecked item doesn't count, and an empty group is dropped. **Added** counts the live contributions and names up to two items by `display_name`, then "and N more"; a group that is all one recipe's add reads "Alex added Tacos (8 items) to Groceries". **Shopping** sends at most once per (actor, list) in a 2-hour window. Events whose row time is more than 15 minutes older than the worker's clock are dropped (a device that synced its checks hours later). The actor's name follows the app's rule (4.7): their `profiles.first_name`, plus their last name's initial (uppercased) and "." ("Alex R.") only when another current member of the event's household has the same first name (case-insensitive) and the actor has a last name.
- **Recipients** (household pushes). Current members of the household other than the actor, with at least one push token, that kind turned on and not in their quiet hours in their own time zone (`private.push_recipients`, 5). Tapping a push opens `/list/<id>`.
- **Tapping a push** (`src/features/push/receiving.ts`). Once signed in, the root layout acts on `useLastNotificationResponse()`, so a cold start and a tap while running both open the list, each response id once (kept in memory). Only `data.url` of the form `/list/<uuid>`, `/recipe/<uuid>` or `/recipes` is followed; anything else is ignored. A list or recipe not on the device yet gets a pull first; if a list is still missing, the Lists tab opens with the toast "That list isn't available", and a missing recipe opens the Recipes tab.
- **While the app is open.** A push shows no OS banner. An `items_added` or `shopping` push becomes an in-app notification (4.11) of kind `itemsAdded` (`plus`, green) or `shopping` (`cart`, blue) with the push's title and body; tapping it opens the list. A recipe-import push becomes a `recipeImport` notification (the recipe tile for ready, the rose warning tile for failed, 4.10) that opens its url. Nothing is posted while that list's screen (or one of its sheets) is showing, since the realtime update shows the change. Opening a list's screen clears its delivered pushes from Notification Center.
- **Registration and settings.** The app registers its Expo token after sign-in and on foreground once the user has allowed notifications, and unregisters it on sign-out. Per-user toggles per kind and quiet hours live in `notification_settings` (5), changed online.
- **Settings > Notifications** (`src/app/(tabs)/settings/notifications.tsx`, the row after Siri adds to; store in `src/features/push/settings.ts`). While the permission was never asked, a card explains what you'd get ("Know when someone in your household adds to a list or starts shopping") with **Turn on notifications**, the only place besides the one-time prompt that asks. Denied, it says notifications are off for Perfect Bite with **Open Settings**. Granted, the card is gone and the settings show: switches "Items added to lists" (`items_added`), "Someone starts shopping" (`shopping`) and **Recipe imports** (`recipe_imports`, on by default, detail "When a recipe you shared is ready, or couldn't be imported"; it turns both recipe-import pushes off), and **Quiet hours**, a switch (22:00–07:00 when first turned on) with From and To compact native time pickers (`@expo/ui` `DatePicker`); a start equal to the end isn't saved and says why. Each change upserts the user's row with the device's time zone (`Intl…resolvedOptions().timeZone`), shown under the pickers; a failed save puts the control back and says so. The time zone is also sent after sign-in and on each foreground when it differs from the one last saved on the device (`push.timeZone`), so quiet hours follow the phone when travelling. Offline, the controls are disabled with "Needs a connection".
- **Asking once.** The first time the user shares a household (they accept an invite, or a pull or Realtime shows someone joined a household the device knew as just theirs), a sheet offers **Turn on** or **Not now**, only while the permission was never asked. It's marked shown (the device setting `push.asked`) as it appears, so it never comes back whatever the answer. Never at launch or on sign-in: those fill the member cache from empty, which doesn't count.

### 4.14 Recipe tags and filtering

Recipes are tagged so the Recipes tab stays usable at 50+ recipes (req 15).

- **The tags.** A fixed list, never free-form, in `src/shared/recipe-tags.ts`, used by the app, the `import-recipe` Edge Function and the Zod schemas. Each value has a slug (stored) and a label (shown). The facets in display order, with how many a recipe may have:
  - Saved, up to 1: want-to-try ("Want to try", es *Por probar*), shared by the household. Favorites aren't a tag any more (Favorites, below); a `favorite` slug stored before that is a value the app doesn't know (kept on save, never shown), so those earlier, household-wide favorites were dropped on purpose. A person's mark, never the AI's: `AI_TAG_FACETS` is every facet but Saved, and the `import-recipe`/`extract-recipe` and `suggest-recipe-tags` prompts, the model's answer schema and their validation (`ExtractedRecipeTagsSchema`, `AiRecipeTagsSchema`) leave it out, so it's neither asked for nor returned; a `saved` key from the model is dropped.
  - Meal, up to 2: breakfast, lunch, dinner, side, dessert, snack, drink.
  - Cuisine, up to 2: american, mexican, latin-american, caribbean, italian, french, spanish, greek, mediterranean, middle-eastern, indian, chinese, japanese, korean, thai, vietnamese, african.
  - Protein, up to 3: chicken, turkey, beef, pork, lamb, fish, shellfish, eggs, tofu-tempeh ("Tofu & tempeh"), beans-lentils ("Beans & lentils").
  - Diet, up to 2: vegetarian, vegan. No gluten-free, dairy-free or nut-free: a wrong guess could hurt someone with an allergy.
  - Method, up to 2: pressure-cooker (label "Instant Pot"), slow-cooker, sheet-pan, one-pot, grill, air-fryer, no-cook.
- **Favorites** (per person). Each member keeps their own favorites and sees the other members'. A favorite is a `recipe_favorites` row (5) for the recipe and the user: household data written through `apply_mutations`, so it syncs, works offline and goes with the household like staples; unfavoriting sets `deleted_at` and favoriting again clears it. Only the user's own row can be written (the server checks `user_id` is the caller and `household_id` is the recipe's). The app counts only rows whose user is a current member of the recipe's household (the member cache, 4.7), so someone who leaves, is removed or deletes their account stops showing at once; `private.remove_member_from_household` also deletes the leaver's rows in that household. **Mine**: the user has a live row; **others'**: another current member has one. Set from the card's long-press menu (4.5) or the Tags sheet's **Favorite** pill (below), both through `setMyFavorite(recipeId, on)` (`src/features/recipes/favorites.ts`). Copy to household doesn't copy favorites. A deleted recipe's favorites are kept, so they come back if it's restored.
- **Time** isn't a tag. `timeBucket(total_minutes)` gives Under 30 min (under 30), Under 1 hour (30 to 60) or Over 1 hour (over 60); a recipe with no time has no bucket.
- **Regions.** Each cuisine maps to a region (american, latin, european, middle-eastern, asian, african) in the same file. Group by cuisine (4.5) draws a section per region, in that order, then "No cuisine"; a recipe with two cuisines in one region shows there once, and one in two regions shows in both.
- **Values the app doesn't know.** `RecipeTagsSchema` keeps the six facets (a missing one becomes empty, so a value stored before Saved existed reads as not saved), drops any other key and keeps every slug. `knownTags()` drops slugs the app doesn't know, for display only; saves write the stored value. So a value added later doesn't break older apps, and they don't erase it.
- **Data** (5): `tags` (null = never tagged), `tags_edited` (set by the first edit by a person; hides "Tagged when you imported it"), `total_minutes` (1 to 2880) and `added_by` (set by the server to whoever inserted the recipe; recipes from before this have none). The first three are field-level last-writer-wins like the other recipe fields, the whole `tags` value as one field (7.1, 7.3).
- **Where tags come from.** An import saves the tags and time the extraction returned. An import that reuses an existing recipe (same canonical URL, 4.10) leaves it untouched, so the AI never overwrites tags a person edited. Recipes made by hand (New recipe, from list items) start untagged. Copy to household keeps tags and time. Existing recipes stay untagged until a member tags them. Favorite and Want to try are set from the Tags sheet or, in one step, from the Recipes tab card's long-press menu (4.5: Favorite / Unfavorite, Want to try / Tried it), which writes `tags` with `tags_edited: true` like Save, changing only that one Saved slug.
- **Suggesting tags** (on every plan, under the user's `recipe_tags` safety limits, 4.12): on a recipe that's already saved, a person can ask the AI to tag it (the same tagging as an import, 6.7). It only *suggests*: the suggestion fills the Tags sheet's pills, the person checks them and taps Save, so nothing is written without someone seeing it and the AI never overwrites tags behind anyone's back. Tags only, never the time (a hand-made recipe has no steps to work it out from). It runs on the recipe's name, notes and live ingredients as the device has them, since the recipe may not have synced yet; the server reads no recipe rows. A recipe with no ingredients can't ask.
- **On the recipe screen**: under the link, one line: a small `tag` glyph (`inkSecondary`, `iconSmall`), then the tag values without facet names, joined with " · ", in facet order then the time bucket ("Lunch · Dinner · American · Chicken · Under 1 hour"), a favorite shown first as its small heart rather than words (`heart.fill` in `primary` when it's the user's, else an outlined `heart` in `inkSecondary` when only others', 4.5), in `footnote` `inkSecondary` on one line truncated at the tail, then a chevron. The whole line is one button (at least 44 pt tall) that opens the Tags sheet; VoiceOver reads every tag in full ("Tags: Your favorite, Lunch, Dinner, …", or "Tags: Sam's favorite, …"; hint "Edits tags"). A favorite counts as a tag here, so a recipe with only a favorite shows its heart rather than **Add tags**. Without any (untagged, or an import that found none), the line reads **Add tags** in `primary`; a new recipe opens that way, with no extra step. **Add tags** opens the **Tags** sheet. While `tags_edited` is false and the recipe has tags other than Saved (or a time), "Tagged when you imported it" (`footnote` `inkSecondary`) shows in the Tags sheet under its title, not on the recipe screen. The **Tags** sheet: Cancel and Save in the toolbar, one group of toggle pills per facet, the Saved group being **Favorite** (the user's own) and **Want to try** (a facet at its maximum disables its other values and says "Pick up to 2"), then an optional, clearable **Total time** in hours and minutes (up to 48 hours). Save writes `tags` (the stored value, so slugs this app doesn't know are kept), `total_minutes` and `tags_edited: true` in one update, and the user's favorite (`setMyFavorite`) in the same transaction when the Favorite pill changed; Cancel writes nothing.
- **Suggest tags in the app** (always shown; no plan hides it). The Tags sheet has a full-width secondary **Suggest tags** button (SF Symbol `sparkles`) above the first facet. Tapping it shows **Suggesting…** with a spinner, disabled, while the rest of the sheet stays usable; Cancel during the call drops the answer. A suggestion replaces the sheet's picks for the five facets the AI tags (keeping slugs this app doesn't know), never Saved (kept as picked) or the time, and a `footnote` (`inkSecondary`) under the button says "Suggested by AI. Check them, then Save."; when nothing clearly fit, "No tags clearly fit this recipe." and the picks stay. The suggested pills are ordinary selected pills, and Save works as above (`tags_edited: true`), so a suggested recipe never shows "Tagged when you imported it". An error shows its message (from `suggest-tags.ts`; `daily_limit` is "You've reached today's limit. Try again tomorrow.", 4.12) as that line in `danger`, never an alert, and the button reads **Suggest tags** again; after a suggestion it reads **Suggest again**. With no ingredients the button is disabled with "Add some ingredients first, so there's something to go on." under it. On the recipe screen, an untagged recipe with ingredients also gets " · **Suggest tags**" (`primary`, its own button) after **Add tags**; it opens the sheet with `suggest=1`, which starts the suggestion as the sheet opens. VoiceOver: the button is "Suggest tags" (hint "Picks tags for this recipe with AI. Nothing is saved until you tap Save."), "Suggesting tags" while it runs, then announces "Tags suggested", the no-fit line or the error; the pill is "Suggest tags" (hint "Opens the tags, with AI suggestions.").
- **Search** (Recipes tab): a field on the green header (4.5), "Search N recipes" (shown once there's a recipe), with a clear button while it has text. It matches recipe names and their live ingredients' display names, ignoring case and accents, on local SQLite, so it works offline. A recipe that matches only through an ingredient shows "Has *chicken thighs*" (`footnote`, the ingredient in 700) in place of its meal and time, and VoiceOver reads "has chicken thighs" in their place. Households group as before; one with no matches is hidden. No results: "No recipes match" (`callout`, `inkSecondary`). The imports card stays at the top. The search text isn't saved.
- **Filters** (Recipes tab, mockup C; `docs/design/recipes-header/` boards B and B3): a **Filters** button at the end of the search field on the green header, after the clear button and a hairline (a 44 pt button with a `line.3.horizontal.decrease` glyph in `onHeader` and no fill while no filters are on; with some on, a filled `onHeader` pill with the glyph and the count, "2", in `header`; VoiceOver "Filters", or "Filters, 2 active") opens the **Filters** sheet: a group of toggle pills per facet, then Time (the three buckets), showing only values some recipe in the user's households has (a facet with none is left out; a value already picked stays so it can be turned off). The Saved group's values are **Favorites** (a favorite of any current member of the recipe's household, the user included), **My favorites** (the user's own; shown only while some recipe has another member's favorite, since until then it matches the same as Favorites) and **Want to try**; the two favorites values are filter values, not tags. This panel is the only place **My favorites** shows (here and in the slot sheet's, 4.16); everywhere else (the shelf, Group by Saved, the slot sheet's chip) favorites means anyone's. Then **Clear** in the toolbar, and a full-width **Show N recipes** CTA that counts live, with the search. Picks apply at once, so closing the sheet any way keeps them; the CTA closes it. Matching: OR within a facet, AND across facets, AND with the search. There's no maximum when filtering. Active values show as chips at the top of the tab's sheet, under the header ("Dinner ✕", VoiceOver "Remove Dinner filter"); tapping one removes it. A plain **Clear** (`primary`, VoiceOver "Clear filters") ends the chips whenever there are any, and clears every filter. A recipe is untagged when it has no known tag and no time; a recipe with only Want to try or only a favorite isn't untagged. The Saved group comes first, like any facet ("Favorites ✕" or "My favorites ✕" as a chip). While filters are on, the end of the results says "N recipes without tags aren't shown" (`footnote`, counting the ones the search found); tapping it replaces the filters with only untagged recipes, shown by an "Untagged ✕" chip (picking a value in the sheet leaves Untagged). No results with filters on: "No recipes match" and a **Clear filters** button. Filters are kept in memory for the app session: they survive tab switches, not a relaunch.

### 4.15 Languages (Mexican Spanish)

The app speaks English and Mexican Spanish (`es-MX`). Building it is planned in `future/i18n-es-mx-tasks.md`. Spanish stays switched off until the UI, the add field and the Spanish catalog all work (the last step below); until then it shows only in development builds.

- **Which language.** By default the app follows the phone: the first of the phone's preferred languages (`expo-localization` `getLocales()`) that the app supports, else English. Any Spanish (`es-MX`, `es-US`, `es-ES`) gets Mexican Spanish. Declaring the languages (`expo-localization` plugin `supportedLocales.ios`, added when Spanish is switched on; not declared yet) makes iOS show **Settings > The Perfect Bite > Language**, which changes the phone's answer for this app and keeps the app, Siri, the share extension and the system's permission prompts in one language. A development build can force a language with `EXPO_PUBLIC_FORCE_LANGUAGE` (`en` or `es`), even before Spanish is switched on.
- **Language picker.** Settings > General > **Language** (its value the app's language, named in itself) opens a picker: **Same as phone** (the default, with the language that gives as its detail), **English**, **Español**, the current one ticked, and the footnote "Only this device changes. Siri and system messages stay in the phone's language." A pick switches the app's strings at once, with no restart. It's a device setting (`app.language`, not synced; Same as phone clears it) and wins over the phone and over `EXPO_PUBLIC_FORCE_LANGUAGE`; it's read before the first render, and a first launch (no settings yet) follows the phone. Languages are named in themselves whatever the app's language (`LANGUAGE_NAMES`). The picker offers Español before Spanish is switched on, so it's how the unreviewed Spanish is tried on a device.
- **Tone.** Mexican Spanish, addressing the user as *tú*, in the style of Mexican consumer apps. Units and food words are Mexican (*jitomate*, *aguacate*, *frijol*, *crema*). A native speaker reviews every string before Spanish is switched on.
- **Strings.** Every string the app shows or speaks (labels, buttons, toasts, empty states, VoiceOver labels and hints, Share as text) comes from `src/i18n/`: `en.ts` is the source, and `es.ts` must have exactly the same keys (typecheck fails otherwise). Plurals use the language's plural rules ("1 artículo", "3 artículos"); dates, times and numbers use `Intl` with the app's language. This spec quotes the English; the Spanish is its translation, not a separate design. Tests run in English.
- **What isn't translated.** Anything a person typed or imported stays as written: item and ingredient names, recipe names, steps and notes, custom aisles, household and list names. A recipe imported from a Spanish page stays in Spanish (6.6).
- **Fixed vocabularies are translated by slug.** System aisles (6.2), recipe tags (4.14) and units are stored as slugs or unit keys, so they're shown in the app's language with no data change. "New category…" treats a name matching a system aisle in either language as that aisle (`Lácteos` picks Dairy & Eggs). Units: `cup` *taza*, `tbsp` *cda*, `tsp` *cdta*, `can` *lata*, `jar` *frasco*, `bag` *bolsa*, `box` *caja*, `bunch` *manojo*, `clove` *diente*, `pinch` *pizca*, `dozen` *docena*, each with its plural; metric and US abbreviations (`g`, `kg`, `ml`, `l`, `oz`, `lb`, `fl oz`, `pt`, `qt`, `gal`) stay as they are.
- **Typing in Spanish** (4.2). One normalizer serves both languages, so phones set to different languages in one household always compute the same key for the same text:
