  - **Accents are folded** for the key: `limón` and `limon`, `jalapeño` and `jalapeno` are one ingredient. The display name keeps what was typed. Existing keys with accents are merged into their folded keys once, by a migration, and each device merges its own copy once at start (`foldAccentKeys`): the catalog keeps the categorized row (seed first), usage counts are summed, the newest household override or staple wins, and two open items that fold to one Combine into the older.
  - **Spanish plurals.** `-s` plurals already work (`aguacates`, `tortillas`, `chiles`). Plurals in `-es` after a consonant (`limones`, `frijoles`, `nueces`, `panes`) can't be told from English by rule (`scones`, `slices`), so they come from a list built from the Spanish catalog's names (`src/shared/spanish-plurals.ts`, generated by `yarn seed:es-plurals`); a test fails if the list would give an English seed name, or its plural, another singular than the English rules do.
  - **Amounts.** The quantity parser (4.2) reads Spanish numbers (`un`, `una`, `uno` to `veinte`, `medio`/`media` = ½), unit words (`taza(s)`, `cucharada(s)`/`cda`, `cucharadita(s)`/`cdta`, `gramo(s)`/`gr`, `kilo(s)`/`kilogramo(s)`, `litro(s)`, `mililitro(s)`, `libra(s)`, `onza(s)`, `galón`/`galones`, `lata(s)`, `frasco(s)`, `bolsa(s)`, `caja(s)`, `manojo(s)`, `diente(s)`, `pizca(s)`, `docena(s)`) and `pieza(s)`/`pza(s)` as a plain count, and drops a `de` right after the unit: `2 tazas de harina` is 2 cup `harina`, `media docena de huevos` ½ dozen `huevos`, `3 piezas de aguacate` 3 `aguacate`. Like the English words, these are accepted whatever the app's language, since the parser is shared with Siri and the Edge Functions.
- **Spanish catalog.** A Spanish seed (`src/shared/seed/ingredients-es.ts`, the shape of 6.2's) adds names as Mexicans type them to the global catalog: a Mexican Spanish twin for the English seed's names, plus Mexico-only names with no English twin (`epazote`, `queso oaxaca`, `bolillo`). Each Spanish name is its own ingredient (own key) tagged `lang = 'es'` (English names `'en'`; names whose language isn't known, `null`). Units are a US supermarket's whatever the language, since the app is for US shoppers: `leche` is `gal` like `milk`. Autocomplete's catalog part (4.2) puts names in the app's language first.
- **Linked ingredients** (`ingredients.same_as`, `linked_by`, 5). The same thing typed in either language can be one item for a household: `pepino` and `cucumber` always share an aisle and, when the household turns on **Combine English and Spanish names**, tint each other and Combine. A non-English name points at the English ingredient it means (`leche` → `milk`; `papa` and `patata` → `potato`), so English is the hub and another language later adds more names pointing at the same English ones. Links are one level only (a target has no link of its own) and only where the two are the same thing on a shopping list: none for `tomate` (a tomatillo in much of Mexico; `jitomate` → `tomato`), `crema`, `chile` or `queso fresco`, which stay unlinked. Links come from the Spanish seed (`linked_by = 'seed'`, reviewed by a person) and from the categorizer (6.4), which returns each new name's translation and whether the two are exact equivalents; an exact one links at once (`linked_by` = the model id). A model-made twin is always a link: the categorizer creates the translation's row only for an exact equivalent it can link, so every name a model adds can be reviewed through its link. A close-but-not-exact translation (`crema` for `sour cream`) creates nothing, and that word, when someone types it, is categorized on its own terms. The seed may still have unlinked Spanish names (`epazote`, `tomate`). Unlinked twins a model made before this rule are listed, and deleted with `--apply`, by `yarn ai:prune-twins` (only rows with `household_count` 0 and no household's use). A wrong link combines things that differ, so links are recorded with who made them and can be cleared in bulk (`yarn ai:unlink`); a seed link wins over a model link on the same name. A person reviews the model's links from a CSV written by `yarn ai:translations` (UTF-8 with a BOM, like the strings' review sheet): one row per model-made pair (English and Spanish key and name, `linked_by`, the English row's aisle and unit, the larger `household_count`, the newer row's `created_at`, an empty `notes`), most-used first, by default only pairs where neither name has `reviewed_at`; `--unpaired` adds grocery names a model categorized that have no link, to spot links it missed, and `--since`, `--all` and `--limit` narrow or widen it. It writes to `exports/` (ignored by git: the names are typed by real households). `--mark-reviewed <file>` sets `reviewed_at` on every name in a reviewed export (a dry run unless `--apply`), so the next export leaves them out. An ingredient's **family key** is `same_as` if it has one, else its own key:
  - Household aisle overrides (4.3.2) are written and read under the family key, so moving `milk` to another aisle moves `leche` with it, and the other way round.
  - The "already on list" tint (4.2) and duplicates and Combine (4.4) compare family keys when the household's setting is on: adding `pepino` to a list with an unchecked Cucumber adds to it. The item keeps the name it was first added under. With it off (the default) they compare each item's own key, so `pepino` and Cucumber are two items.
  - **The setting** (`households.combine_languages`, off by default) is household data: every member sees and can change it, on the household's screen under Household settings (4.7), a switch "Combine English and Spanish names" ("Leche and milk become one item on your lists."). Turning it on merges nothing already on a list; twins combine the next time one is added or reopened. Turning it off leaves combined items as they are. The links themselves stay in the catalog whatever the setting.
  - Usage history and the usual unit still use the ingredient's own key.
- **AI.** The categorizer (6.4) accepts English and Spanish names, shelves and picks units as a US supermarket would whatever the language, and returns each name's translation for linking. Recipe extraction (6.6) keeps the page's language. Push notifications (4.13) are in English for now; using the recipient's language needs the server to store it (11.1).

### 4.16 Meal plan (req 16)

A household plans its week: what's for breakfast, lunch and dinner each day. *In progress*: `tasks.md` tracks which parts have landed. Mockups: https://claude.ai/artifact/HQKX1qKsoFgNNkgCujWewW (board 1 A and C, board 2 F, board 3 G, board 4 Idea 1, board 5, and the "Plan → tap a slot" prototype). Several recipes in a meal: https://claude.ai/artifact/QkLNzvx5DdDLGwMpGbZmWp (`docs/design/plan-slot-recipes/`, boards A to E). As on the other boards, the floating tab bar is a mock; the app keeps `NativeTabs`.

- **The plan belongs to the household**, like recipes: every member sees and edits the same plan, and it syncs (7) like other household data. A week runs Sunday to Saturday. A **slot** is a day and a meal: breakfast, lunch or dinner, labelled as the Meal tag's values (4.14), each in a fixed hue (breakfast `amber`, lunch `teal`, dinner `plum`; the aisle colours' `soft` and `strong`).
- **An entry** is a recipe from the household or a note: **Leftovers** or **Eating out** (stored as slugs, shown in the app's language) or free text (**Note…**, up to 60 characters, kept as typed, 4.15). **A slot holds any number of recipes, or one note.** There's no limit, so a party dinner can hold eight dishes. A slot's recipes are all alike (no main or side): `position` only orders them, and every place that shows a slot's recipes shows them in that order. Adding a recipe puts it last (`position` one more than the slot's highest live entry) beside the slot's recipes, soft-deleting a note there; a recipe already in the slot isn't added again. Putting a note in a slot soft-deletes its live entries and inserts the note. Reordering sets only the moved row's `position`, halfway between its new neighbours' (it's a `double`). If two devices add recipes to the same slot offline, both rows survive sync and both show. A note and recipes both live (filled offline on two devices): the recipes show and the note is ignored; two notes: the one created last shows. The next change to the slot that puts a note there, or clears it, takes them all out. A recipe that's deleted drops out: its entries aren't shown, so a slot of only that recipe reads as empty.
- **Which household.** With one household, its plan. With several, the week toolbar starts with the household's name and a chevron (a native menu of the user's households; VoiceOver "Household: Our place"). The choice is a device setting (`plan.householdId`), falling back to the current list's household (4.1). Planning from a recipe always uses the recipe's household.
- **Where it lives** is a device setting (`plan.tab`, not synced, so a phone and an iPad can differ), default off: Settings > **Meal plan** > **Plan tab**, a switch with the footnote "Your week plan gets its own tab. Off, it lives in Recipes under This week."
  - On (board 4): a **Plan** tab (SF Symbol `calendar`) between Recipes and Settings. Its root is the week screen, "This week" (`largeTitle`) on the green header, with the week toolbar on the green.
  - Off (board 5; `docs/design/recipes-header/` board B): the Recipes tab's title switches views. It reads the view's name ("Recipes", or "This week" on the week) with a `chevron.down` after it, one button (VoiceOver "Recipes, switch view") that opens a native menu of **All recipes** (`book`) and **This week** (`calendar`), the current one ticked. With the Plan tab on, the title is plain "Recipes". This week replaces the search field (and the Filters in it) and the sort button with the week screen, leaving the title and the +; the week toolbar is the first row of the sheet. The chosen view is kept for the app session. Turning the setting off while on the Plan tab lands on Recipes > This week.
  - Either way, All recipes starts with a **This week** shelf (4.5): the recipes planned from today through Saturday in every household, each at its first slot ("Thu dinner"). Its **See all** opens Recipes > This week, or the Plan tab when it's on. Notes, Leftovers and Eating out aren't on it.
- **The week toolbar**: the week (‹ Oct 11 – 17 ›; VoiceOver "Previous week", "Next week", "Week of October 11"), **This week** while another week shows, the cart (**Shop this week**, below) and the **view toggle** (board 4 Idea 1): two icon segments, **Agenda** (`list.bullet`) and **Grid** (`square.grid.3x3`), VoiceOver "View: Grid" with each segment's selected state. The view is a device setting (`plan.view`), default Grid, the same in both places. Weeks go back and forward without limit.
- **Grid** (`docs/design/plan-grid/` boards B and D, with several recipes `docs/design/plan-slot-recipes/` boards A and B): days Sunday to Saturday down the side, Breakfast, Lunch and Dinner across (headers in their meal hues). Every cell is at least 104 pt tall (`planExpandCell`). A one-recipe cell shows its photo (the household's own, else the site's, 4.5, over the recipe's tile colour, which shows with no photo, while it loads and if it fails) as a 4:3 strip across the top (`rounded.sm`), with the name in `caption` under it, two lines at most. A cell with several recipes is drawn as the **Grid cells** setting says (below). A note cell is `surfaceMuted` with the note in italic `inkSecondary`; an empty cell is a dashed `controlBorder` outline with a +. Today's day label is a `primary` pill. All seven days fit at the default text size while no slot has more than one recipe; several recipes make their day taller, and the week scrolls, as it does at larger text sizes. VoiceOver per cell: "Thursday dinner, Chicken tacos", "Thursday dinner, Chicken tacos, Mexican rice, Guacamole" (every recipe, whatever the cell shows), "Thursday lunch, empty". **Opening a day**: on the week screen a day's label is a button (VoiceOver "Friday 16, expanded" or "collapsed", with the expanded state) that opens the day into one `surface` card spanning the three meal columns: a block per meal, at least 64 pt, with hairlines between. A recipe block has the meal's name in its hue's `strong` (`caption`) and then a row per recipe, in order: a 76 × 57 pt photo (`rounded.sm`), the recipe's name (`headline`, two lines) and its total time (`footnote` `inkSecondary`, "1 hr 30 min"); every recipe, however many. A note block shows the note and "Note"; an empty block shows a dashed photo-sized box with a + and "Nothing planned" (no outline or + on past days). Tapping a block does what tapping its cell does, and blocks keep the cells' VoiceOver labels. One day is open at a time, and tapping its label again closes it. Today is open when the week screen first shows (none in a week without today); the choice is kept, across weeks and the view toggle, while the screen stays mounted. Opening and closing animate the row's height over the `motion` duration, or switch at once under Reduce Motion. The plan modal's days don't open, so its taps always place.
- **Grid cells** (`docs/design/plan-slot-recipes/` boards A and B): a device setting `plan.cellStyle` (`src/features/meal-plan/cell-style.ts`), Settings > Meal plan > **Grid cells**, after Plan tab: a segmented control (VoiceOver "Grid cell style") of **Split photo** (board B; the default and the fallback for any unknown value, never-set included) and **Stacked** (board A). It changes only cells holding two or more recipes, on the week screen and in the plan modal; the open day and the agenda always list every recipe. **Split photo**: the one-recipe cell's 4:3 strip, split side by side into a piece per recipe, `spacing.xxs` apart, each the recipe's photo (cover) over its tile colour; under it the names, one line each (`caption`, truncated). With more than three recipes: the first three pieces and names, then a fourth `surfaceMuted` piece reading "+4" (`caption` `ink`) and a last line "+4 more" (`caption` `inkSecondary`). **Stacked**: every recipe in order, however many, each a full-width photo strip 40 pt tall (`rounded.sm`) over its name (`caption`, two lines at most), with a `border` hairline and `spacing.xs` between recipes; the cell, and so its day, grows to fit. (This replaces the Plan grid design trial, `plan.gridStyle`, whose Expand style is now the grid; a saved `plan.gridStyle` is ignored.)
- **Agenda** (board 1 A; with several recipes `docs/design/plan-slot-recipes/` board C): a card per day, the day on the left, then a line per meal: the meal's letter chip, then a line per recipe in the slot (its name and total time, every recipe alike and in order), the note in italic, or "+ Add lunch" (`primary`). Today's card has a `primary` outline. In the current week, the days before today fold into one **Earlier this week · Sun – Wed** row at the top (a chevron; tapping it unfolds them for the app session; none on Sunday). Other weeks show all seven days.
- **Past days** stay editable (to record what was actually eaten) but are quieter: names in `inkSecondary`, and an empty past slot has no + or "Add" (a plain cell, or "—" in the agenda), though tapping it still opens the slot sheet.
- **Tapping a slot.** Empty: the **slot sheet** (`docs/design/plan-slot-picker/` boards A–C; `now.png` there is the sheet before), titled with the day ("Thursday 15", in the meal's hue) over the meal (`sheetTitle`), then a search field, a row of chips, a line with the count and the sort, the household's recipes, and a **Not cooking?** bar pinned to the bottom. It opens on every recipe of the household, most recently added first (`created_at`), with nothing filtered, so a recipe imported a minute ago is at the top whatever its tags. **Chips** (one row, scrolling sideways): the **Filters** pill and a chip per active filter, as under Add to recipe's search (`RecipeFilterBar`, 4.14; Filters opens the same filter panel inside the sheet), then three quick chips that toggle: the slot's meal ("Breakfast": the meal tag), **Favorites** (Saved: Favorites, anyone's in the household) and **Want to try** (Saved: Want to try); **My favorites** is only in the filter panel. The quick chips are the panel's own values: turning one on shows it picked in the panel and counts in "Filters · N", and it isn't repeated as a ✕ chip. Search, chips and filters all narrow together. **The line under the chips**: "All recipes" with nothing on, else "4 recipes" (`sectionLabel` `inkSecondary`), and on the right the sort, a menu (`primary`) of **Newest first** and **A–Z**; the sort lasts while the sheet is open. Ties go A–Z. **Rows**: the tile, the name, and a second line (`footnote` `inkSecondary`) of the total time (when known) and "Added today", "Added yesterday", the weekday for the last seven days ("Added Mon"), else the date ("Added Oct 12", with the year when it isn't this year). With any chip or filter on, a plain **Clear filters** follows the list. Nothing to show: "No recipes yet" for a household without recipes, else "No recipes match". **Not cooking?** (pinned to the sheet's bottom above the home indicator, on `background` with a `border` hairline above; the list scrolls under it): the label (`sectionLabel` `inkSecondary`) and three equal `surface` buttons, **Leftovers**, **Eating out** and **Note…**, each an icon over its label. Search matches names ignoring case and accents (4.14). **Picking recipes** (`docs/design/plan-slot-picker/d-multi-select`, its states `a`–`d`): each recipe row ends in a tick circle (26 pt, a `controlBorder` ring). Tapping a row ticks it (the row turns `primarySoft` and the circle fills `primary` with an `onPrimary` check; VoiceOver "Caesar salad, selected") and tapping it again unticks it; any number can be ticked, with no cap. Ticks are kept while the search, chips, filters and sort change (a recipe can be ticked, searched away and another found), and go when the sheet closes. With anything ticked, the count line starts "2 selected · " ("2 selected · All recipes", "2 selected · 3 recipes"), and the Not cooking? bar gives way to the **add bar** in its place (on `background` with the same hairline): a sideways row of the ticked recipes in the order ticked, each a `surface` chip with its tile, name and a ✕ (VoiceOver "Remove Caesar salad") that unticks it, over a plain **Clear** (`primary`; unticks everything) and the full-width primary button **Add to dinner** with one ticked or **Add 3 to dinner** (VoiceOver "Add 3 recipes to dinner"). Unticking the last brings the Not cooking? bar back. Leftovers, Eating out and Note… fill the slot at once and close the sheet, as before. The button writes the slot once (`applyPlanChanges`): the ticked recipes go in last, in the order ticked, all equal (no main or side), then the sheet closes and the week shows "Added Caesar salad to Thu dinner" or "Added 3 recipes to Thu dinner" with **Undo**, which puts the slot back as it was. When the sheet replaces what's in the slot (opened from a note's **Replace…** or **Replace all…**), the button reads **Replace dinner** / **Replace with 3**, it replaces everything first, and the toast says "Replaced Thu dinner". A note: a native menu with **Replace…** (the slot sheet), **Edit note…** (free-text notes) and **Clear**, which asks nothing and shows "Cleared Thursday dinner" with **Undo**. Recipes (one or more): the **meal sheet** (`docs/design/plan-slot-recipes/` board D), titled like the slot sheet, with a card of the slot's recipes in order, each row its tile, name (`headline`) and total time; tapping a row opens the recipe. Each row has a remove button (`minus.circle` in `danger`, VoiceOver "Remove Caesar salad") that takes it out with a toast "Removed Caesar salad" and **Undo**, and a drag handle (`line.3.horizontal`) that reorders, as the Your lists sheet's Edit does (4.1). The card's last row is **Add a recipe** (a `plus` and the label in `primary`), which opens the **add sheet** (board E): the slot sheet titled "Add to dinner" under "Tuesday 13 · Dinner", a `primarySoft` line "With Lemon ricotta pasta, Caesar salad" (the slot's recipes, truncated), no Not cooking? bar, and the slot's recipes in the list ticked, disabled and captioned "Already in dinner" (a filled `surfaceMuted` circle with an `inkSecondary` check, at the disabled opacity; they're never in the add bar or its count). Recipes are ticked as in the slot sheet (state `d`); with none ticked there's no bar at all. **Add to dinner** / **Add 2 to dinner** adds them last, in the order ticked, and goes back to the meal sheet, which shows "Added 2 recipes to dinner" with **Undo**. Under the card, a footnote (`inkSecondary`) "Tap a recipe to open it. Drag to change the order they show in.", then **Replace all…** (the slot sheet; its **Replace with 3** replaces everything in the slot with the ticked recipes) and **Clear dinner** (`dangerSoft`), which closes the sheet and shows "Cleared Thursday dinner" with **Undo**, putting every recipe back. Removing the last recipe closes the sheet the same way.
- **Plan from a recipe** (the prototype): **Plan** (`calendar`, a compact button after Add to a list and Cook in the recipe screen's action dock, 4.5) on the recipe screen, and **Plan…** in the recipe's Recipes card menu, open the **plan modal** whatever the setting: a sheet with the grid for the recipe's household on the current week (with the week arrows), the recipe's tile and name on its green header, "Tap a slot to plan it" under it, **Cancel** and **Done**. The modal's cells are drawn as the week's (Grid cells included) and its days don't open. Each tap adds the recipe to that slot, after the recipes already there (an empty slot just gets it; a note there is replaced); the cell turns `primarySoft` with a `primary` border, and a toast says "Planned for Thu dinner" ("Added to Thu dinner" beside other recipes, "Replaced Eating out on Sat dinner" over a note), with **Undo**. A slot that already holds the recipe takes no tap: the toast says "Already planned for Thu dinner". Tapping a cell placed in this visit takes it out again and restores what was there. Column headers of the recipe's meal tags are highlighted. The CTA reads **Done** or **Done · 2 planned**. Nothing is written until Done, which writes every change in one transaction; Cancel or swiping the sheet away writes nothing. VoiceOver per cell adds "double-tap to plan", "to add" (beside other recipes), "to replace" (a note), "to remove" or "already planned".
- **Plan badges**: the recipe screen's header band shows a pill per upcoming entry of the recipe (today or later, the first three then "+2"), "Thu dinner", or "Oct 21 dinner" beyond this week, in the meal's `strong` hue; tapping one opens the plan modal.
- **Shop this week** (board 3 G): the cart opens a sheet, "Shop the rest of the week" (Thursday to Saturday; for a future week, "Shop the week of Oct 18", every day; disabled for past weeks). It has the list picker (the household's lists, the current one first) and a checklist of the week's recipe entries, each with its day and meal (every recipe in a slot is an entry of its own, in slot order). A recipe already on the chosen list (it has a contribution there) starts unticked with "already on Groceries". **Add N to Groceries** adds the ticked entries' ingredients at 1×, the household's staples left out, combined across recipes and with the list (4.4 bulk add), as one add with the toast "Added 3 recipes · 18 items", **Undo** and **Open list**. A recipe planned twice is added twice. **Review N items** first shows the merged ingredients as Add to list's checklist (4.5).

---

## 5. Data model

Postgres (Supabase). Row ids are UUIDv7 generated on the client so rows can be created offline. Most tables have `created_at` and `updated_at` (server-set; left out of the DDL below). Exceptions with no `updated_at`: `household_members`, `household_invites`, `ingredient_households`, `plans`, `plan_features`, `household_plans`, `admin.admin_actions`, `device_credentials`. Household data synced through `apply_mutations` has `field_clock jsonb` for conflict resolution (7.3), and soft-deletable rows have `deleted_at` (needed for sync); `categories`, `household_category_overrides`, `ingredient_usage` and `ingredient_unit_usage` have no `deleted_at`, and `households` / `household_members` have no `field_clock` (they change only through functions).

**Foreign keys**: every `household_id` is `on delete cascade`, so deleting a household removes its data. Children of a list or recipe reference it together with the household (`foreign key (list_id, household_id) references lists (id, household_id) on delete cascade`, likewise for `recipe_id`; each parent has `unique (id, household_id)`), which is what keeps a child in its parent's household. References to `auth.users` on rows that outlive their author (`checked_by`, `added_by`, `created_by`, `used_by`) are `on delete set null` (per-user rows, such as `user_settings`, `notification_settings` and `push_tokens`, are `on delete cascade`), as are `list_items.merged_into` and `user_settings.default_list_id`. The DDL below shows plain references for brevity; the migrations are the source of truth.

**Ingredient key**: ingredients are identified by `normalized_name` (text primary key). The client can always compute it, offline included, so list items reference an ingredient without waiting for the server. No provisional ids, no rewriting.

```sql
-- System categories (global, shared across all users) and households' custom ones
create table categories (
  id            uuid primary key,
  slug          text unique,          -- e.g. 'produce'; null for custom
  name          text not null,
  household_id  uuid references households on delete cascade, -- null = system category
  default_rank  int,                   -- default store-walk order for system categories
  field_clock   jsonb not null default '{}',  -- custom ones sync as household data (7.3)
  check ((slug is not null and household_id is null) or (slug is null and household_id is not null))
);

create table ingredients (
  normalized_name     text primary key,
  display_name        text not null,
  default_category_id uuid references categories,     -- null while pending
  status              text not null default 'pending', -- pending | categorized | failed
  is_grocery_item     boolean,                          -- null until categorized
  categorized_by      text,                             -- e.g. model id, 'seed', 'admin'
  default_unit        text,                             -- usual unit until a household has history (4.2): a unit or 'count'; null = unknown
  lang                text,                             -- 'en' | 'es' (4.15); null when not known
  same_as             text references ingredients,      -- the English ingredient this name means (4.15); one level only
  linked_by           text,                             -- who made the same_as link: 'seed', a model id, or 'admin'; null without a link
  reviewed_at         timestamptz,                      -- when a person reviewed the name's translation (yarn ai:translations, 4.15); null until then
  attempts            int not null default 0,
  household_count     int not null default 0,           -- see 6.5
  created_at          timestamptz not null default now(),
  updated_at          timestamptz not null default now()
);

-- Which households have used an ingredient. Service role only; used to maintain household_count.
create table ingredient_households (
  ingredient_name text references ingredients on delete cascade,
  household_id    uuid references households on delete cascade,
  primary key (ingredient_name, household_id)
);

-- Households (req 7)
create table households (
  id                uuid primary key,
  name              text not null,
  combine_languages boolean not null default false, -- Combine English and Spanish names (4.15); any member sets it with set_combine_languages
  created_by        uuid references auth.users on delete set null -- informational only; roles live in household_members
);

-- A user can be in many households (Q6). Every household has at least one owner,
-- and every user at least one membership; both are enforced by the household functions.
create table household_members (
  household_id uuid references households on delete cascade,
  user_id      uuid references auth.users on delete cascade,
  role         text not null default 'member', -- owner | member
  joined_at    timestamptz not null default now(), -- used for owner promotion
  position     double precision not null,          -- this user's household order (per user)
  primary key (household_id, user_id)
);
create index on household_members (user_id);

create table household_invites (
  id           uuid primary key,
  household_id uuid references households on delete cascade,
  token_hash   text not null unique,
  created_by   uuid references auth.users,
  created_at   timestamptz not null default now(),
  expires_at   timestamptz not null,
  used_by      uuid references auth.users,
  used_at      timestamptz,
  invited_email text check (invited_email = lower(invited_email) and invited_email ~ '^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$'), -- null = link-only
  revoked_at   timestamptz,
  declined_at  timestamptz
);
-- One open (not used, revoked or declined) invite per household and email.
create unique index on household_invites (household_id, invited_email)
  where invited_email is not null and used_at is null and revoked_at is null and declined_at is null;

-- Household data
create table lists (
  id           uuid primary key,
  household_id uuid not null references households,
  name         text not null,
  emoji        text,
  color        text check (color in ('green', 'blue', 'rose', 'amber', 'grey')),  -- null = Default (4.1)
  position     double precision not null,
  archived_at  timestamptz,
  deleted_at   timestamptz,
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}'
);

create table recipes (
  id           uuid primary key,
  household_id uuid not null references households,
  name         text not null,
  notes        text,
  source_url   text,
  tags          jsonb,                           -- 4.14; null = never tagged
  tags_edited   boolean not null default false,  -- a person has edited the tags
  total_minutes int check (total_minutes between 1 and 2880),
  steps         jsonb,                           -- null = none, else 1–100 entries (private.recipe_steps_ok): step strings of 1–2000 chars and, between them, section headings (4.5)
  step_ingredients jsonb,                        -- 4.5; null = never matched (private.recipe_step_ingredients_ok)
  servings      int check (servings between 1 and 100),
  image_url     text check (image_url like 'https://%' and char_length(image_url) <= 2048),
  photo_path    text,                            -- 4.5; the household's own photo, null = none (recipes_photo_path_shape)
  source_pages  jsonb,                           -- 4.10; the original pages of a photo import, null = none (recipes_source_pages_shape)
  added_by      uuid references auth.users on delete set null,  -- the server sets it on insert
  deleted_at   timestamptz,
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}'
);
-- recipes_steps_shape (private.recipe_steps_ok): an array of 1–100 entries with at least one step. A step is
-- a string of 1–2000 characters; a heading is { "section": <1–80 chars> } or { "section": …, "choice": true },
-- no other keys, at most 20 of them. Cleaning (cleanRecipeSteps) also drops a heading with no step after it
-- and a heading straight after another; the check doesn't. On the device it's recipes.steps as JSON text.
-- recipes_tags_shape: tags may only have the keys saved, meal, cuisine, protein, diet and method,
-- each an array of strings. The values aren't checked, so adding one needs no migration. A value
-- without saved (stored before it existed) reads as not saved.
-- recipes_photo_path_shape: '<household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg' (lowercase uuids), its first folder
-- equal to the recipe's household_id. Synced and merged per field through field_clock like the other
-- columns; shown instead of image_url when set. The object lives in the recipe-photos bucket (below).
-- recipes_source_pages_shape (private.recipe_source_pages_ok): an array of 1–3 distinct paths in
-- page order, each '<household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg' (lowercase uuids), its folders the row's
-- own household_id and id. Synced and merged per field like photo_path (the whole array is one
-- field). The objects live in the recipe-pages bucket (below). On the device it's recipes.source_pages
-- as JSON text (local migration 0024_recipe_source_pages), null = none.
-- recipes_step_ingredients_shape: { "v": 1, "basis": "<hash>", "steps": [[{ "id": "<recipe_ingredient_id>",
-- "quantity": null }], …] }, one entry per step: 1–100 steps of at most 80 uses each, quantity null
-- (all of it, or not stated) or the step's positive share. The ids aren't checked against
-- recipe_ingredients; the app drops deleted ones. basis is stepIngredientsBasis(steps, live
-- ingredient ids) when the match was made; when it differs from the recipe's now, the match is
-- stale but still shown.

-- The household's own recipe photos (4.5): Storage bucket recipe-photos, private, image/jpeg only,
-- 2 MB per object (also in supabase/config.toml for local). Objects at
-- <household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg. Policies on storage.objects: a member of the household in
-- the first folder (storage.foldername) may select, insert (name of that shape) and delete there;
-- nobody else may, and nobody may update (a new photo is a new object). An admin
-- (public.is_admin(), 4.12) may also select any object (recipe_photos_select_admin), so the portal
-- can sign URLs for the AI photos; not audited.
-- Cleanup is on the server, so an offline device can't leave objects behind. Triggers queue into
-- private.recipe_photo_deletions: the old object when photo_path changes or is cleared, a recipe's
-- object when its row is deleted (a purge or a household's cascade), and the whole
-- '<household_id>/' prefix when a household is deleted (by an owner, its last member leaving, or
-- account deletion). Every 15 minutes cron calls the photo-cleanup Edge Function (pg_net, with the
-- Vault secrets project_url and photo_cleanup_key) when anything is queued; it claims entries at
-- least an hour old (claim_recipe_photo_deletions, which drops objects a recipe points at again, as
-- after Undo, and ones already gone), removes them through the Storage API (SQL can't delete
-- Storage files) and completes them (complete_recipe_photo_deletions). Both are service role only.
-- An AI photo (6.11) is queued here too, by complete_recipe_photo_ai, as soon as it's uploaded: if
-- the app never sets it as photo_path (killed mid-way), the sweep removes it; once it does, the
-- sweep drops the entry as after Undo.
-- A recipe's original pages (4.10): Storage bucket recipe-pages, set up as recipe-photos: private,
-- image/jpeg only, 2 MB per object (also in supabase/config.toml), objects at
-- <household_id>/<recipe_id>/<uuid>.jpg, select, insert (name of that shape) and delete for members
-- of the household in the first folder, no update. Cleaned up through its own queue
-- private.recipe_page_deletions: each old path no longer in the array when source_pages changes or
-- is cleared, the recipe's '<household_id>/<recipe_id>/' prefix when its row is deleted (a purge or
-- a household's cascade; it also catches uploads that never made it into source_pages), and the
-- '<household_id>/' prefix when a household is deleted. claim_recipe_page_deletions /
-- complete_recipe_page_deletions (service role only) work as the photo ones: entries at least an
-- hour old, never a page a recipe points at again. The same cron job calls photo-cleanup when any
-- queue has entries.

-- The household's meal plan (4.16). One row per entry in a slot (a day and a meal). A slot holds any
-- number of recipes, ordered by position, or one note.
create table meal_plan_entries (
  id           uuid primary key,
  household_id uuid not null references households on delete cascade,
  plan_date    date not null,                       -- the calendar day, no time zone
  meal         text not null check (meal in ('breakfast', 'lunch', 'dinner')),
  position     double precision not null default 0, -- order within the slot
  kind         text not null check (kind in ('recipe', 'leftovers', 'eating-out', 'note')),
  recipe_id    uuid,                                -- (recipe_id, household_id) references recipes
  note         text,                                -- kept as typed (4.15)
  added_by     uuid references auth.users on delete set null, -- the server sets it on insert
  deleted_at   timestamptz,
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}',
  constraint meal_plan_entries_recipe_kind check ((kind = 'recipe') = (recipe_id is not null)),
  constraint meal_plan_entries_note_kind check (
    (kind = 'note') = (note is not null)
    and (note is null or (note = btrim(note) and char_length(note) between 1 and 60))
  )
);
create index on meal_plan_entries (household_id, plan_date);

create table list_items (
  id              uuid primary key,
  list_id         uuid not null references lists,
  household_id    uuid not null references households,  -- denormalized for RLS
  ingredient_name text not null references ingredients,
  display_name    text not null,                         -- what the user typed or said
  note            text,
  checked_at      timestamptz,
  checked_by      uuid references auth.users,            -- the server sets it with checked_at (7.1)
  added_by        uuid references auth.users,            -- the server sets it on insert
  deleted_at      timestamptz,
  merged_into     uuid references list_items,            -- set by apply_mutations when merged (7.3)
  photo_path      text,                                  -- the item's photo in item-photos, null = none
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}'
);
-- at most one live unchecked item per ingredient per list
create unique index one_open_item_per_ingredient
  on list_items (list_id, ingredient_name)
  where deleted_at is null and checked_at is null;
-- photo_path: '<household_id>/<list_item_id>/<uuid>.jpg', lowercase, its folders the row's own
-- household and id (check list_items_photo_path_shape). Synced, field-level LWW like note.
-- Item photos: Storage bucket item-photos, set up as recipe-photos (above): private, image/jpeg
-- only, 2 MB, member select/insert/delete on the first folder, no update. Cleanup is the same too,
-- through its own queue private.item_photo_deletions (the old object when photo_path changes or is
-- cleared, an item's object when its row is deleted, the household's prefix when a household is
-- deleted) and claim_item_photo_deletions / complete_item_photo_deletions. The same cron job calls
-- photo-cleanup when any queue (these, recipe photos' or recipe pages') has entries, and
-- photo-cleanup sweeps all three buckets.

create table list_item_contributions (
  id           uuid primary key,
  list_item_id uuid not null references list_items,
  household_id uuid not null references households,
  source       text not null default 'manual', -- manual | recipe | siri
  recipe_id    uuid references recipes,        -- set when source = 'recipe'
  quantity     numeric,                        -- null = unspecified
  unit         text,
  added_by     uuid references auth.users,     -- the server sets it on insert
  deleted_at   timestamptz,
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}'
);

create table recipe_ingredients (
  id              uuid primary key,
  recipe_id       uuid not null references recipes,
  household_id    uuid not null references households,
  ingredient_name text not null references ingredients,
  display_name    text not null,
  quantity        numeric,
  unit            text,
  note            text,
  position        double precision not null,
  deleted_at      timestamptz,
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}'
);

-- Household ingredient history for autocomplete (4.2). Synced in M5 like other household data,
-- except use_count, which merges as a counter (each device's increments and decrements add up)
-- rather than last-writer-wins. display_name, last_used_at and hidden_at are last-writer-wins.
create table ingredient_usage (
  household_id    uuid not null references households,
  ingredient_name text not null,                         -- ingredient key
  display_name    text not null,                         -- most recent text typed for it
  use_count       int not null default 0,
  last_used_at    timestamptz not null,
  hidden_at       timestamptz,                           -- "Remove from suggestions"; cleared by the next use
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}',
  primary key (household_id, ingredient_name)
);

-- Units each ingredient is bought in, for its usual unit (4.2). Household data synced in M5, kept out
-- of ingredient_usage so it merges with plain counters: use_count merges as a counter (like
-- ingredient_usage.use_count) and last_used_at as the max of both sides. No other merge logic.
create table ingredient_unit_usage (
  household_id    uuid not null references households,
  ingredient_name text not null,                         -- ingredient key
  unit_key        text not null,                         -- a supported unit, or 'count' for a quantity with no unit
  use_count       int not null default 0,
  last_used_at    timestamptz not null,
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}',           -- unused (no LWW fields); kept for uniformity
  primary key (household_id, ingredient_name, unit_key)
);

-- A household's aisle for an ingredient (req 6, 4.3.2). Household data: every member sees it.
-- category_id is the only field written (LWW through apply_mutations); null means "the default",
-- which is how Reset to default is written, so a later change can still win.
create table household_category_overrides (
  household_id    uuid not null references households on delete cascade,
  ingredient_name text not null references ingredients,
  category_id     uuid references categories on delete cascade, -- a system category or one of this household's
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}',
  primary key (household_id, ingredient_name)
);

-- Household staples, "Always have" (4.5). Household data written through apply_mutations
-- (deleted_at only: unmarking sets it, marking again clears it).
create table household_staples (
  household_id    uuid not null references households on delete cascade,
  ingredient_name text not null,                                  -- ingredient key
  created_by      uuid references auth.users on delete set null,  -- the server sets it on insert
  deleted_at      timestamptz,
  field_clock     jsonb not null default '{}',
  primary key (household_id, ingredient_name)
);

-- Favorites, per person (4.14). Household data written through apply_mutations, each member only
-- their own rows (user_id is the caller, household_id the recipe's); readable by every member of the
-- household, so others' favorites show. Unfavoriting sets deleted_at; leaving deletes the leaver's rows.
create table recipe_favorites (
  recipe_id    uuid not null references recipes on delete cascade,
  user_id      uuid not null references auth.users on delete cascade,
  household_id uuid not null references households on delete cascade,
  deleted_at   timestamptz,
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}',
  primary key (recipe_id, user_id)
);

-- Aisle orders (req 9, 4.3.3). Each row is a whole order: category ids, first aisle first, no
-- duplicates, each a system category or a custom one of the same household. Missing aisles go at
-- the end and unknown ids are skipped when read (4.3.3), so deleting an aisle leaves the arrays alone.
-- (Replaced the per-user, cross-household user_category_order, which was dropped with its data.)

-- The household's order. Household data: written through apply_mutations, category_ids as one LWW field.
create table household_category_order (
  household_id uuid primary key references households on delete cascade,
  category_ids jsonb not null,                                   -- array of category id strings
  updated_by   uuid references auth.users on delete set null,    -- the server sets it
  field_clock  jsonb not null default '{}'
);

-- A member's own order for one household. Written directly (PostgREST), not through apply_mutations.
-- Deleted when they leave the household (and with the user or household).
create table user_household_category_order (
  user_id      uuid references auth.users on delete cascade,
  household_id uuid references households on delete cascade,
  category_ids jsonb not null,
  primary key (user_id, household_id)
);

-- Display info for showing members ("Alex added...", Households screen). auth.users isn't readable by clients.
-- Readable by the user and by anyone who shares a household with them; filled from the Google profile.
-- Names: at sign-up, given_name / family_name from the auth metadata when there's a given name,
-- else full_name, then name, split at the first space (the first word, then the rest), else the
-- email's part before the @ as the first name, else "Someone". The user can update their own first
-- and last name (and avatar_url). private.full_name(first_name, last_name) is the first name plus
-- " " and the last name when there is one: the inviter's name in invites, and the name the admin
-- portal shows, searches and records (under the key display_name; the column itself is gone).
create table profiles (
  user_id      uuid primary key references auth.users on delete cascade,
  first_name   text not null,          -- trimmed, 1-50 characters
  last_name    text,                   -- trimmed, 1-50 characters; null when there is none
  avatar_url   text
);

create table user_settings (
  user_id         uuid primary key references auth.users on delete cascade,
  default_list_id uuid references lists
);

-- Push notifications (4.13). Per user, read and written online; never through the outbox or sync.
-- One row per signed-in device. No direct client access: register_push_token / unregister_push_token.
create table push_tokens (
  token    text primary key,                    -- the Expo push token, ExponentPushToken[…]
  user_id  uuid not null references auth.users on delete cascade,  -- indexed
  platform text not null check (platform in ('ios', 'android'))
);

-- A missing row means the defaults. Quiet hours are both null (off) or both set and different;
-- a start later than the end wraps past midnight. time_zone must be in pg_timezone_names (trigger).
create table notification_settings (
  user_id           uuid primary key references auth.users on delete cascade,
  items_added       boolean not null default true,
  shopping          boolean not null default true,
  recipe_imports    boolean not null default true,  -- the sharer's "ready" and "couldn't import" pushes (4.13)
  quiet_hours_start time,
  quiet_hours_end   time,
  time_zone         text not null default 'UTC'   -- IANA name
);

-- Device credentials (6.12): a scoped token the share extension uploads with, never a Supabase session.
-- No client grants: register-device-credential, the revoke functions and share-ingest (service role) only.
create table device_credentials (
  id              uuid primary key,
  user_id         uuid not null references auth.users on delete cascade,
  installation_id uuid not null,                       -- random, made once per app install, kept across sign-outs
  token_hash      bytea not null unique,               -- sha256 of the 32-byte random token; the token isn't stored
  scopes          text[] not null default '{share.ingest}',   -- later 'siri.add'
  created_at      timestamptz not null default now(),
  last_used_at    timestamptz,
  expires_at      timestamptz not null,                -- 90 days after issue
  revoked_at      timestamptz,
  revoked_reason  text                                 -- 'replaced', 'sign_out', 'admin'
);
-- One live credential per (user_id, installation_id): a partial unique index where revoked_at is null.

-- Share imports (4.10, 6.12): one row per shared recipe, the operation, keyed by the envelope id.
-- No client access to the table; the sharer reads their own rows through my_share_imports, and every
-- write is a service-role function (share-ingest, the worker's RPCs) or retry_share_import.
create table share_imports (
  id                uuid primary key,                  -- the envelope id
  user_id           uuid not null references auth.users on delete cascade,  -- from authentication, never the payload
  credential_id     uuid references device_credentials on delete set null,  -- null: submitted with a session
  household_id      uuid not null,
  list_id           uuid,
  add_to_list       boolean not null,
  url               text not null,
  url_key           text not null,                     -- canonicalRecipeUrl
  title             text,                              -- page.title from the envelope, for the imports card; kept
  payload_hash      bytea not null,                    -- sha256 of the canonical (url, household, list, addToList, page)
  page              jsonb,                             -- the Safari capture; nulled at done, failed, or after 24 h
  status            text not null check (status in ('received', 'extracting', 'extracted', 'committing', 'done', 'failed')),
  attempts          integer not null default 0,
  next_attempt_at   timestamptz not null default now(),
  lease_token       uuid,
  lease_expires_at  timestamptz,
  error_code        text,                              -- e.g. 'not_a_recipe', 'daily_limit'
  error_kind        text check (error_kind in ('transient', 'permanent', 'waiting')),
  resets_at         timestamptz,                       -- daily_limit / limit_reached: when the limit resets
  metered_at        timestamptz,                       -- the import was charged (once per operation)
  extraction        jsonb,                             -- RecipeImportResponse; nulled 7 days after done
  -- the receipt, compact and kept with the row:
  recipe_id         uuid,
  applied_list_id   uuid,                              -- null for recipe-only, and with list_gone
  list_gone         boolean not null default false,    -- no list was left, so it was saved recipe-only
  items_added       integer,
  items_combined    integer,
  committed_at      timestamptz,
  client_created_at timestamptz,
  app_version       text,
  source            text
);

-- The sharer's own share imports, without page or extraction (security_invoker off; filters user_id = auth.uid()).
create view my_share_imports as
  select id, household_id, list_id, add_to_list, url, title, status, attempts, next_attempt_at, error_code, error_kind,
         resets_at, recipe_id, applied_list_id, list_gone, items_added, items_combined, committed_at,
         client_created_at, created_at, updated_at
  from share_imports where user_id = auth.uid();

-- Audit log of admin functions and delete_account (4.8, 4.12), one row per call. In the `admin` schema; no foreign keys,
-- so a row outlives the user or household it describes.
create table admin.admin_actions (
  id                  uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  action              text not null,                         -- e.g. 'delete_user', 'delete_account'
  target_user_id      uuid,
  target_household_id uuid,
  details             jsonb not null default '{}',
  note                text,                                  -- who asked and why
  performed_by        text not null default session_user,    -- 'postgres' from the dashboard; the admin's user id from the portal
  created_at          timestamptz not null default now(),
  target_plan_id      text                                   -- no foreign key, so a deleted plan keeps its history
);

-- Admins (4.12): who may use the admin portal. In `private`, RLS on, every privilege revoked from public, anon,
-- authenticated and service_role; edited in the Table Editor. public.is_admin() reads it for the caller.
create table private.admins (
  user_id  uuid primary key references auth.users on delete cascade,
  added_at timestamptz not null default now()
);

-- User activity (4.12 User activity, 6.5): one row per change a signed-in user's write made, written by the
-- private.log_user_activity() triggers. In `private`, RLS on, no policies, every privilege revoked from the client
-- roles (public, anon, authenticated): service role only, like the other server-only tables. Indexed on (user_id, created_at desc), (user_id, area, created_at desc) and
-- (created_at). Rows older than 90 days are deleted daily (the user-activity-retention cron job).
create table private.user_activity (
  id           bigint generated always as identity primary key,
  user_id      uuid not null,                       -- no foreign key, like admin.admin_actions; delete_account deletes the rows itself
  household_id uuid,                                -- null for per-user rows (settings, devices)
  created_at   timestamptz not null default now(),
  txid         bigint not null default txid_current(),  -- rows from one transaction (one gesture) share it
  area         text not null,                       -- 'lists', 'recipes', 'plan', 'aisles', 'household', 'settings', 'devices'
  action       text not null,                       -- the catalog in 4.12, e.g. 'item.checked'
  entity_id    text,                                -- the row's id or key
  data         jsonb not null default '{}'          -- names as they were then and small values, never free text
);

-- Plans (4.12). A household's plan is its household_plans row; every household has one.
create table plans (
  id         text primary key,                 -- 'founding', 'unlimited', 'free'
  name       text not null,
  is_default boolean not null default false     -- at most one (partial unique index); new households only
);

create table plan_features (
  plan_id text references plans on delete cascade,
  feature text not null check (feature in ('recipe_import', 'recipe_photo_ai')),
  primary key (plan_id, feature)
);

-- Written with the default plan by create_household and private.create_personal_household (4.7);
-- no row means no features. Changed in the portal, never cleared.
create table household_plans (
  household_id uuid primary key references households on delete cascade,
  plan_id      text not null references plans,  -- indexed
  assigned_at  timestamptz not null default now()  -- reset by a before update trigger (private.set_assigned_at)
);

create table plan_limits (                      -- 4.12; null or no row = no monthly limit
  plan_id       text references plans on delete cascade,
  feature       text check (feature in ('recipe_import', 'recipe_photo_ai')),
  monthly_limit integer check (monthly_limit >= 0),  -- per calendar month, UTC, per household
  primary key (plan_id, feature)
);

-- Safety limits (4.12): per user, the same on every plan. Null = no limit.
create table ai_safety_limits (
  key        text primary key check (key in ('categorize', 'step_match', 'recipe_tags', 'recipe_import_link',
                                             'recipe_import_photo', 'recipe_import', 'recipe_photo_ai',
                                             'share_ingest')),
  per_minute integer check (per_minute >= 0),
  per_day    integer check (per_day >= 0)    -- the user's UTC day
);
```

Note the `create table` order above is for readability; the real migration must create referenced tables first.

**Server-only tables** (service role only: RLS on with no policies, no client grants; pg_cron deletes rows older than 30 days, except flagged `ai_calls` rows and the rows linked to them, kept 180 days; no user id except on `ai_calls` and `private.user_activity`, per 6.5):
- `ai_calls`: one row per model attempt by `runAiTask`, plus one per AI photo moderation check (`recipePhotoModeration`) and image call (`recipePhotoImage`, 6.11) (task, `provider:model`, prompt version, status (`ok`, `api_error`, `invalid_output`, `timeout`, `refused`, `error`), instructions, prompt, output, tokens, duration, error, `fallback_of`), for debugging prompts, watching cost and finding misuse (6.4). `recipe_id` (references `recipes`, `on delete set null`) is the recipe an AI photo's rows are for, null for every other task; deleting the recipe nulls it and keeps the row. A failed attempt the provider answered also keeps that answer: `http_status` (integer) and `error_body` (the response body, cut to 4,000 characters), both null on `ok` rows and on failures with no HTTP answer (invalid output, a timeout). `raw_finish_reason` (text) is the provider's own finish reason beside the SDK's unified `finish_reason`, and a failed attempt keeps `provider_metadata` (jsonb), the parts of the provider's metadata that say why it was blocked (6.4); both are null on image rows. It records who made the call: `user_id` (references `auth.users`, `on delete set null`) and `household_id` (references `households`, `on delete set null`) when the request has one, both null for calls with no signed-in caller (the `yarn ai:*` scripts and evals), indexed on (`user_id`, `created_at desc`). Deleting the account or the household nulls them and keeps the row; no other copy of who made a call is kept. Retention (the daily `ai-calls-retention` job): a row is deleted after 30 days unless it, the row its `fallback_of` points to, or a row whose `fallback_of` is its id, is flagged (`private.ai_call_flag`, 4.12); those are deleted after 180 days, so a refused image keeps the description holding the recipe the user wrote, and the other way round.
- `recipe_imports`: the extraction cache (6.6), keyed by (`url_key`, `prompt_version`), holding the cleaned result and `is_recipe`.
- `private.applied_counters` (`table_name`, `row_key`, `hlc`): counter deltas `apply_mutations` has already applied, so a re-sent mutation doesn't count twice (7.3).
- `private.user_activity` (above; 4.12 User activity): what each user's writes did, with their user id and the household, kept 90 days rather than 30 (the daily `user-activity-retention` job), so an admin can follow a user's last three months. No app or portal client can read it: admins read it only through `admin_user_activity` (4.12), which audits the first page. `delete_account` and `admin.delete_user` delete the user's rows and scrub the user from other users' entries (4.8).

**Local-only tables** (SQLite, never synced; `src/db/schema.ts`): `device_settings`, `outbox` (7.1), `failed_imports` (4.10) and `household_member_names` (4.7).

`household_member_names` (`household_id`, `user_id`, `first_name` (not null), `last_name` (null when there is none), `joined_at`; key `household_id, user_id`; no foreign keys) is a cache of `get_household_members`, so the app can name members offline. It is never pushed (not in `mutate.ts` or the outbox). Each fill replaces one household's rows: after every full pull, for each of the user's households, and when Realtime reports a membership change in a household. A failed call keeps the rows the household had. It is purged with the household (`purge.ts`) and on sign-out and account deletion (`wipeLocalData`).

**AI usage counters** (service role only, in the `private` schema; RLS on, privileges revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`). Each AI function has a per-user table keyed by (`user_id`, UTC `day`), holding the requests in the current fixed one-minute window (`requests_this_minute`, `minute_start`) and the day's count; the per-minute safety limit is checked against the window and the daily one against the day's count (4.12, `public.ai_safety_limits`). The monthly limits (4.12) are per household and only for the two plan features, so their counts also go to `private.household_ai_usage` (`household_id` references `households` on delete cascade, `feature` (`recipe_import` or `recipe_photo_ai`), UTC `day`, `used`; key all three), in `plan_limits`' units, written by the same `check_*_limit` call; the monthly limit is checked against the sum of the month's days (`private.monthly_usage(household, feature)`) and `private.monthly_limit(feature, household)`. Categorize, tags and step matching have no monthly limit and no household counts. There's no total across households. Only its `check_*_limit` function (`security definer`, `service_role` only) writes them, and pg_cron deletes rows older than 40 days daily, so a month's sum is always complete.
- `categorize_usage`: LLM-bound names (6.4), checked by `check_categorize_limit(user_id, new_names)` (safety key `categorize`).
- `recipe_import_usage`: link imports (6.6), checked by `check_recipe_import_limit(user_id, household_id)` (safety keys `recipe_import_link` and `recipe_import`; the household's `recipe_import`).
- `recipe_tags_usage`: tag suggestions (`suggestions`, 6.7), checked by `check_recipe_tags_limit(user_id, household_id)` (safety key `recipe_tags`).
- `step_match_usage`, `recipe_photos_usage` and `recipe_photo_import_usage`: step matches (6.8), page-photo requests (6.9) and photo imports (6.10), checked by `check_step_match_limit` (safety key `step_match`) and `check_recipe_photo_limit` (safety keys `recipe_import_photo` and `recipe_import`; the household's `recipe_import`, shared with link imports) (each `(user_id, household_id)`) and `check_recipe_photos_limit` (`(user_id)`). Page photos aren't AI and have no feature, safety row or monthly limit: `check_recipe_photos_limit` keeps a hard-coded 10 a minute and 50 a day.
- `recipe_photo_ai_usage` (`photos`): AI photos reserved (6.11), written by `reserve_recipe_photo_ai` (safety key `recipe_photo_ai`; the household's `recipe_photo_ai`); a released slot isn't given back, since its calls were made.
- `plan_limits` and `ai_safety_limits` (above) are in `public` but have no client access either: RLS on, every privilege revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`; only the dashboard and these `security definer` functions read them (`private.safety_limit(key)` reads a safety row).

**AI photos** (6.11; service role only, in `private`, RLS on, privileges revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`; not synced). `private.recipe_photo_ai (recipe_id uuid primary key references recipes on delete cascade, household_id uuid not null, created_by uuid references auth.users on delete set null, description text, path text, status text check in ('pending', 'done'), created_at)` only records that a recipe has had its one AI photo: a `done` row stays when the photo is later removed or replaced. Three `security definer` functions, `service_role` only:
- `reserve_recipe_photo_ai(user_id, recipe_id)` returns one `(status, household_id)` row. `blocked` first, when the user has a `private.ai_photo_blocks` row (below; no slot, no usage). It locks the recipe row, so two calls for one recipe can't both reserve. `not_found` unless the recipe exists, isn't deleted and the user is a member of its household; `already_generated` when it has a `done` row or a `pending` one under 10 minutes old; `monthly_limit` when the recipe's household has reserved its plan's monthly number (4.12; under a per-household advisory lock); none of these writes anything; else it counts the reservation in `recipe_photo_ai_usage`, inserts a `pending` row (or replaces a stale one) and returns `ok` with the household id.
- `complete_recipe_photo_ai(recipe_id, description, path)` marks the pending row `done` with the description and path (returning whether there was one) and always queues `path` in `private.recipe_photo_deletions` (above), so an uploaded photo the app never sets is cleaned up.
- `release_recipe_photo_ai(recipe_id)` deletes a `pending` row, so a failed generation doesn't use the recipe's one photo; a `done` row is never released.

`private.ai_photo_blocks (user_id uuid primary key references auth.users on delete cascade, blocked_at timestamptz not null default now(), refusals integer not null, last_call_id uuid references public.ai_calls on delete set null)`: users whose AI photos are blocked after repeated refused prompts (6.11), one row each, until an admin clears it. Same access as `private.recipe_photo_ai` (RLS on, no policies, privileges revoked). Written only by the `after insert` trigger on `ai_calls` (`private.block_ai_photos_on_refusal`, `security definer`): `refusals` is the count that blocked them and `last_call_id` the refused row that did. `private.ai_photo_refusals(user_id, since)` counts a user's refused rows of the two tasks since `since` or their latest `unblock_ai_photos` audit row, whichever is later, the one place for what counts. `admin_unblock_ai_photos` (4.12) deletes the row. Deleting the user deletes the block; `admin.admin_actions.performed_by` is nullable for the automatic block's audit row.

**Unknown ingredients on insert**: clients can't write `ingredients` (RLS). A `before insert` trigger on `list_items` and `recipe_ingredients` (a `security definer` function) inserts a `pending` ingredient row if the name is missing (`on conflict do nothing`) and records the household in `ingredient_households`. So a list item can always be pushed, even before categorization has run.

**Ingredient history and Siri**: `ingredient_usage` and `ingredient_unit_usage` are written by the app in the same local transaction as the add. Siri adds are drained by the app (4.9) and counted the same way; once the `siri-add` Edge Function exists, the server will record the use for adds it applies first, and it syncs down like any other household row.

**Effective category** for an ingredient in a household (the household of the list or recipe it's on) = that household's `household_category_overrides.category_id` if the row exists and it isn't null, else `ingredients.default_category_id`, else "Sorting..." (pending) or Other (failed). Someone in two households has a separate set of overrides and custom aisles in each.

**Row-level security**
- `ingredients`, system `categories` (`household_id is null`): readable by any authenticated user; writable only by the service role and the insert trigger above.
- `ingredient_households`: no client access.
- Custom `categories` and `household_category_overrides`: household data, readable by members of their household only (`household_id in (select private.my_household_ids())`); written through `apply_mutations` (custom categories: insert with `household_id`, `name` and `created_at`, then `name`; overrides: `category_id` only, which must be a system category or a custom one of the same household).
- `household_category_order`: household data, readable by members of its household; written through `apply_mutations` (`category_ids` only; insert or update, no delete). The server rejects (`invalid aisle order`) anything but an array of distinct category ids, each a system category or a custom one of the same household.
- `user_household_category_order`: read/write/delete only where `user_id = auth.uid()` and `household_id in (select private.my_household_ids())`, written directly (upsert/delete), not through `apply_mutations`; the same `category_ids` check (a trigger). `private.remove_member_from_household` deletes the leaver's row.
- `user_settings`: read/write only where `user_id = auth.uid()`, written directly (upsert/delete), not through `apply_mutations`.
- `notification_settings` (4.13): select, insert and update only where `user_id = auth.uid()`, written directly (upsert), no delete.
- `push_tokens` (4.13): RLS on, no client grants. Two `security definer` functions for `authenticated` only: `register_push_token(token, platform)` checks the token's shape (`^(Exponent|Expo)PushToken\[[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\]$`, ≤ 200 chars; else `invalid token`) and the platform (`invalid platform`) and upserts the row for `auth.uid()`, bumping `updated_at`; a token held by another user moves to the caller (the device changed hands). `unregister_push_token(token)` deletes the caller's row, and is a no-op for someone else's or an unknown token. `private.push_recipients(household_id, actor, kind, at)` (`service_role` only; `kind` is `items_added` or `shopping`, else `invalid kind`) returns `(user_id, token, platform)`, one row per device, for the household's current members other than the actor who have that kind on and aren't in quiet hours (`[start, end)`) at `at` in their own `time_zone`; a missing settings row counts as the defaults.
- `device_credentials` (6.12): RLS on, no policies, no client grants. `register-device-credential` (service role) issues them; `revoke_my_device_credential(p_installation_id)` (`security definer`, `authenticated` only) revokes the caller's live credential for that installation (`revoked_reason` `sign_out`; a no-op for none); `admin_revoke_device_credentials` (4.12) revokes a user's. Deleting the user deletes them.
- `share_imports` (4.10, 6.12): RLS on, no policies, no client grants on the table. `my_share_imports` (granted `select` to `authenticated` only) is the client's only way in: the caller's own rows, without `page` or `extraction`. `share_import_status(p_ids uuid[])` (`authenticated`) returns `(id, status)` for the caller's own rows among `p_ids` (others' and unknown ids are left out, so they read as "not on the server"), and `retry_share_import(p_id)` (`authenticated`, the owner only; `not found` `P0002` otherwise) moves a `failed` row back to `received` with `attempts` 0, the same id and no new charge once `metered_at` is set. The worker's functions (`claim_share_import`, `reserve_share_import`, `save_share_extraction`, `release_share_import`, `commit_share_import`, 6.12) are `service_role` only. A daily pg_cron job, `share-imports-retention`, nulls `page` on `done` and `failed` rows and on any row older than 24 hours, nulls `extraction` 7 days after `done`, and deletes rows older than a year.
- The `send-notifications` worker's tables (4.13, 8.5), in `private` with RLS on and no client access: `push_sent (household_id, actor, list_id, kind, sent_at)`, one row per group sent (the shopping 2-hour window, and a log; rows older than 7 days are deleted daily by the `push-sent-retention` cron job), and `push_receipts (ticket_id, token, created_at)`, each ok Expo ticket until its receipt is checked (dropped after 24 hours). The worker reaches them, the queue and `private.push_recipients` through `public` functions executable by `service_role` only: `read_household_events(qty, vt)`, `archive_household_events(ids)`, `release_household_events(ids)`, `notification_group(kind, list_id, actor, ids)`, `notification_recipients(household_id, actor, kind, at)`, `record_push_sent`, `delete_push_tokens(tokens)`, `save_push_receipts(receipts)`, `due_push_receipts(min_age, limit)` and `delete_push_receipts(ticket_ids)`.
- Household tables: readable where `household_id` is one of the caller's households: `household_id in (select private.my_household_ids())`, where `private.my_household_ids()` is a `stable security definer` function returning the caller's memberships (wrapped in `select` so Postgres evaluates it once per query).
- `households`, `household_members`: members can read the households they belong to and those households' member lists (names and roles; needed for the Households screen). Changes go through `security definer` functions (`create_household`, `rename_household`, `set_combine_languages` (any member, 4.15), `create_invite`, `accept_invite`, `leave_household`, `remove_member`, `set_member_role`, `delete_household`, and `get_household_members` for reading; `reorder_households` is planned, not built; executable by `authenticated` only, not `anon`), which enforce the rules in 4.7 (at least one owner, at least one household per user, owner-only actions). No direct writes.
- `household_invites`: no direct access; `security definer` functions only, all for `authenticated`: `create_invite(household, email?)` (members; with an email, raises `already a member` for a member's email and revokes the open invite to the same email first), `preview_invite` (the join screen's household name, inviter and status: valid, member, used or expired; revoked and declined read as used), `accept_invite` (by link, whoever has it), `list_household_invites` (members; open, unexpired invites), `revoke_invite` (any member of the invite's household), `my_invites` (open, unexpired invites to the caller's email, case-insensitive, for households they aren't in), and `accept_invite_by_id` / `decline_invite` (only for an invite to the caller's email; anyone else gets `invite not found`, the same as an unknown id). "The caller's email" is their `auth.users.email`, and only once `email_confirmed_at` is set (not the token's email claim), so an unconfirmed password sign-up with someone else's address sees and can use none of their invites; Auth has "Confirm email" on.
- `copy_recipe(recipe_id, target_household_id)` (not built yet, 4.5): `security definer`; checks the caller is a member of both households.
- `delete_account()` (4.8): `security definer`, `search_path = ''`, executable by `authenticated` only (revoked from `public`, `anon` and `service_role`); acts only on `auth.uid()`. It runs `private.delete_user_core`, which, like the leaving rules, is callable only by `postgres` and the `security definer` functions that use it, and writes its `admin.admin_actions` row itself, so the caller never touches the `admin` schema.
- The `admin` schema (`admin_actions`, `delete_user`, 4.12): `postgres` (the dashboard) only. Not exposed by the API; usage on the schema, every privilege on `admin_actions` and execute on its functions are revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` (each function revokes its own execute: a per-schema default privilege can't take away Postgres's global PUBLIC execute). The leaving rules, `private.remove_member_from_household(household_id, user_id)`, return `left`, `promoted` or `deleted` and are callable only by `postgres` and the `security definer` functions that use them.
- `private.admins` (4.12): no client access, not even `service_role`. `is_admin()` (`stable security definer`, `search_path = ''`) is executable by `authenticated` only. `private.require_admin()` raises `not authorized` (`42501`) unless the caller is an admin; only `postgres` and the `security definer` `public.admin_*` portal functions that call it first can execute it.
- `plans`, `plan_features`, `household_plans`, `plan_limits`, `ai_safety_limits` (4.12): no client access (RLS on, no policies, privileges revoked from `anon` and `authenticated`); edited through the portal's `security definer` `admin_*` plan and safety-limit functions (4.12; executable by `authenticated` only, admins checked first), or unaudited in the Table Editor. Their helpers (`private.plan_snapshot`, `private.plan_household_count`, `private.log_plan_action`, `private.lock_plan`, `private.require_note`, `private.safety_limit`) and the triggers' functions can't be executed by any client role. `has_feature(user_id, feature, household_id)` is `stable security definer` in `public` so Edge Functions can call it with the service role (like `check_categorize_limit`); execute is revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`. `my_household_features()` returns the caller's features per household for `authenticated`; it's `security definer` with `search_path = ''`, since run as the caller it couldn't read the plan tables.
- Writes to household data go through `apply_mutations` (7.3), which enforces the same household check. Clients have `select` only on household tables. Its whitelist: `lists`, `recipes`, `list_items`, `list_item_contributions`, `recipe_ingredients`, `meal_plan_entries`, `categories` (custom; `household_id`, `name`, `created_at`), `household_category_overrides` (`category_id`), `household_category_order` (`category_ids`), `household_staples` (`deleted_at`), `recipe_favorites` (`deleted_at`; insert and update only for the caller's own `user_id`, with `household_id` the recipe's, else `not your favorite`), and `ingredient_usage` / `ingredient_unit_usage` (`use_count` as a counter; `last_used_at` as a max on unit usage).

---

## 6. Ingredient categorization and recipe extraction (req 4, 5, 5.1, 14)

### 6.1 Flow
1. Client adds an item. It normalizes the name and looks in its local ingredient cache.
2. **Hit**: use the cached category. Done.
3. **Miss**: the client creates the list item with `ingredient_name` set (it already has the key), shows "Sorting...", and keeps a local `pending` ingredient row. Categorize is on every plan (4.12), so there's no plan check on the device.
4. Right after the add, the client sends that add's new names in one request (6.3) to the `categorize-ingredients` Edge Function. A `rate_limited` or `daily_limit` answer (the user's safety limits, 4.12) leaves them pending, silently, for a later catch-up; a refused request costs nothing.
5. The function checks no plan feature. Per name: if the ingredient exists and is categorized, return it. Otherwise upsert a `pending` row (`on conflict do nothing`) and collect it into one LLM call. Concurrent requests for the same new name are safe: both upsert the same key; whichever finishes the LLM call first writes the result, and the other's write is skipped if the row is already `categorized`.
6. The response returns `normalized_name -> { status, category_id, is_grocery_item, default_unit, lang, same_as, linked_by }` for every name, plus `twins`: the translations' rows (6.4, 4.15) the request linked (and created, when new), each with its `display_name`. The client updates its cache, adds the twins it doesn't have, copies the languages and links, and moves the items into their sections. Other devices get the update through the catalog pull and Realtime on `ingredients` (7.2, 7.4); any device also learns it by sending its own `pending` names.
7. **Retries**: there is no server sweep. The client retries its local `pending` and `failed` names at launch, foreground and reconnect (the catch-up, 6.3). A server row left `pending` (the client went away, a provider error, the kill switch) is harmless: the next add of that name, by anyone, sends it to the LLM again.

"First time we see any ingredient, regardless of which user logged it" is enforced by step 5: the LLM is only called for names with no categorized row in the global table.

### 6.2 System categories [Default]
In default store-walk order:
Produce, Bakery, Deli, Meat & Seafood, Dairy & Eggs, Frozen, Pantry & Dry Goods, Canned & Jarred, Pasta & Grains, Baking, Spices & Seasonings, Condiments & Sauces, Snacks, Beverages, Breakfast & Cereal, Household & Cleaning, Personal Care, Baby, Pet, Other.

Seed the `ingredients` table with about 1,300 common grocery items pre-categorized (`categorized_by = 'seed'`, `is_grocery_item = true`, `household_count` high enough to pass the 6.5 filter) so most early adds never hit the LLM. Every seed item also has a default unit (4.2); `src/shared/seed/ingredients.ts` groups each category's names by it. The list is hand-curated, then checked against the categorizer: `yarn ai:check-seed` runs every seed name through the categorize prompt, saves the answers to `eval/results/seed-check.json` (in the repo, so paid LLM output survives a database reset), and lists every aisle or unit that differs; the seed follows the model's answer except where it calls a real item non-grocery. The golden-set names (6.4) are kept out of the seed so the eval keeps testing names the model hasn't seen.
