- Every mutation is a **field-level patch**: `{ table, id, fields: { name: value, ... }, hlc }`, appended to the local **outbox** in the same transaction as the local write.
- `hlc` is a hybrid logical clock timestamp (wall clock + counter + device id), so ordering reflects when the edit was made, not when it reached the server. The client keeps its clock monotonic and adopts any larger HLC it sees from the server.
  - Wire format: `'<wall clock ms, 13 digits>-<counter, 4 hex digits>-<device id>'` (device id: letters, digits, `_` or `-`, up to 64), e.g. `1760000000000-0000-3f9a…`. Fixed widths make plain text comparison order it by time, then counter, then device.
- The usage tables and `household_category_overrides` have composite keys, so their mutations name the row with `key` (`{ household_id, ingredient_name[, unit_key] }`) instead of `id`; the usage tables and send `use_count` changes as `counters: { use_count: <delta> }`, which add up across devices instead of last-writer-wins. `ingredient_unit_usage.last_used_at` merges as the later of both sides.
- `fields` may not include the row key, `updated_at`, `field_clock` or `merged_into` (server-owned). `household_id` and `created_at` take effect on insert only; a row can't move to another household.
- `recipes.added_by` is the server's: `apply_mutations` sets it to the caller on insert (with no field clock) and ignores a client value on insert and update, so the app never sends it. So are `list_items.added_by` and `list_item_contributions.added_by` (the caller, on insert) and `list_items.checked_by`, which follows `checked_at`: the caller when `checked_at` goes from null to set (an insert that's already checked included), null when it goes back to null, unchanged when a later check time replaces an earlier one, and clocked with `checked_at` whenever it changes, so a pull replaces the checker the device wrote (an Undo that re-checks with the member who had checked it shows that member until then). The app still writes and sends them; the server ignores the sent values, so the household's pushes (8) always name the member who did it. `tags`, `tags_edited` and `total_minutes` are last-writer-wins like the other recipe fields, the whole `tags` value as one field (4.14), and so is `step_ingredients` (4.5). `jsonb` values such as `tags` go over the wire as JSON, and pull compares them by content, not key order, since Postgres reorders `jsonb` keys.
- **Client side** (`src/sync/`): `hlc.ts` is the pure clock; `clock.ts` keeps the device id and last HLC in device settings, written in the same transaction as each write, so the clock stays monotonic across launches. Every write to household data (households, lists, items, contributions, recipes, recipe ingredients, usage, custom categories, category overrides) and to the per-user tables (category order, settings) goes through `mutate.ts` (`insertRow`, `updateRows`, `upsertRow`, `deleteRows`), which writes the row, its local `field_clock` (each field's HLC from its last write) and one outbox mutation per row in one transaction. An insert sends every column but the key, counters, server-owned ones and ones the server doesn't take from clients (a custom category's `slug` and `default_rank`, an override's `created_at`); an update sends exactly the fields it set. The local `outbox` table keeps each mutation in wire shape plus its `household_id` (null for per-user rows), so a lost membership can drop its mutations (7.2).
  - Custom categories (`categories` with a `household_id`: insert with `household_id`, `name` and `created_at`, then renames) and `household_category_overrides` (`category_id` only; Reset to default sends `category_id: null` rather than deleting) are household data and go through `apply_mutations` with field-level LWW, like lists.
  - `recipe_favorites` (4.14) is household data keyed by `{ recipe_id, user_id }`, sent with `household_id` on insert and `deleted_at` as its one field, like `household_staples`; pulled per household, so every member's device gets everyone's favorites.
  - `household_category_order` (4.3.3) is keyed by `household_id` (`key: { household_id }`) and sends `category_ids` as one field, so the save with the later HLC wins whole, the way `tags` does; the server sets `updated_by`.
  - Per-user tables aren't in `apply_mutations`; their mutations use the same shape (`key: { user_id, ... }`), and `user_household_category_order` (`key: { user_id, household_id }`), which hard-deletes, sends `delete: true` for Use household order. A save and a delete for the same order still queued are coalesced into the latest one before push, so an older queued save can never bring back an order the user removed. `households` isn't in `apply_mutations` either (it changes through the household functions, 4.7). Push (`src/sync/push.ts`) sends these one at a time in their place in the queue: per-user rows as direct writes (upsert by key, or delete; RLS keeps them to the user's own rows, so last push wins rather than HLC), and a new household through `create_household`. Other household changes aren't synced yet and are recorded as rejected.
  - `list_items.photo_path` (4.4, local migration `0023_item_photo_path`) is synced like `note`: pushed and pulled, merged per field. Only the path syncs; the photo itself is in the `item-photos` bucket.
  - `recipes.source_pages` (4.10, local migration `0024_recipe_source_pages`, JSON text) is synced like `photo_path`: pushed and pulled, merged per field, the whole array as one field. Only the paths sync; the pages are in the `recipe-pages` bucket.
  - Moving to household aisles (local migration `0011_household_aisles`) kept no aisle data: per-user overrides, custom aisles, the order rows that pointed at them, and their queued outbox mutations (the new server would reject them forever) were dropped on the device, as on the server.
  - Moving to aisle orders per household (4.3.3; local migrations `0026_household_aisle_order`, which adds both order tables and custom aisles' `created_at`, and `0027_drop_tmp`, drizzle-kit's name for the drop) kept no order data: `user_category_order` and all its queued outbox mutations, rejected ones too, were dropped on the device, as on the server. Push still refuses a queued `user_category_order` change locally (`retired`) as a fallback.
  - A usage row whose `use_count` reaches 0 is deleted locally only; the decrement goes out as a counter delta, so the server keeps the row at 0, and a row at 0 or below reads as absent.
  - Not synced up: the catalog (`ingredients`, system categories) and device settings. The recipes list's "recently changed" sort bumps the local `updated_at` only.

### 7.2 Push and pull
- **Push**: the outbox worker sends batches to the `apply_mutations` Postgres function in order, retrying with stash's backoff schedule. Mutations are idempotent (keyed by row id + field + hlc; counters by row + hlc). Each mutation is applied on its own and gets one result, in order: `applied`, `unchanged` (nothing newer, e.g. a re-send), `merged` (with `merged_into`, 7.3), or `rejected` with a SQLSTATE `code` and `message` (`42501` not a member, `22023` malformed, or a constraint error). A rejected mutation never blocks the rest of the batch. Clients have no direct insert/update/delete on household tables.
- **The server as a writer.** A share import (6.12) writes household data the way a device does: each write is a mutation applied by `private.apply_mutation(mutation, uid)` (the per-row function `apply_mutations` wraps), with the sharer as `uid`, so the membership check, field LWW, soft deletes, counter dedupe, `added_by` / `created_by` and the open-item merge (7.3) all apply. `commit_share_import` calls it directly, with no per-mutation subtransaction, so one rejection rolls the whole import back. Its HLCs use the device id `srv`: the wall time is the larger of `clock_timestamp()` in ms and one more than the largest `field_clock` wall among the rows it updates, and the counter is the write's index. So a server update (a reopen's `checked_at = null`, a soft-deleted contribution) beats clocks that are ahead from device skew, as a device's HLC would after seeing them, and devices observe `srv` clocks on pull like any other. **Nothing changes in pull**: the rows land in the normal tables with normal `field_clock`, `updated_at` and soft deletes, and Realtime cues every household device to pull them. Because a late-committing transaction can land behind a moved cursor, a server write transaction must stay well under pull's 5 s overlap: no fetching or model work inside it.
- **Pull**: Supabase Realtime on the tables of every household the user belongs to, the user's own preference tables, the user's `household_members` rows, and `ingredients` (for categorization results) while in the foreground. On launch, foreground, or reconnect, pull every row with `updated_at > cursor` (soft-deleted rows included).
- **Cursors are per household**, not global. A household the user just joined has rows older than any existing cursor, so joining starts that household's cursor at zero (a full pull for that household only).
  - **Client side** (`src/sync/pull.ts`): the households pulled are the user's `household_members` rows on the server. Within a household each table keeps its own cursor (the server `updated_at` text of the newest row applied); a household's custom categories and overrides are household tables (categories first, then overrides, before its lists), filtered by `household_id`. The user's preference tables (category order, settings) and the catalog (system categories, `household_id is null`, then `ingredients`) have their own cursors. Tables are read parents first, oldest change first, in pages; rows sharing a page's last `updated_at` are fetched together, and each table's first page reaches 5 s behind its cursor in case an earlier transaction committed late (re-applying a row is a no-op). A row SQLite refuses (its parent arrived after its table was read) stops that table with the cursor before it, so the next pull retries it.
  - Rows apply with the 7.3 rule against the local `field_clock`: a remote field wins unless the local clock for it is newer (a local edit the server hasn't seen). Fields the server keeps no clock for (per-user tables, `households`) win unless an unpushed outbox mutation sets them, and a row with an unpushed local delete isn't brought back. `use_count` is the server's total plus this device's unpushed deltas (0 or less removes the local row), and `ingredient_unit_usage.last_used_at` is the later of both sides. Local `updated_at` becomes the later of local and server. Pull writes never go to the outbox. Every pulled HLC is observed, so later local writes beat it.
  - A remote open item that clashes with a different local open item for the same ingredient on the same list Combines silently (4.4): the local item is soft-deleted and its contributions move to the remote one, as `apply_mutations` will do when the local one is pushed. So does a local open item of another name of its family (4.15) that this device opened (added, unchecked or restored) and hasn't pushed, since that's when `apply_mutations` merges across a family; one already pushed stays, as on the server.
  - Catalog rows only update ingredients the device already has (7.4), and a non-final server status never replaces a categorized one. Pulled names the device doesn't know are added as pending, for categorization (6.3).
  - Not covered by pull: per-user rows deleted on another device (the server hard-deletes them, so there's nothing to pull; Realtime delete events cover them, below), and a `user_settings.default_list_id` pointing at a list this device doesn't have, which keeps the local value.
  - **Realtime** (`src/sync/realtime.ts`, migration `realtime.sql` adds the tables to the `supabase_realtime` publication): while signed in, in the foreground and online, the app keeps one channel per household (every household table filtered to it), one for the user's own rows (`household_members`, category order, settings, filtered to the user) and one for `ingredients`. A household's custom categories and overrides are on its channel, filtered by `household_id`, so one member's aisle change reaches the others. An event doesn't write to SQLite: it schedules a cursor pull of just what it touched (that household, the user's tables, or the catalog), so remote changes take the pull path above, and the screens' live queries show them like local writes (a remote check moves the row to Checked with the same animation). Events within 300 ms share one pull. Every channel also pulls its scope once it's subscribed, which covers changes made while it was joining or rejoining. A `household_members` event for the user does a full pull, which reads memberships again; channels are opened and closed to match. Deletes can't be filtered, so delete events for other users' rows arrive and are ignored. The user's own deleted order rows are applied from the event's key (the default replica identity sends the primary key), unless this device has an unpushed change to that row. Background or offline closes every channel; foreground reopens them and a reconnect opens new ones.
  - **List presence** (`src/features/lists/presence.ts`, migration `list_presence.sql`; "Alex is adding", 4.1): separate from the sync channels, whose topics are per client, each list on screen has one private presence channel, topic `list:<listId>`, the same for every member. It carries only presence, `{ user_id, adding }` keyed by user, never any list data or typed text. Realtime authorizes private channels with RLS on `realtime.messages`: a select policy lets the caller join and receive presence, an insert policy lets them track it, both only for `extension = 'presence'` on a `list:` topic whose list is in one of the caller's households (`private.can_use_list_topic(realtime.topic())`). Nothing else (broadcast, other topics) is allowed. A join waits for the previous leave of the same topic to finish, since Realtime hands back an existing channel for a topic; after a rejoin the device tracks again.
- **Membership changes** drive the rest: a new membership subscribes and pulls that household; a lost membership (left, removed, household deleted) unsubscribes, deletes that household's rows from SQLite, and drops its pending outbox mutations (4.7). The server rejects mutations for households the user no longer belongs to; the client treats that rejection as "drop", not "retry".
  - **Client side** (`src/sync/purge.ts`, called by the full pull): the pull keeps the household list the server last gave it. After pulling the households still listed, any household in the previous list and not in this one is purged in one transaction: its rows in every household table (its overrides, custom aisles and aisle order included, with the user's own aisle order for it and that order's queued mutations, rejected ones too), all its outbox mutations (rejected ones too), its pull cursors (so joining again pulls it in full), the per-list view-mode and last-opened-list device settings that point into it, and a Siri default list in it (set to null locally, as the server's `on delete set null` does). Pull writes, so nothing goes to the outbox. A household the server has never listed (e.g. one created on this device and not pushed yet) is never purged, and an empty list (everyone has at least one household) is treated as a bad answer: nothing is purged. Realtime closes that household's channel when the pull reports the new list, and a new membership opens one and is pulled in full (above).

### 7.3 Conflicts
- **Field-level last-write-wins by HLC**: `apply_mutations` writes a field only if the incoming HLC is newer than `field_clock[field]`, then updates `field_clock`. So A checking an item while B edits its note keeps both changes.
- **Delete wins** over concurrent edits to the same row (a `deleted_at` set at any HLC is not undone by a field edit; an explicit undo is a new mutation that clears it).
- **Same ingredient added twice offline**: the unique index rejects the second open item. `apply_mutations` handles this inside the function: it re-points the incoming item's contributions to the existing open item and marks the incoming item as merged (an automatic Combine): soft-deleted, with `list_items.merged_into` set to the open item. Contributions that arrive later for a merged item follow `merged_into`, and an edit that would reopen a merged item merges it again. An item that opens (is inserted, unchecked or restored) beside an open item of another name of its family (4.15: `pepino` beside `cucumber`) is merged into that one the same way (the oldest, if more); twins already both open, from before their link, aren't merged by an edit. The client sees the merge on its next pull and drops its local row. This never fails the batch.
- **Per-list lock for open items.** The merge above decides from what the transaction can see, so two transactions opening an item for one key at once each miss the other: for the same name the unique index (`one_open_item_per_ingredient`) then fails the later one instead of merging it, and for two names of one family (which no index covers) both stay open. `apply_mutation` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('list:' || list_id, 0))` before deciding whether a `list_items` write leaves a second open item for its ingredient or family, and `commit_share_import` (6.12) takes the same lock before checking its preconditions, so device pushes and server imports on one list decide one at a time. It's backwards compatible: no client change.
- Contributions are append-only in practice (each add creates a new row), so they rarely conflict.
- **Activity log**: `apply_mutations` writes are logged by the table triggers (4.12 User activity), as `auth.uid()`, like any other write; it sets nothing itself. A server writer has no `auth.uid()`, so it sets the transaction-local `app.actor_uid` to the user it acts for (`commit_share_import`: the sharer), and the triggers log as `coalesce(auth.uid(), app.actor_uid)`. A mutation that changes nothing (`unchanged`) writes no row and logs nothing; a merge logs `item.merged`.

### 7.4 Ingredient catalog on device
The device caches the seed set plus every ingredient it has seen, and fetches unknown names on demand. Category updates arrive via Realtime or pull. The local catalog mirrors each row's `lang`, `same_as` and `linked_by` (4.15): the bundled seeds fill them, a categorize answer brings its names' and adds the twins it linked (6.1), and pull copies the server's, a cleared link included.

### 7.5 Sync status
Settings > "Sync status" (as stash): pending outbox count, last successful sync, last error, and a "Sync now" button.
- **Client side**: `src/sync/status.ts` `syncStatus(inputs)` is the one rule, from the outbox counts (pending and rejected), the push and pull states (`lastSuccessAt`, `lastError`) and what's running (`src/sync/activity.ts`, reported by push and pull, with the OS's connectivity). In order: signed out → "Not syncing"; offline → "Offline"; a push or pull running → "Syncing…"; a standing push or pull error → "Sync error" (push's error shown first, as "Sending: …"; pull's as "Receiving: …"); pending mutations → "Syncing…"; nothing ever got through → "Not synced yet"; else "Synced". Last successful sync is the later of the last push and pull that got through. Rejected mutations are counted on the screen ("Not accepted") but don't block "Synced", since they never go. The account card's badge and the Settings row show the same label. Sync now pushes (ignoring the backoff), then does a full pull; it's disabled while offline or while either is running.

---

## 8. Architecture

### 8.1 Stack [Default]
| Layer | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| App | Expo (same SDK as stash, 57) + Expo Router, TypeScript | Web-developer friendly; same stack as stash |
| Local store | expo-sqlite + Drizzle | Offline-first; same as stash |
| Native | Swift App Intents extension via `@bacons/apple-targets` | Only for Siri; proven in stash |
| Backend | Supabase (Postgres, Auth, Realtime, Edge Functions, pg_cron) | Global catalog, sharing, and the LLM proxy need a server |
| Auth | Google via Supabase Auth; dev email-code stub until OAuth IDs exist | Same as stash |
| LLM | Vercel AI SDK in an Edge Function; the model is set per task by env var (`AI_MODEL_CATEGORIZE` etc.), currently Gemini 3.8 Flash (`google:gemini-3.8-flash`); Anthropic is a registered alternative | Key stays server-side; provider is config |
| UI and i18n | `@expo/ui`, `expo-glass-effect`, `expo-image`, `expo-symbols`; `i18next` + `react-i18next` (4.15) | Native controls; strings in one place |
| Crash reporting | Sentry's free Developer plan. App: `@sentry/react-native`, errors from release builds only, no tracing or replays, the user tagged by id alone. Edge Functions: `@sentry/deno` (`_shared/sentry.ts`), every 500 with the function's name, only when `SENTRY_DSN` is set | Find crashes in production without collecting list contents |
| Other native | `expo-mail-composer`, `expo-sms` (invites), `react-native-webview` (recipe page capture, 4.10), `expo-keep-awake`, `expo-haptics` | |
| Shared code | Zod schemas, normalizer, quantity parser in `src/shared/`, imported by app and Edge Functions | One implementation of the ingredient key |

### 8.2 App structure
Follows stash's layout (Expo Router routes in `src/app/`, app logic in sibling folders). Folder-level only; the files change too often to list.

```
src/
  app/              routes. Tabs: (tabs)/lists, (tabs)/recipes (+ filter), (tabs)/plan, (tabs)/settings
                    (households/[id], sync, siri, notifications, language, always-have, aisle-colors, developer).
                    Stack and sheets: list/[id] (edit, add-to-recipe, item/[itemId]), recipe/[id]
                    (add-to-list, cook, steps, tags), lists-sheet, add-recipe, import-recipe,
                    category-order, household-invite, plan-slot, invite/[token], sign-in
  features/
    lists/          the List tab's screen, add bar and chips, rows, view modes, suggestions, usage
    recipes/        the Recipes tab, recipe screen parts, search/filter/sort, import and share reconciliation (4.10)
    meal-plan/      the week screen (grid, agenda, toolbar), queries and writes (4.16)
    categories/     categorization client and catch-up, aisle order, accent folding
    households/     households, invites, staples
    siri/           App Group access, queue drain, list snapshot (writes lists.json)
    notifications/  in-app banners (4.11); per-kind content builders live with their feature
    plans/          feature checks (4.12)
    push/           push registration, taps and foreground pushes, Settings > Notifications, ask once (4.13)
    settings/       device settings, language, sync status, your name
    auth/           sign-in, sign-out, delete account, local wipe
  domain/           pure TS, no React/IO: duplicate detector, quantity math and display, category
                    resolver, suggestion rank, kitchen rounding, usual unit
  shared/           used by app AND Edge Functions: zod schemas (siri, share, recipe import, tags),
                    ingredient normalizer, quantity parser, recipe-jsonld, display name, seeds
  db/               Drizzle schema mirroring section 5 plus local-only tables, migrations
  sync/             hlc, outbox writes (mutate), push (apply_mutations), pull, realtime, purge, status
  lib/              supabase client, google sign-in, ids
  i18n/             strings (4.15): en.ts (the source), es.ts (exactly en's keys), languages, useT,
                    formatters, the strings review export
targets/
  _shared/          SiriQueue.swift, ShareQueue.swift (App Group queue writers, Codable envelopes)
  add-intent/       AddToPerfectBiteIntent.swift (with ListEntity)
  share-recipe/     share extension (4.10): SwiftUI sheet, Preprocess.js
supabase/
  migrations/       schema, RLS, triggers, apply_mutations, household functions, seeds, rate limits
  functions/        categorize-ingredients/, import-recipe/, suggest-recipe-tags/, send-notifications/
                    (push worker, 8.5), register-device-credential/, share-ingest/, share-import-worker/
                    (6.12, 8.6), _shared/ (AI tasks and registry, fetch-page). Account deletion
                    is the delete_account SQL function.
  tests/            pgTAP, one file per area (RLS, apply_mutations, invites, plans, meal plan, ...)
```

Business logic in `domain/` and `shared/` must be pure TypeScript with no React, network, or SQLite dependencies, so it is unit-testable and runs in Deno.

### 8.3 Local development
Identical to stash. Copy its README sections, `mise.toml`, `.yarnrc.yml`, `.env.example`, `supabase/functions/.env.example`, `supabase/config.toml` (new `project_id`, ports moved to 553xx so stash and this project can run side by side), and `AGENTS.md`, then adapt.

**Prerequisites**: Node, Yarn 4 (pinned in `mise.toml` and `packageManager`), Docker (OrbStack works), the Supabase CLI, Xcode with CocoaPods.

**One-time setup**
```bash
yarn install
supabase start            # local Postgres, Auth, Realtime, Studio (http://127.0.0.1:55323)
supabase db reset         # apply migrations + seed.sql
cp .env.example .env.local                                   # publishable key from `supabase status`
cp supabase/functions/.env.example supabase/functions/.env   # GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY etc.
```

**Each session** (separate terminals)
```bash
supabase start                                               # no-op if running
supabase functions serve --env-file supabase/functions/.env  # Edge Functions, hot reload
yarn expo start                                              # Metro
```
As in stash, don't rely on the edge runtime that `supabase start` launches; use `functions serve`. A `503` from `http://127.0.0.1:55321/functions/v1/<name>` means it isn't running.

**Dev sign-in**: dev builds can sign in with the seeded dev user's password (`DEV_PASSWORD_EMAIL` / `DEV_PASSWORD` in `src/features/auth/account.ts`, matching `seed.sql`), or with email + 6-digit code, which local Supabase delivers to the local mail viewer (`http://127.0.0.1:55324`). Google sign-in needs the `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_*` client ids in `.env.local`.

**Development build**: Siri (the intent extension) needs a development build, not Expo Go: `yarn expo run:ios` (simulator) or `yarn expo run:ios --device`. On a physical device, set `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` to the Mac's LAN IP. Device signing steps: copy stash's checklist from its `docs/spikes.md` (including the one-time `xcodebuild ... -allowProvisioningUpdates` build).

**Database changes**: `supabase migration new <name>`, write SQL, `supabase db reset`. Tests in `supabase/tests/` (pgTAP) run with `supabase test db`.

**Local categorization**: `categorize-ingredients` runs locally against Gemini 3.8 Flash (`google:gemini-3.8-flash`) using `GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY` in `supabase/functions/.env`. Set `AI_MODEL_CATEGORIZE=mock` to use a deterministic fake (keyword rules) with no key and no cost; tests always use the mock.

**Seed data**:
- System categories and the seed ingredients (about 1,300 English and 320 Spanish, with their default units and links) come from migrations, so they exist locally and hosted alike.
- `supabase/seed.sql` (local only) adds one dev user with a password, a household they own with a "Groceries" list as their default, and makes **unlimited** the default plan, so households made locally get every feature (4.12), and so `supabase db reset` doesn't sign you out for good.
- To test sharing, sign a second simulator or device in as another user (code flow) and invite them. A seeded second user and shared household would make that quicker, but don't exist yet.

**Checks** (before declaring any task done, as in stash's `AGENTS.md`): `yarn expo lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn jest`, `supabase test db`, `yarn dlx expo-doctor`.

### 8.4 Reuse from stash (copy, don't share)
The two apps are independent; there are no shared packages. Copy what's useful from `../stash` into this repo and adapt it freely:
- `src/sync/` outbox, drain, backoff, upload (extend with HLC and field patches).
- `targets/save-intent/` and `src/features/capture/app-group-queue.ts` (Siri intent and queue drain).
- `src/features/auth/` dev sign-in stub and account deletion.
- Edge Function `_shared/` (AI SDK provider registry, auth helpers), Supabase test setup, `AGENTS.md`, lint/tsconfig/jest config.
- `DESIGN.md` structure (tokens), with this app's own visual design.

### 8.5 Household events queue (4.13)
pgmq queue `household_events` (RLS on the queue and archive tables, no policies; only `postgres`/`service_role` touch it), filled by three `security definer` triggers in `private` (execute revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated`). They fire for every write path, `apply_mutations` or not, and never fail the write: a failed `pgmq.send` raises a warning.
- `private.queue_items_added`, after insert on `list_item_contributions`, when `added_by` is set and `deleted_at` is null: `{ kind: 'items_added', household_id, list_id (from the item), actor: added_by, contribution_id, list_item_id, recipe_id, source, at: created_at }`. A recipe add queues one event per ingredient. A merge in `apply_mutations` (7.3) re-points contributions with an update, so it queues nothing more; undoing an add (an update of `deleted_at`) queues nothing either.
- `private.queue_shopping`, after update of `checked_at` on `list_items`, when it goes from null to set and `checked_by` is set: `{ kind: 'shopping', household_id, list_id, actor: checked_by, list_item_id, at: checked_at }`. Re-checks, unchecks and items created already checked queue nothing.
- **The worker.** Edge Function `send-notifications` (`index.ts` wiring, `handler.ts` logic, `store.ts` database, `expo.ts` Expo Push client). pg_cron runs `private.request_send_notifications()` every minute, which calls it through `pg_net` with the Vault secrets `project_url` and `notifications_worker_key` (a secret API key, sent as `apikey`; anyone else gets 401), and only when there are events queued or receipts to check. Without the secrets it does nothing; seeding them locally is in `AGENTS.md`. Each run:
  - reads up to 500 events with a 2-minute visibility timeout and drops (archives) unusable ones and stale ones (`at` more than 15 minutes before the worker's clock);
  - groups the rest per (household, actor, list, kind). A group that isn't ready (4.13) is released (visible again at once) so the next run reads all its events together; a ready one is re-read (`notification_group`: the list's name, the actor's short name, the live rows, the last push for this actor, list and kind) and archived after it's sent, or dropped when nothing is left, the list is gone, it's in the shopping window or nobody is left to tell (`notification_recipients`). A group whose send fails is left to reappear after the visibility timeout and is tried again until it goes stale;
  - sends `{ to, title: list name, body, data: { url: '/list/<id>', kind, householdId }, sound: 'default', threadId: list id }` per recipient device to `https://exp.host/--/api/v2/push/send` in chunks of 100, with `Authorization: Bearer $EXPO_ACCESS_TOKEN` when that secret is set, retrying 429, 5xx and network errors up to 3 tries (1 s, then 2 s apart). A ticket error `DeviceNotRegistered` deletes that token; ok tickets are kept in `push_receipts`;
  - checks receipts at least 15 minutes old through `/--/api/v2/push/getReceipts` (up to 1,000), deletes tokens whose receipt says `DeviceNotRegistered`, and forgets the checked receipts.
  Bodies: "Alex added milk, eggs and 4 more" (up to two item names, items counted once even when a Combine added twice), "Alex added Tacos (8 items)" (every live contribution from one recipe; counted per contribution), "Alex is shopping"; names go through as written, with no markup; a missing name reads "Someone".
- **Recipe-import events** (4.13), on `household_events` itself (no sibling queue, so the worker, its cron check and its archive stay one). `private.queue_recipe_import`, after update of `status` on `share_imports`, when it becomes `done` (`commit_share_import`) or `failed` (`release_share_import`, or `claim_share_import` failing a job with `lease_lost`), in the same transaction (a rolled-back commit queues nothing): `{ kind: 'recipe_import', share_import_id, user_id, household_id, status: 'done' | 'failed', at: now() }`. The worker sends each at once, not grouped, after the same staleness check: it re-reads the job (`recipe_import_notification(id)`: status, recipe name, applied list's name, household name, `list_gone`, `error_code`, `resets_at`) and drops the event when the job is gone or its status has moved on (a Try again), then sends to every push token of `user_id` only (`recipe_import_recipients(user_id, at)`: `recipe_imports` on and not in quiet hours; otherwise the event is archived unsent), with `data: { url: '/recipe/<id>' | '/recipes', kind: 'recipe_import', status, shareImportId }` and no `threadId`. Titles "*Lemon pasta* is ready" / "Couldn't import that recipe"; bodies as 4.13, the reason by `error_code` in 4.10's words (`not_a_recipe`, `invalid_url`, `not_entitled`, `not_a_member`, `daily_limit`, `limit_reached` with the UTC date of `resets_at`, `fetch_failed`; anything else "Something went wrong."). The household "added" events of the same commit are ordinary `items_added` events with the sharer as actor.

### 8.6 Share import worker (6.12)
pg_cron runs `private.request_share_import_worker()` every minute (job `share-import-worker`), which calls the `share-import-worker` Edge Function through `pg_net` with the Vault secrets `project_url` and `share_import_worker_key` (a secret API key, sent as `apikey`; anyone else gets 401), and only when a `share_imports` row is due (`received` or `extracted` with `next_attempt_at` passed, or a lease expired). Without the secrets it does nothing; seeding them locally is in `AGENTS.md`, and on hosted they're added by hand, like the other workers' secrets. `share-ingest`'s `waitUntil` start (6.12) only saves up to a minute; this job is what recovers crashed or slow runs, since every claim is leased. A second daily job, `share-imports-retention`, purges pages and extractions and old rows (5).

---

## 9. Testing and acceptance criteria

Unit tests (Jest) required for: ingredient normalizer, quantity parser (including number words), quantity math and display rule, duplicate detector, category resolver, the categorize-on-add client (one request per add; catch-up on launch, foreground and reconnect), HLC. Edge Function tests with a mocked LLM. SQL tests for RLS (one household can't read another's data; one household can't read another's overrides or custom aisles) and for `apply_mutations` (field-level LWW, delete wins, offline duplicate merge). A Swift/Zod contract test for the Siri envelope.

Acceptance criteria:
1. **Add**: typing `2 lbs chicken thighs` and return creates one item with quantity 2, unit lb, under Meat & Seafood (seed data), with the keyboard still up.
2. **Recipe from recipe**: creating "Tacos" with 5 ingredients and adding it to a list with one ingredient unchecked adds 4 items, all showing under Tacos in By recipe view.
3. **Recipe from selection**: selecting 3 list items and Add to recipe → New recipe creates a recipe with those 3 ingredients, and those items now group under it.
4. **Auto-categorize**: adding an ingredient that exists in the catalog categorizes instantly offline.
5. **First-seen LLM**: adding a never-seen ingredient shows "Sorting..." then moves to a section; the `ingredients` row exists with `categorized_by` set; a second user adding it later triggers no LLM call (verify via function logs).
6. **Batching**: adding a 12-ingredient recipe with 8 unknown ingredients makes exactly one function request and one LLM call.
7. **Override**: user A moves "tahini" to Pantry; user B in the same household sees it in Pantry on every list and device; a user in another household still sees the default.
8. **Shared household**: A invites B's email to a shared household ("Test home" below) and sends nothing; when B opens the app (signed in with that email) the list switcher shows an invite dot and the Your lists sheet has A's invite, A's household screen lists B's email under Invited, and after B taps Join the invite goes, A's Invited section is empty on its next load, and B sees its lists in a new section of the sheet next to B's personal household; an item checked by A appears checked on B's device within a few seconds while both are online.
9. **Duplicates**: adding `milk` to a list that has `1 gal milk` shows no prompt, yields one row whose total counts the new add as one, and shows the toast with the keyboard still up; with no unit history for milk the toast reads "Added to milk (now 1 gal + 1)", and when milk is usually bought by the gallon it reads "Added to milk (now 2 gal)", with a single Undo, which restores the original row. Adding a recipe with 3 ingredients already open on the list asks nothing: all 3 Combine, one toast "Added Tacos · N items" appears, and its Undo restores the list exactly.
10. **Category order**: user A drags Dairy above Produce; A's lists show Dairy first on all of A's devices; B's order is unchanged.
11. **Multiple lists**: create, rename, reorder and delete lists from the Your lists sheet; picking one switches the List tab to it, deleting the current list moves the tab to the next; items are independent per list.
12. **View modes**: switching to By recipe shows recipe sections plus Other items; an item from two recipes appears under both, and checking it in one checks it in both.
13. **Offline**: in airplane mode, add/check/delete items and reorder categories; on reconnect everything syncs with no duplicates.
14. **Concurrent offline edits**: A (offline) checks "eggs" while B (online) edits its note; after A reconnects, the item is checked and has B's note.
15. **Offline duplicate merge**: A and B both add `milk` to the same list while offline; after both reconnect, there is one row with both contributions.
16. **Siri**: with the phone locked, "Hey Siri, add to The Perfect Bite" then "two avocados" replies within ~3 s; the item appears on B's device in the default list under Produce without A opening the app (when A is online; needs `siri-add`, not built yet; today it appears once A opens the app).
17. **Siri, named list and offline**: "add to Costco in The Perfect Bite" adds to Costco; in airplane mode the add is kept and appears after reconnect and app launch, exactly once.
18. **Multiple households**: a user in two households sees both sections on Lists and Recipes; a user in one household sees no household UI.
19. **Household owns data**: B leaves "Test home"; within one sync, its lists disappear from B's devices and B's queued edits for it are dropped; A still has all of it. B's personal household is untouched.
20. **Last member**: the last member leaving a household deletes it (after confirm); a user leaving their only household gets a new empty personal household.
21. **Copy recipe** (not built yet): copying "Tacos" to another household creates an independent recipe there; editing one doesn't change the other.
22. **Isolation**: SQL tests prove a user can't read or write any row of a household they aren't in, including right after leaving or removal, and can't perform owner-only actions as a member.

---

## 10. Build order (milestones for Claude Code)

Each milestone should end with a working app and passing tests.

1. **M0 Skeleton + spikes**: Expo app (default template, stash's config copied per 8.4), tabs for Lists / Recipes / Settings, SQLite + Drizzle, Supabase project with migrations folder, CI running lint, typecheck, and tests. Dev sign-in stub. Spikes: Siri phrase recognition for "The Perfect Bite" (4.9), token refresh from the intent extension (4.9), sync engine evaluation (7.0). Record results in `docs/spikes.md`.
2. **M1 Local lists**: lists and items fully local. Add bar with quantity parser and normalizer, check off, delete with undo, multiple lists.
3. **M2 Categories local**: seed categories and seed ingredients bundled in the app, By category view, per-user overrides and category order stored locally.
4. **M3 Duplicates**: duplicate detector, quantity math, contributions model, single and bulk prompts.
5. **M4 Recipes**: recipe CRUD, add recipe to list, make recipe from selection, By recipe view.
6. **M5 Backend and sync**: Supabase schema + RLS + triggers, `apply_mutations`, outbox with HLC, push/pull, Realtime, sync status screen. (Or the chosen sync engine, per 7.0.)
   - *Landed early* for the read-only Households screen: `households`, `household_members` (with RLS, `private.my_household_ids()` and household-mates' profiles readable), `create_household` and `get_household_members`. The household is created locally through the outbox (`ensureStarterData`), and the push sends it with `create_household` like other household data (7.1); the launch-time `registerHouseholds` that did this before sync is gone.
   - *Also landed early*, so the data can be browsed in Studio: the rest of the section 5 schema (catalog with seed, per-user preferences, household data, `user_settings`), with RLS and the unknown-ingredient trigger (`household_invites` came later, with invites; see M7). M5 moved household writes behind `apply_mutations`, and sync replaced the dev-only "Push local data to Supabase" snapshot, which is gone.
7. **M6 Categorization**: `categorize-ingredients` Edge Function with the AI SDK, categorize-on-add client with catch-up, "Sorting..." state, retries, golden-set eval.
   - *Landed*: the function (6.1, 6.4) with its LLM boundaries, rate limits, global daily cap and `off` kill switch; `ai_calls`; Gemini 3.8 Flash with thinking `low` (`docs/spikes.md` §4); the categorize-on-add client and catch-up (6.3); "Sorting..."; failed names retried up to 3 attempts; default units (prompt categorize.v2, since v3); the seed (about 1,300 English names, later joined by about 320 Spanish), checked with `yarn ai:check-seed`; the golden-set eval (99/100 on categorize.v2 when it landed).
8. **M7 Households**: multiple memberships, household sections in Lists/Recipes, Households settings, invites via Universal Links, join/leave/remove/roles/delete, per-household sync cursors and local purge on lost access, copy recipe. **Done** except Universal Links (`future/universal-links-tasks.md`; the app scheme is used), copy recipe (`future/copy-to-household-tasks.md`) and household reordering (`future/wishlist.md`). (The schema and RLS for multiple households land in M5; M7 is the UI and flows.)
   - *Landed early*: a read-only Households settings screen (Settings > Households lists local households; tapping one shows its name and members with Owner/Member and "You", fetched online via `get_household_members`). It uses `households`, `household_members`, `create_household` and `get_household_members`, pulled forward from M5/M7 (see M5). Rename, leave, remove and roles are still M7. *Also landed early (with M5)*: invites: `household_invites` with `create_invite` / `preview_invite` / `accept_invite`, an "Invite someone" button on the household screen and the join screen (`src/app/invite/[token].tsx`), using the app scheme until Universal Links have a domain. *Email invites* (designs S1–S4, R2b): invites can name an email; the two-step invite sheet, the Invited section on the household screen and invite cards on the Lists home, since moved to the Your lists sheet by M11 (`my_invites`, `accept_invite_by_id`, `decline_invite`).
9. **M8 Siri**: intent extension, `ListEntity` from the list snapshot, App Group queue + drain, `siri-add` Edge Function (categorizes unknown names inline by calling the shared `categorize()`), default list setting, onboarding tip ("Try: Hey Siri, add to The Perfect Bite"). **Done** except the `siri-add` Edge Function and the extension's upload through the shared Keychain (`future/siri-tasks.md`): adds reach the server when the app drains the queue.
10. **M9 Polish**: empty states, haptics, accessibility (Dynamic Type, VoiceOver labels), account deletion, Google sign-in replacing the stub (done; the stub stays in dev builds), TestFlight.
11. **M10 Recipe import** (4.10, 6.6), after M6's categorizer works, since extraction is a bigger LLM call. **Done**: everything below has landed; the end-to-end device pass and the extension's first build are manual checks still to run.
    - *Spec*: this section, 4.10 and 6.6.
    - *Server*: shared schemas and `canonicalRecipeUrl`; the JSON-LD recipe reader with real-site fixtures; safe page fetch and readable text; the `extract-recipe` AI task and prompt, with a recipe mock; the `import-recipe` Edge Function with rate limits, global cap and the `recipe_imports` cache; `yarn ai:recipe` and the recipe eval (`yarn ai:eval:recipe`).
    - *App, before the extension*: the client import (`importRecipe`: recipe reuse by URL, bulk add with Combine, undo, typed errors); the "Add recipe from link…" / "Import from link" sheet and toast.
    - *Share extension*: share envelope and `lastOpenedListId` snapshot schemas with the Swift contract test; the `targets/share-recipe/` extension; draining the share queue in the app; failed imports on the Recipes tab.
    - *End-to-end pass on a device*: Safari with and without JSON-LD, Chrome, a URL in text, a paywalled page, a non-recipe page, offline, signed out, and the same recipe twice.
12. **M11 List tab** (4.1, 4.7; mockups E, G, H): the Lists home goes. The List tab shows the current list as its root, the header's name opens the Your lists sheet (switching, invites, create, reorder, archived lists), and opening a list from elsewhere switches the tab to it. **Done**.

Later work landed outside these milestones, each tracked in `tasks.md` while it was built: households' staples (Always have, 4.5), admin and plans (4.12), recipe tags and filtering with the Saved facet (4.14), push notifications (4.13, 8.5; hosted setup by hand in `docs/push-setup.md`), Mexican Spanish (4.15; built, not yet switched on), and the meal plan (4.16; in progress).

---

## 11. Later

### 11.1 Notifications (req 13)
Push for adds and shopping, with per-kind toggles and quiet hours, moved out of Later: see 4.13, 5 and 8.5. Still Later:
- **Location reminders**: a reminder near a saved store (local, on device).
- **Per-household muting**: turning pushes off for one household while keeping them for another.
- **Pushes in the recipient's language** (4.15): the worker writes them in English; it needs each user's language stored on the server.

### 11.2 Web app
Turn on react-native-web for planning on a laptop (lists, recipes, households). Siri, the App Group queue, and native Google sign-in stay iOS-only; web uses Supabase's Google OAuth redirect. Offline on web is Later still.

### 11.3 Multi-item Siri adds
"Milk, eggs, and a bag of limes" becomes three items. Splitting on "and" naively breaks items like "mac and cheese", so `siri-add` would ask the LLM to split and parse when the text contains a list separator. Offline, the text stays one item until the server can split it.

### 11.4 Other
- Copy or move a list to another household.
- Per-store category orders (Q5).
- Manual drag ordering within a section.
- Sign in with Apple, if this ever goes to the public App Store (4.8).
- A household unit system, US (default) or metric, for the starting unit of ingredients the household hasn't bought yet (4.2). Planned in `future/units-tasks.md`.
- More languages beyond English and Mexican Spanish (4.15).

---

## 12. Open questions

| # | Question | Default used in this spec |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | ~~(req 7.1) Who controls shared data?~~ | **Decided**: the household owns it. All members edit equally; owners (one or more) manage membership and can delete the household (4.7). |
| Q2 | ~~(req 7.1) When someone leaves, do they keep a copy?~~ | **Decided**: no; data stays with the household. Leaving your only household gives you a new empty personal one. |
| Q3 | ~~(req 7.1) What happens to a joiner's existing data?~~ | **Decided**: nothing; it stays in their own household(s). Joining only adds access. |
| Q4 | ~~Should many users recategorizing the same ingredient eventually change the global default?~~ | **Decided**: no. Overrides stay per household (shared by its members, not by the rest of the app). |
| Q5 | Should category order be per user (all devices) or per device (e.g. different stores)? | Per user, synced (built that way). Per-store orders are a natural later feature. |
| Q6 | ~~Can a user belong to more than one household?~~ | **Decided**: yes, any number (4.7). |
| Q7 | ~~Should custom categories be shareable with the household?~~ | **Decided**: yes. Custom aisles and aisle overrides belong to the household and every member shares them (4.3.2). The aisle order is the household's too, and each member can keep their own for it (4.3.3). |
| Q8 | ~~Requirement 3 is missing from the original list.~~ | **Decided**: there was no requirement 3; nothing was dropped. |
| Q9 | ~~Should checked items auto-clear after some time?~~ | **Decided**: no, manual Clear (4.1, 4.6). |
| Q10 | ~~Prompt on in-app duplicate adds, or auto-Combine with an undo toast?~~ | **Decided**: auto-Combine with an undo toast, for single adds and recipe adds alike. A recipe add has no "already on the list" step; one toast with **Undo** takes back the whole add (4.4). |
| Q11 | ~~Is the Siri default list per user or per household?~~ | **Decided**: per user (`user_settings.default_list_id`). |
| Q12 | ~~Final app name.~~ | **Decided**: "The Perfect Bite" as the display name; the M0 spike confirms Siri recognizes it and picks the phrase variants. |
| Q13 | Google Cloud project for OAuth client IDs: reuse stash's or a new one? | Closed: a new project, same setup as stash. |
| Q14 | ~~Copy stash code or share packages?~~ | **Decided**: copy when useful; no shared packages. The apps evolve independently. |
| Q15 | Repo name and bundle id. | `perfect-bite`, `com.jgp.perfectbite`. The display name stays "The Perfect Bite". |
| Q16 | Can any member invite people, or only owners? | Any member. |
| Q17 | Can the sharer dismiss a failed share import (4.10), and does that hide it on all their devices? | Not yet: a failed share shows **Try again** only, and stays in the imports card until it succeeds or the row is deleted after a year (5). A `dismiss_share_import(id)` for the owner would be the server-state answer. |
